1	# Plan 3: AI Model Architecture
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Implement the full ZH-Copilot neural network in PyTorch — Custom CNN (spatial), Transformer (entities), MLP (scalars), LSTM (temporal), Strategy Encoder, Action Decoder, and Value Head. Model runs on M4 Mac via MPS backend.
6	
7	**Architecture:** Five encoders fuse into an LSTM. The LSTM output feeds three heads: policy (action decoder), value (win probability), and strategy (game phase embedding). The action decoder is auto-regressive: action type → target pointer → spatial position. All hyperparameters come from `config.MODEL`.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** PyTorch 2.x, MPS (Apple Silicon GPU), pytest
10	
11	---
12	
13	## File Structure
14	
15	```
16	src/zh_copilot/model/
17	├── __init__.py              # exports ZHCopilotModel + forward signature
18	├── spatial_encoder.py       # Custom CNN: (B, C, H, W) → (B, cnn_output_dim)
19	├── entity_encoder.py        # Transformer: (B, N, D) → (B, N, dim) + pooled (B, dim)
20	├── scalar_encoder.py        # MLP: (B, scalar_dim) → (B, mlp_hidden)
21	├── temporal.py              # LSTM: fused → (B, lstm_hidden) + hidden state
22	├── strategy_encoder.py      # Strategy: lstm_out → (B, strategy_dim)
23	├── action_decoder.py        # Action type + pointer + spatial heads
24	├── value_head.py            # Win probability: (B, lstm_hidden) → (B, 1)
25	└── zh_model.py              # ZHCopilotModel: wires everything together
26	
27	tests/model/
28	├── __init__.py
29	├── test_spatial_encoder.py
30	├── test_entity_encoder.py
31	├── test_scalar_encoder.py
32	├── test_temporal.py
33	├── test_action_decoder.py
34	├── test_value_head.py
35	└── test_zh_model.py
36	```

135	```python
136	# src/zh_copilot/model/spatial_encoder.py
137	"""Custom CNN encoder for the spatial game map.
138	
139	Input:  (B, C, H, W) — B=batch, C=spatial_channels (~40), H=W=128
140	Output: (B, output_dim) — pooled feature vector
141	
142	Architecture:
143	  6 residual blocks with squeeze-excitation attention.
144	  Downsampling: 128 → 64 → 32 → 16 → 8 → 4
145	  Final: global average pool → linear projection
146	"""
147	
148	import torch
149	import torch.nn as nn
150	import torch.nn.functional as F
151	
152	
153	class SqueezeExcitation(nn.Module):
154	    """Channel attention: re-weights channels based on global context."""
155	
156	    def __init__(self, channels: int, reduction: int = 4):
157	        super().__init__()
158	        hidden = max(channels // reduction, 4)
159	        self.fc = nn.Sequential(
160	            nn.Linear(channels, hidden),
161	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
162	            nn.Linear(hidden, channels),
163	            nn.Sigmoid(),
164	        )
165	
166	    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
167	        # x: (B, C, H, W)
168	        w = x.mean(dim=(2, 3))          # (B, C) global average pool
169	        w = self.fc(w).unsqueeze(-1).unsqueeze(-1)  # (B, C, 1, 1)
170	        return x * w
171	
172	
173	class ResidualBlock(nn.Module):
174	    """Residual conv block with optional downsampling and SE attention."""
175	
176	    def __init__(self, in_ch: int, out_ch: int, stride: int = 1):
177	        super().__init__()
178	        self.conv = nn.Sequential(
179	            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, out_ch, kernel_size=3, stride=stride, padding=1, bias=False),
180	            nn.BatchNorm2d(out_ch),
181	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
182	            nn.Conv2d(out_ch, out_ch, kernel_size=3, stride=1, padding=1, bias=False),
183	            nn.BatchNorm2d(out_ch),
184	        )
185	        self.se = SqueezeExcitation(out_ch)
186	        self.shortcut = nn.Sequential(
187	            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, out_ch, kernel_size=1, stride=stride, bias=False),
188	            nn.BatchNorm2d(out_ch),
189	        ) if (stride != 1 or in_ch != out_ch) else nn.Identity()
190	        self.relu = nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
191	
192	    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
193	        return self.relu(self.se(self.conv(x)) + self.shortcut(x))
194	
195	
196	class SpatialEncoder(nn.Module):
197	    """Custom CNN for spatial map encoding.
198	
199	    Progressively downsamples 128×128 → 4×4 using 6 residual blocks.
200	    Channels double every 2 blocks. Global average pool → linear projection.
201	
202	    Args:
203	        in_channels:  Number of input channels (spatial_channels, ~40)
204	        base_channels: Initial channel width (doubles per stage)
205	        num_blocks:   Total residual blocks (must be even, default 6)
206	        output_dim:   Output feature dimension (default 256)
207	    """
208	
209	    def __init__(
210	        self,
211	        in_channels: int = 40,
212	        base_channels: int = 32,
213	        num_blocks: int = 6,
214	        output_dim: int = 256,
215	    ):
216	        super().__init__()
217	        assert num_blocks % 2 == 0, "num_blocks must be even"
218	
219	        # Initial projection: in_channels → base_channels, no downsample
220	        self.stem = nn.Sequential(
221	            nn.Conv2d(in_channels, base_channels, kernel_size=3, padding=1, bias=False),
222	            nn.BatchNorm2d(base_channels),
223	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
224	        )
225	
226	        # Build residual blocks: each pair doubles channels and halves spatial dims
227	        blocks = []
228	        in_ch = base_channels
229	        for i in range(num_blocks):
230	            out_ch = base_channels * (2 ** (i // 2))
231	            stride = 2 if (i % 2 == 0 and i > 0) else 1  # downsample on even blocks
232	            blocks.append(ResidualBlock(in_ch, out_ch, stride=stride))
233	            in_ch = out_ch
234	        self.blocks = nn.Sequential(*blocks)
235	
236	        # Global average pool → linear projection
237	        self.pool = nn.AdaptiveAvgPool2d(1)
238	        self.proj = nn.Linear(in_ch, output_dim)
239	
240	    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
241	        # x: (B, C, H, W)
242	        x = self.stem(x)
243	        x = self.blocks(x)
244	        x = self.pool(x).flatten(1)   # (B, final_channels)
245	        x = self.proj(x)              # (B, output_dim)
246	        return x
247	```

313

317	    out_all, pool_all = encoder(x, mask_all)

320	    assert not torch.allclose(pool_5, pool_all)

358	
359	```python
360	# src/zh_copilot/model/entity_encoder.py
361	"""Transformer encoder for the entity (unit/building) list.
362	
363	Input:
364	  features: (B, N, D)  — per-entity feature vectors
365	  mask:     (B, N)     — True where entity slot is occupied
366	
367	Output:
368	  per_entity: (B, N, model_dim)  — contextual embedding per entity
369	  pooled:     (B, model_dim)     — masked mean pool over valid entities
370	
371	Uses PyTorch's built-in MultiheadAttention for efficiency.
372	Padding mask applied so invalid (empty) entity slots don't pollute attention.
373	"""
374	
375	import torch
376	import torch.nn as nn
377	import torch.nn.functional as F
378	
379	
380	class EntityEncoder(nn.Module):
381	    """Multi-layer Transformer encoder for the entity list.
382	
383	    Args:
384	        feature_dim: Raw entity feature dimension (entity_feature_dim, 32)
385	        model_dim:   Transformer hidden dimension (transformer_dim, 256)
386	        num_layers:  Number of Transformer encoder layers (8)
387	        num_heads:   Number of attention heads (8)
388	        ff_dim:      Feed-forward hidden dim (default 4 × model_dim)
389	        dropout:     Dropout rate (default 0.1)
390	    """
391	
392	    def __init__(
393	        self,
394	        feature_dim: int = 32,
395	        model_dim: int = 256,
396	        num_layers: int = 8,
397	        num_heads: int = 8,
398	        ff_dim: int | None = None,
399	        dropout: float = 0.1,
400	    ):
401	        super().__init__()
402	        ff_dim = ff_dim or model_dim * 4
403	
404	        # Project raw features into transformer dimension
405	        self.input_proj = nn.Linear(feature_dim, model_dim)
406	
407	        # Stack of Transformer encoder layers
408	        layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
409	            d_model=model_dim,
410	            nhead=num_heads,
411	            dim_feedforward=ff_dim,
412	            dropout=dropout,
413	            batch_first=True,   # (B, N, D) convention
414	            norm_first=True,    # pre-norm (more stable training)
415	        )
416	        self.transformer = nn.TransformerEncoder(layer, num_layers=num_layers)
417	
418	    def forward(
419	        self,
420	        features: torch.Tensor,   # (B, N, D)
421	        mask: torch.Tensor,       # (B, N) bool — True where entity is valid
422	    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
423	        """
424	        Returns:
425	            per_entity: (B, N, model_dim)
426	            pooled:     (B, model_dim) — mean over valid entities
427	        """
428	        # Project features
429	        x = self.input_proj(features)   # (B, N, model_dim)
430	
431	        # PyTorch TransformerEncoder expects src_key_padding_mask where
432	        # True = IGNORE (padding). Our mask is True = VALID, so invert.
433	        padding_mask = ~mask   # (B, N) — True where slot is empty
434	
435	        # Run transformer (masked attention)
436	        x = self.transformer(x, src_key_padding_mask=padding_mask)  # (B, N, model_dim)
437	
438	        # Masked mean pool over valid entities
439	        valid = mask.unsqueeze(-1).float()           # (B, N, 1)
440	        pooled = (x * valid).sum(dim=1)              # (B, model_dim)
441	        count = valid.sum(dim=1).clamp(min=1.0)      # (B, 1) avoid div-by-zero
442	        pooled = pooled / count
443	
444	        return x, pooled
445	```

533	- [ ] **Step 3: Implement scalar_encoder.py**
534	
535	```python
536	# src/zh_copilot/model/scalar_encoder.py
537	"""Two-layer MLP encoder for global scalar game state.
538	
539	Input:  (B, scalar_dim) — concatenated per-player + game-level scalars
540	Output: (B, hidden_dim) — encoded feature vector
541	"""
542	
543	import torch
544	import torch.nn as nn
545	
546	
547	class ScalarEncoder(nn.Module):
548	    """Two-layer MLP with LayerNorm for global scalar state.
549	
550	    Args:
551	        input_dim:  Number of input scalar features (global_scalar_dim, 80)
552	        hidden_dim: Output feature dimension (global_mlp_hidden, 128)
553	        dropout:    Dropout rate (default 0.1)
554	    """
555	
556	    def __init__(self, input_dim: int = 80, hidden_dim: int = 128, dropout: float = 0.1):
557	        super().__init__()
558	        self.net = nn.Sequential(
559	            nn.Linear(input_dim, hidden_dim),
560	            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
561	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
562	            nn.Dropout(dropout),
563	            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim),
564	            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
565	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
566	        )
567	
568	    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
569	        return self.net(x)
570	```

573	
574	```python
575	# src/zh_copilot/model/temporal.py
576	"""LSTM temporal module — maintains game history context across decision steps.
577	
578	Input:  (B, input_dim) fused feature vector from CNN + Transformer + MLP
579	Output: (B, hidden_dim) LSTM output + new hidden state
580	
581	The hidden state is passed across decision steps by the caller (training loop
582	or inference engine) to give the model memory of past observations.
583	"""
584	
585	import torch
586	import torch.nn as nn
587	
588	
589	class TemporalModule(nn.Module):
590	    """Single-layer LSTM for temporal context.
591	
592	    Args:
593	        input_dim:  Dimension of fused input (fusion_dim, 512)
594	        hidden_dim: LSTM hidden state size (lstm_hidden, 512)
595	        dropout:    Dropout on output (default 0.1)
596	    """
597	
598	    def __init__(self, input_dim: int = 512, hidden_dim: int = 512, dropout: float = 0.1):
599	        super().__init__()
600	        self.hidden_dim = hidden_dim
601	        self.lstm = nn.LSTM(
602	            input_size=input_dim,
603	            hidden_size=hidden_dim,
604	            num_layers=1,
605	            batch_first=True,
606	        )
607	        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
608	
609	    def forward(
610	        self,
611	        x: torch.Tensor,                               # (B, input_dim)
612	        hidden: tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor] | None = None,
613	    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]]:
614	        """
615	        Args:
616	            x:      (B, input_dim) — current fused features
617	            hidden: (h_n, c_n) LSTM state from previous step, or None for zero init
618	
619	        Returns:
620	            out:    (B, hidden_dim) — LSTM output for this step
621	            hidden: (h_n, c_n) — updated LSTM state to pass to next step
622	        """
623	        # LSTM expects (B, seq_len, input_dim) — seq_len = 1 per decision step
624	        out, hidden = self.lstm(x.unsqueeze(1), hidden)  # out: (B, 1, hidden_dim)
625	        out = self.dropout(out.squeeze(1))               # (B, hidden_dim)
626	        return out, hidden
627	```

652	The action decoder is auto-regressive:

760	```python
761	# src/zh_copilot/model/value_head.py
762	"""Value head — estimates win probability from LSTM output.
763	
764	Input:  (B, lstm_hidden)
765	Output: (B, 1) — win probability in [0, 1]
766	
767	Used by the actor-critic PPO training loop.
768	"""
769	
770	import torch
771	import torch.nn as nn
772	
773	
774	class ValueHead(nn.Module):
775	    """Two-layer MLP value head.
776	
777	    Args:
778	        input_dim:  LSTM output dimension (lstm_hidden, 512)
779	        hidden_dim: Hidden layer size (value_hidden, 256)
780	    """
781	
782	    def __init__(self, input_dim: int = 512, hidden_dim: int = 256):
783	        super().__init__()
784	        self.net = nn.Sequential(
785	            nn.Linear(input_dim, hidden_dim),
786	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
787	            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1),
788	            nn.Sigmoid(),   # output in [0, 1] = win probability
789	        )
790	
791	    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
792	        return self.net(x)
793	```

795	- [ ] **Step 4: Implement action_decoder.py**
796	
797	```python
798	# src/zh_copilot/model/action_decoder.py
799	"""Auto-regressive action decoder.
800	
801	Given LSTM output and entity embeddings, produces:
802	  1. action_type_logits: (B, num_action_types) — what to do
803	  2. pointer_logits:     (B, N)               — which entity to target
804	  3. spatial_logits:     (B, R, R)            — where on the map
805	
806	All outputs are raw logits (no softmax applied here — done at sampling time).
807	"""
808	
809	import torch
810	import torch.nn as nn
811	import torch.nn.functional as F
812	
813	
814	class ActionDecoder(nn.Module):
815	    """Auto-regressive action decoder.
816	
817	    Args:
818	        lstm_dim:          LSTM output dimension (512)
819	        entity_dim:        Transformer entity embedding dimension (256)
820	        num_action_types:  Number of discrete action types (21)
821	        spatial_resolution: Spatial output grid size (64)
822	    """
823	
824	    def __init__(
825	        self,
826	        lstm_dim: int = 512,
827	        entity_dim: int = 256,
828	        num_action_types: int = 21,
829	        spatial_resolution: int = 64,
830	    ):
831	        super().__init__()
832	        self.spatial_resolution = spatial_resolution
833	
834	        # 1. Action type head: LSTM → logits over action types
835	        self.action_type_head = nn.Sequential(
836	            nn.Linear(lstm_dim, lstm_dim // 2),
837	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
838	            nn.Linear(lstm_dim // 2, num_action_types),
839	        )
840	
841	        # 2. Pointer network: dot-product attention
842	        # Query = linear(lstm_out), Keys = entity embeddings
843	        self.ptr_query = nn.Linear(lstm_dim, entity_dim)
844	
845	        # 3. Spatial head: deconvolve LSTM output → (R, R) probability map
846	        # LSTM → (8, 8) → upsample to (64, 64)
847	        init_spatial = 8
848	        self.spatial_head = nn.Sequential(
849	            nn.Linear(lstm_dim, init_spatial * init_spatial * 64),
850	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
851	        )
852	        self.spatial_upsample = nn.Sequential(
853	            nn.ConvTranspose2d(64, 32, kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 8→16
854	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
855	            nn.ConvTranspose2d(32, 16, kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 16→32
856	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
857	            nn.ConvTranspose2d(16, 1,  kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 32→64
858	        )
859	        self._init_spatial = init_spatial
860	
861	    def forward(
862	        self,
863	        lstm_out: torch.Tensor,      # (B, lstm_dim)
864	        entity_emb: torch.Tensor,    # (B, N, entity_dim)
865	        entity_mask: torch.Tensor,   # (B, N) bool — True where valid
866	    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
867	        """
868	        Returns:
869	            action_logits:  (B, num_action_types) — raw logits
870	            pointer_logits: (B, N) — raw logits (masked invalid → -inf)
871	            spatial_logits: (B, R, R) — raw logits (R = spatial_resolution)
872	        """
873	        B = lstm_out.shape[0]
874	        N = entity_emb.shape[1]
875	
876	        # 1. Action type logits
877	        action_logits = self.action_type_head(lstm_out)  # (B, num_action_types)
878	
879	        # 2. Pointer network
880	        query = self.ptr_query(lstm_out).unsqueeze(1)    # (B, 1, entity_dim)
881	        # Scaled dot-product: (B, 1, entity_dim) × (B, entity_dim, N) → (B, 1, N)
882	        scale = entity_emb.shape[-1] ** 0.5
883	        ptr_logits = torch.bmm(query, entity_emb.transpose(1, 2)).squeeze(1) / scale  # (B, N)
884	        # Mask invalid entities: set to -inf so they can't be selected
885	        ptr_logits = ptr_logits.masked_fill(~entity_mask, float("-inf"))
886	
887	        # 3. Spatial head
888	        s = self.spatial_head(lstm_out)                               # (B, 8*8*64)
889	        s = s.view(B, 64, self._init_spatial, self._init_spatial)    # (B, 64, 8, 8)
890	        s = self.spatial_upsample(s).squeeze(1)                      # (B, R, R)
891	
892	        return action_logits, ptr_logits, s
893	```

965	- [ ] **Step 3: Implement strategy_encoder.py**
966	
967	```python
968	# src/zh_copilot/model/strategy_encoder.py
969	"""Strategy encoder — produces a continuous strategy embedding.
970	
971	Combines LSTM context with the human copilot override signal
972	(one-hot over 6 strategy buttons: attack, defend, rush, farm, turtle, auto).
973	
974	Output feeds back into the policy head as conditioning.
975	The model learns strategic archetypes from replays — not hardcoded plans.
976	
977	Input:
978	    lstm_out:         (B, lstm_dim)        — current game context
979	    strategy_override: (B, num_buttons)    — one-hot human signal (or zeros for auto)
980	
981	Output: (B, strategy_dim) — continuous strategy embedding
982	"""
983	
984	import torch
985	import torch.nn as nn
986	
987	
988	class StrategyEncoder(nn.Module):
989	    """Strategy encoder combining LSTM context and human override.
990	
991	    Args:
992	        lstm_dim:     LSTM output dimension (512)
993	        strategy_dim: Output strategy embedding dimension (64)
994	        num_buttons:  Number of copilot UI buttons (6)
995	    """
996	
997	    def __init__(
998	        self,
999	        lstm_dim: int = 512,
1000	        strategy_dim: int = 64,
1001	        num_buttons: int = 6,
1002	    ):
1003	        super().__init__()
1004	        self.net = nn.Sequential(
1005	            nn.Linear(lstm_dim + num_buttons, strategy_dim * 2),
1006	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
1007	            nn.Linear(strategy_dim * 2, strategy_dim),
1008	            nn.Tanh(),   # bound to [-1, 1] — stable conditioning signal
1009	        )
1010	
1011	    def forward(
1012	        self,
1013	        lstm_out: torch.Tensor,          # (B, lstm_dim)
1014	        strategy_override: torch.Tensor, # (B, num_buttons)
1015	    ) -> torch.Tensor:                   # (B, strategy_dim)
1016	        x = torch.cat([lstm_out, strategy_override], dim=-1)
1017	        return self.net(x)
1018	```
1019

1140	```python
1141	# src/zh_copilot/model/zh_model.py
1142	"""ZHCopilotModel — full model wiring all components together.
1143	
1144	Forward pass:
1145	  spatial map  → SpatialEncoder  → (B, 256)
1146	  entities     → EntityEncoder   → (B, 256)
1147	  scalars      → ScalarEncoder   → (B, 128)
1148	                 → cat + Fusion MLP → (B, 512)
1149	                 → TemporalModule (LSTM) → (B, 512)
1150	                 → StrategyEncoder → (B, 64)
1151	                 → ActionDecoder → logits
1152	                 → ValueHead → value
1153	
1154	All hyperparameters from config.MODEL and config.STATE.
1155	"""
1156	
1157	import torch
1158	import torch.nn as nn
1159	
1160	from zh_copilot.config import MODEL, STATE
1161	from zh_copilot.model.spatial_encoder import SpatialEncoder
1162	from zh_copilot.model.entity_encoder import EntityEncoder
1163	from zh_copilot.model.scalar_encoder import ScalarEncoder
1164	from zh_copilot.model.temporal import TemporalModule
1165	from zh_copilot.model.strategy_encoder import StrategyEncoder
1166	from zh_copilot.model.action_decoder import ActionDecoder
1167	from zh_copilot.model.value_head import ValueHead
1168	
1169	
1170	class ZHCopilotModel(nn.Module):
1171	    """Full ZH-Copilot policy and value network.
1172	
1173	    Input batch dict:
1174	        spatial:            (B, spatial_channels, H, W)
1175	        entity_features:    (B, max_entities, entity_feature_dim)
1176	        entity_mask:        (B, max_entities) bool
1177	        global_scalars:     (B, global_scalar_dim)
1178	        strategy_override:  (B, num_strategy_buttons) one-hot
1179	
1180	    Output dict:
1181	        action_logits:  (B, num_action_types)
1182	        ptr_logits:     (B, max_entities)
1183	        spatial_logits: (B, spatial_action_resolution, spatial_action_resolution)
1184	        value:          (B, 1) in [0, 1]
1185	        strategy_emb:   (B, strategy_dim)
1186	        hidden:         (h_n, c_n) LSTM state for next step
1187	    """
1188	
1189	    def __init__(self):
1190	        super().__init__()
1191	
1192	        # Encoders
1193	        self.spatial_enc = SpatialEncoder(
1194	            in_channels=STATE.spatial_channels,
1195	            base_channels=MODEL.cnn_base_channels,
1196	            num_blocks=MODEL.cnn_blocks,
1197	            output_dim=MODEL.cnn_output_dim,
1198	        )
1199	        self.entity_enc = EntityEncoder(

1200	            feature_dim=STATE.entity_feature_dim,
1201	            model_dim=MODEL.transformer_dim,
1202	            num_layers=MODEL.transformer_layers,
1203	            num_heads=MODEL.transformer_heads,
1204	        )
1205	        self.scalar_enc = ScalarEncoder(
1206	            input_dim=STATE.global_scalar_dim,
1207	            hidden_dim=MODEL.global_mlp_hidden,
1208	        )
1209	
1210	        # Fusion: concatenate all encoder outputs → project to fusion_dim
1211	        fused_dim = MODEL.cnn_output_dim + MODEL.transformer_dim + MODEL.global_mlp_hidden
1212	        self.fusion = nn.Sequential(
1213	            nn.Linear(fused_dim, MODEL.fusion_dim),
1214	            nn.LayerNorm(MODEL.fusion_dim),
1215	            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
1216	        )
1217	
1218	        # Temporal
1219	        self.temporal = TemporalModule(
1220	            input_dim=MODEL.fusion_dim,
1221	            hidden_dim=MODEL.lstm_hidden,
1222	        )
1223	
1224	        # Heads
1225	        self.strategy_enc = StrategyEncoder(
1226	            lstm_dim=MODEL.lstm_hidden,
1227	            strategy_dim=MODEL.strategy_dim,
1228	            num_buttons=STATE.num_strategy_buttons,
1229	        )
1230	        self.action_decoder = ActionDecoder(
1231	            lstm_dim=MODEL.lstm_hidden + MODEL.strategy_dim,  # conditioned on strategy
1232	            entity_dim=MODEL.transformer_dim,
1233	            num_action_types=MODEL.num_action_types,
1234	            spatial_resolution=MODEL.spatial_action_resolution,
1235	        )
1236	        self.value_head = ValueHead(
1237	            input_dim=MODEL.lstm_hidden,
1238	            hidden_dim=MODEL.value_hidden,
1239	        )
1240	
1241	    def forward(
1242	        self,
1243	        batch: dict[str, torch.Tensor],
1244	        hidden: tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor] | None,
1245	    ) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor | tuple]:
1246	        # --- Encode ---
1247	        spatial_feat = self.spatial_enc(batch["spatial"])               # (B, 256)
1248	        entity_emb, entity_pool = self.entity_enc(
1249	            batch["entity_features"], batch["entity_mask"]
1250	        )                                                                # (B, N, 256), (B, 256)
1251	        scalar_feat = self.scalar_enc(batch["global_scalars"])          # (B, 128)
1252	
1253	        # --- Fuse ---
1254	        fused = torch.cat([spatial_feat, entity_pool, scalar_feat], dim=-1)  # (B, 640)
1255	        fused = self.fusion(fused)                                            # (B, 512)
1256	
1257	        # --- Temporal ---
1258	        lstm_out, new_hidden = self.temporal(fused, hidden)             # (B, 512)
1259	
1260	        # --- Strategy ---
1261	        strategy_emb = self.strategy_enc(lstm_out, batch["strategy_override"])  # (B, 64)
1262	
1263	        # --- Action decoder (conditioned on strategy) ---
1264	        lstm_with_strategy = torch.cat([lstm_out, strategy_emb], dim=-1)  # (B, 576)
1265	        action_logits, ptr_logits, spatial_logits = self.action_decoder(
1266	            lstm_with_strategy, entity_emb, batch["entity_mask"]
1267	        )
1268	
1269	        # --- Value head ---
1270	        value = self.value_head(lstm_out)                               # (B, 1)
1271	
1272	        return {
1273	            "action_logits":  action_logits,
1274	            "ptr_logits":     ptr_logits,
1275	            "spatial_logits": spatial_logits,
1276	            "value":          value,
1277	            "strategy_emb":   strategy_emb,
1278	            "hidden":         new_hidden,
1279	        }
1280	
1281	    def count_parameters(self) -> int:
1282	        return sum(p.numel() for p in self.parameters() if p.requires_grad)
1283	```

Follow TDD: write tests first, verify they fail, implement, verify they pass, commit.\n\n## Project context\n- Repo: /Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot\n- Python venv: /Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/.venv (activate with `source .venv/bin/activate`)\n- Config already exists at src/zh_copilot/config.py with STATE.spatial_channels=40, STATE.spatial_resolution=128, MODEL.cnn_base_channels=32, MODEL.cnn_blocks=6, MODEL.cnn_output_dim=256\n- tests/model/ directory doesn't exist yet — create tests/model/__init__.py too\n- Working branch: main\n\n## File 1: tests/model/__init__.py\nEmpty file.\n\n## File 2: tests/model/test_spatial_encoder.py\n```python\n# tests/model/test_spatial_encoder.py\n\"\"\"Tests for the spatial map CNN encoder.\"\"\"\n\nimport torch\nimport pytest\nfrom zh_copilot.model.spatial_encoder import SpatialEncoder\nfrom zh_copilot.config import MODEL, STATE\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef encoder():\n    return SpatialEncoder(\n        in_channels=STATE.spatial_channels,\n        base_channels=MODEL.cnn_base_channels,\n        num_blocks=MODEL.cnn_blocks,\n        output_dim=MODEL.cnn_output_dim,\n    )\n\n\ndef test_output_shape(encoder):\n    x = torch.zeros(2, STATE.spatial_channels, STATE.spatial_resolution, STATE.spatial_resolution)\n    out = encoder(x)\n    assert out.shape == (2, MODEL.cnn_output_dim)\n\n\ndef test_output_dtype(encoder):\n    x = torch.zeros(2, STATE.spatial_channels, STATE.spatial_resolution, STATE.spatial_resolution)\n    out = encoder(x)\n    assert out.dtype == torch.float32\n\n\ndef test_single_batch(encoder):\n    x = torch.zeros(1, STATE.spatial_channels, STATE.spatial_resolution, STATE.spatial_resolution)\n    out = encoder(x)\n    assert out.shape == (1, MODEL.cnn_output_dim)\n\n\ndef test_different_inputs_produce_different_outputs(encoder):\n    x1 = torch.zeros(1, STATE.spatial_channels, STATE.spatial_resolution, STATE.spatial_resolution)\n    x2 = torch.ones(1, STATE.spatial_channels, STATE.spatial_resolution, STATE.spatial_resolution)\n    out1 = encoder(x1)\n    out2 = encoder(x2)\n    assert not torch.allclose(out1, out2)\n\n\ndef test_gradient_flows(encoder):\n    x = torch.randn(2, STATE.spatial_channels, STATE.spatial_resolution, STATE.spatial_resolution,\n                    requires_grad=True)\n    out = encoder(x)\n    loss = out.sum()\n    loss.backward()\n    assert x.grad is not None\n    assert x.grad.shape == x.shape\n\n\ndef test_parameter_count(encoder):\n    params = sum(p.numel() for p in encoder.parameters())\n    assert params < 10_000_000, f\"Too many params: {params:,}\"\n```\n\n## File 3: src/zh_copilot/model/spatial_encoder.py\n```python\n# src/zh_copilot/model/spatial_encoder.py\n\"\"\"Custom CNN encoder for the spatial game map.\n\nInput:  (B, C, H, W) — B=batch, C=spatial_channels (~40), H=W=128\nOutput: (B, output_dim) — pooled feature vector\n\nArchitecture:\n  6 residual blocks with squeeze-excitation attention.\n  Downsampling: 128 → 64 → 32 → 16 → 8 → 4\n  Final: global average pool → linear projection\n\"\"\"\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\n\n\nclass SqueezeExcitation(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Channel attention: re-weights channels based on global context.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, channels: int, reduction: int = 4):\n        super().__init__()\n        hidden = max(channels // reduction, 4)\n        self.fc = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Linear(channels, hidden),\n            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),\n            nn.Linear(hidden, channels),\n            nn.Sigmoid(),\n        )\n\n    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n        w = x.mean(dim=(2, 3))\n        w = self.fc(w).unsqueeze(-1).unsqueeze(-1)\n        return x * w\n\n\nclass ResidualBlock(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Residual conv block with optional downsampling and SE attention.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, in_ch: int, out_ch: int, stride: int = 1):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.conv = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, out_ch, kernel_size=3, stride=stride, padding=1, bias=False),\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(out_ch),\n            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),\n            nn.Conv2d(out_ch, out_ch, kernel_size=3, stride=1, padding=1, bias=False),\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(out_ch),\n        )\n        self.se = SqueezeExcitation(out_ch)\n        self.shortcut = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, out_ch, kernel_size=1, stride=stride, bias=False),\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(out_ch),\n        ) if (stride != 1 or in_ch != out_ch) else nn.Identity()\n        self.relu = nn.ReLU(inplace=True)\n\n    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n        return self.relu(self.se(self.conv(x)) + self.shortcut(x))\n\n\nclass SpatialEncoder(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Custom CNN for spatial map encoding.\n\n    Progressively downsamples 128×128 → 4×4 using 6 residual blocks.\n    Channels double every 2 blocks. Global average pool → linear projection.\n\n    Args:\n        in_channels:  Number of input channels (spatial_channels, ~40)\n        base_channels: Initial channel width (doubles per stage)\n        num_blocks:   Total residual blocks (must be even, default 6)\n        output_dim:   Output feature dimension (default 256)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        in_channels: int = 40,\n        base_channels: int = 32,\n        num_blocks: int = 6,\n        output_dim: int = 256,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        assert num_blocks % 2 == 0, \"num_blocks must be even\"\n\n        self.stem = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Conv2d(in_channels, base_channels, kernel_size=3, padding=1, bias=False),\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(base_channels),\n            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),\n        )\n\n        blocks = []\n        in_ch = base_channels\n        for i in range(num_blocks):\n            out_ch = base_channels * (2 ** (i // 2))\n            stride = 2 if (i % 2 == 0 and i > 0) else 1\n            blocks.append(ResidualBlock(in_ch, out_ch, stride=stride))\n            in_ch = out_ch\n        self.blocks = nn.Sequential(*blocks)\n\n        self.pool = nn.AdaptiveAvgPool2d(1)\n        self.proj = nn.Linear(in_ch, output_dim)\n\n    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n        x = self.stem(x)\n        x = self.blocks(x)\n        x = self.pool(x).flatten(1)\n        x = self.proj(x)\n        return x\n```\n\n## Steps\n1. Create tests/model/__init__.py (empty)\n2. Write tests/model/test_spatial_encoder.py\n3. Run tests — confirm they FAIL with ModuleNotFoundError\n4. Write src/zh_copilot/model/spatial_encoder.py\n5. Run tests — confirm all 6 PASS\n6. ---

## SPEC COMPLIANCE REPORT

### ❌ ISSUES FOUND

**Issue 1: Downsampling path does not match specification**

The spec requires: "downsampling 128→64→32→16→8→4" (6 different spatial dimensions)

Actual implementation: 128→128→128→64→64→32→32 (only 3 different spatial dimensions)

The implementation achieves:
- Block 0-1: stride=1 each → 128×128 (no downsampling)
- Block 2-3: stride=2 then stride=1 → 64×64
- Block 4-5: stride=2 then stride=1 → 32×32

To match the spec, each of the 6 blocks should downsample by a factor of 2, achieving the complete 128→64→32→16→8→4 path.

---

## Code Quality Review

### ✅ APPROVED

**Summary:** The SpatialEncoder implementation is well-structured, clean, and follows PyTorch best practices. All critical aspects are solid.

**Strengths:**

1. **Clean, Readable Code:**
   - Clear class hierarchy with logical separation of concerns (SqueezeExcitation, ResidualBlock, SpatialEncoder)
   - Concise and direct implementations without unnecessary boilerplate
   - Good use of nn.Sequential for clean module composition

2. **Proper Docstrings:**
   - Module-level docstring clearly describes input/output shapes and architecture
   - Class docstrings explain purpose and key parameters
   - Parameter documentation with types and default values

3. **PyTorch Best Practices:**
   - ✅ Uses `nn.Module` correctly with proper `super().__init__()` calls
   - ✅ No deprecated APIs (no old-style functional calls)
   - ✅ Uses `bias=False` with BatchNorm (correct pattern for BN+Conv)
   - ✅ Proper use of `inplace=True` for ReLU (minor optimization in training)
   - ✅ Adaptive pooling (`AdaptiveAvgPool2d`) is flexible and correct
   - ✅ `nn.Identity()` properly used for skip connections when needed
   - ✅ Type hints on forward methods

4. **No Unnecessary Complexity:**
   - Architecture is straightforward: stem → residual blocks → global pool → projection
   - Channel doubling strategy (every 2 blocks) is clear and efficient
   - Stride pattern is correct: only downsample on even-indexed blocks after block 0

5. **Tests are Meaningful:**
   - ✅ Output shape validation (batch-aware)
   - ✅ Data type check
   - ✅ Edge case: single batch element
   - ✅ Semantic correctness: different inputs produce different outputs
   - ✅ Gradient flow verification (critical for training)
   - ✅ Parameter count sanity check
   - ✅ Uses config values (matches deployment settings)
   - ✅ Fixture pattern properly implemented

6. **No Obvious Bugs:**
   - Channel progression is mathematically correct: out_ch = base_channels * (2^(i//2))
   - Stride logic correctly applies downsampling every other block starting from block 1
   - Shortcut connection properly handles channel/spatial mismatches
   - SE module correctly computes channel-wise attention
   - Forward pass flow is logical and maintains tensor shapes properly

**Minor observations (not issues):**
- Uses `requires_grad=True` in gradient test—good practice for explicit intent
- Configuration imported but reasonable, shows integration with project settings
- All parameters fit within sanity check (<10M params for a 128×128 input encoder is appropriate)

**Verdict:** Ready for production. Code is maintainable, well-tested, and implements the architecture as intended.

## Review of EntityEncoder Implementation

- ✅ Output: `pooled` (B, 256) → masked mean pooled from `per_entity`
- ✅ Mask: (B, N) bool → True where entity is valid

**Architecture:**

- ✅ 8 heads: `num_heads=8` (MODEL.transformer_heads)
- ✅ Masked mean pool: Lines 66-69 implement correct pooling with mask handling

**Masking:**

- ✅ Mask is applied to src_key_padding_mask preventing invalid slots from influencing attention
- ✅ Pooling respects mask: `valid = mask.unsqueeze(-1).float()` with clamped count to avoid division by zero

### Code Quality

**Architecture & Design:**

- ✅ Class docstring documents all parameters with types and defaults
- ✅ Forward method has inline comments explaining tensor dimensions and semantics

**Tensor Operations:**

- ✅ Proper use of `unsqueeze(-1)` for broadcasting in masked operations

## REVIEW REPORT: ScalarEncoder & TemporalModule

**Status:** ⚠️ APPROVED WITH CRITICAL ISSUE

### Specification Compliance

✓ **ScalarEncoder** (src/zh_copilot/model/scalar_encoder.py)
- Input shape: (B, 80) ✓
- Output shape: (B, 128) ✓
- Architecture: 2-layer MLP with LayerNorm ✓
- Layer structure: Linear(80→128) → LayerNorm → ReLU → Dropout → Linear(128→128) → LayerNorm → ReLU

✓ **TemporalModule** (src/zh_copilot/model/temporal.py)
- Input shape: (B, 512) ✓
- Output shape: (B, 512) ✓
- Architecture: Single LSTM ✓
- Hidden state: Returns tuple[Tensor, Tensor] (h, c) ✓
- Proper handling of hidden state persistence ✓

✓ **Test Suite** (tests/model/test_temporal.py)
- All 5 required tests present and **PASSING**
  - test_scalar_encoder_output_shape
  - test_scalar_encoder_gradient
  - test_temporal_output_shape
  - test_temporal_hidden_state_persists
  - test_temporal_gradient_flows

### Code Quality Assessment

✓ **Strengths:**
- Clean, readable code with proper imports
- Full type hints on forward methods (PyTorch standard)
- Good docstrings with parameter documentation
- Proper PyTorch conventions (batch_first=True for LSTM)
- All parameters registered correctly (27K for ScalarEncoder, 2.1M for TemporalModule)
- Gradient flow verified in all tests
- Sensible defaults (dropout=0.1, no bias issues)
- Device compatibility confirmed (CPU works, GPU-compatible)

✓ **Documentation:**
- Module-level docstrings present and informative
- Parameter documentation complete with config references
- Return types clearly documented

### Critical Issue Identified

❌ **ISSUE: inplace=True ReLU Operations**
- **Location:** src/zh_copilot/model/scalar_encoder.py, lines 26 and 30
- **Severity:** CRITICAL (best practice violation)
- **Impact:** Can cause issues with gradient checkpointing, distributed training, mixed precision, and advanced training techniques
- **Current Impact:** Tests pass because simple scenarios don't trigger the issue
- **Fix Required:** Change `nn.ReLU(inplace=True)` to `nn.ReLU()` (or explicit `inplace=False`)

```python
# Current (PROBLEMATIC)
nn.ReLU(inplace=True),

# Should be
nn.ReLU(),  # inplace=False is default
```

**Note:** This pattern also appears in spatial_encoder.py, indicating a codebase-wide pattern. However, this review focuses only on ScalarEncoder/TemporalModule.

### Verdict

**Result:** ❌ **ISSUES: Inplace ReLU operations violate PyTorch best practices**

The code is functionally correct and all tests pass, but the use of `inplace=True` in ReLU operations is a critical code quality issue that must be addressed before production use. This will cause problems with gradient checkpointing, distributed training, and other advanced training techniques.
```

> AGENT

The `inplace=True` flag is intentional from the plan spec and is a standard memory optimization in PyTorch — not a critical issue. All tests pass. Proceeding.

   Transformer entity embedding dimension (256)\n        num_action_types:   Number of discrete action types (21)\n        spatial_resolution: Spatial output grid size (64)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def nn.ReLU(inplace=True),\n            nn.ConvTranspose2d(16, 1,  kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 32→64\n        )\n        self._init_spatial = init_spatial\n\n    def forward(\n        self,\n        lstm_out: torch.Tensor,      # (B, lstm_dim)\n        entity_emb: torch.Tensor,    # (B, N, entity_dim)\n        entity_mask: torch.Tensor,   # (B, N) bool — True where valid\n    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n        B = lstm_out.shape[0]\n\n        action_logits = self.action_type_head(lstm_out)\n\n        query = self.ptr_query(lstm_out).unsqueeze(1)\n        scale = entity_emb.shape[-1] ** 0.5\n        ptr_logits = torch.bmm(query, entity_emb.transpose(1, 2)).squeeze(1) / scale\n        ptr_logits = ptr_logits.masked_fill(~entity_mask, float(\"-inf\"))\n\n        s = self.spatial_head(lstm_out)\n        s = s.view(B, 64, self._init_spatial, self._init_spatial)\n        s = self.spatial_upsample(s).squeeze(1)\n\n        return action_logits, ptr_logits, s\n```\n\n## Steps\n1. Write tests/model/test_action_decoder.py\n2. Run tests — confirm FAIL\n3. Write value_head.py and action_decoder.py\n4. Run tests — confirm all 6 PASS\n5. DONE_WITH_CONCERNS: One deviation from the spec — `masked_fill` uses `-1e9` instead of `float("-inf")` in `action_decoder.py`. The spec's test file `test_action_logits_finite` asserts `torch.isfinite(ptr_logits).all()` over all 1024 slots, but `test_pointer_respects_mask` requires masked positions to have values `< -1e4`. Using true `-inf` satisfies the second but breaks the first (since `isfinite(-inf)` is False). Using `-1e9` satisfies both: it is finite and is less than `-1e4`. This is semantically equivalent for softmax/argmax purposes in practice.

\"auto\" button\n    override_attack = torch.zeros(1, STATE.num_strategy_buttons)\n    override_attack[:, 0] = 1.0  # \"attack\" button\n\n    out_auto   = enc(lstm_out, override_auto)\n    out_attack = enc(lstm_out, override_attack)\n    assert not torch.allclose(out_auto, out_attack)\n```\n\n## File 2: src/zh_copilot/model/strategy_encoder.py\n```python\n# src/zh_copilot/model/strategy_encoder.py\n\"\"\"Strategy encoder — produces a continuous strategy embedding.\n\nCombines LSTM context with the human copilot override signal\n(one-hot over 6 strategy buttons: attack, defend, rush, farm, turtle, auto).\n\nInput:\n    lstm_out:          (B, lstm_dim)       — current game context\n    strategy_override: (B, num_buttons)   — one-hot human signal (or zeros for auto)\n\nOutput: (B, strategy_dim) — continuous strategy embedding\n\"\"\"\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\n\n\nclass StrategyEncoder(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Strategy encoder combining LSTM context and human override.\n\n    Args:\n        lstm_dim:     LSTM output dimension (512)\n        strategy_dim: Output strategy embedding dimension (64)\n        num_buttons:  Number of copilot UI buttons (6)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        lstm_dim: int = 512,\n        strategy_dim: int = 64,\n        num_buttons: int = 6,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.net = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Linear(lstm_dim + num_buttons, strategy_dim * 2),\n            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),\n            nn.Linear(strategy_dim * 2, strategy_dim),\n            nn.Tanh(),\n        )\n\n    def forward(\n        self,\n        lstm_out: torch.Tensor,          # (B, lstm_dim)\n        strategy_override: torch.Tensor, # (B, num_buttons)\n    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n        x = torch.cat([lstm_out, strategy_override], dim=-1)\n        return self.net(x)\n```\n\n## Steps\n1. ---

# Command & Conquer Generals Zero Hour - Headless Engine Architecture Documentation

## Executive Summary

The C&C Generals Zero Hour (GeneralsMD) source code is a complex Win32-based RTS engine with clearly separated game logic and rendering layers. The codebase uses a modular architecture with subsystem initialization, making it feasible to build a headless engine by stubbing out rendering/audio components and focusing on the core GameLogic and GameEngine systems.

---

## 1. TOP-LEVEL STRUCTURE

### Directory Organization

```
cczh-source-code/
├── Generals/               # Original C&C Generals (v1.0)
│   └── Code/
│       ├── GameEngine/    # Core game logic (platform-agnostic)
│       ├── GameEngineDevice/  # Rendering/audio (platform-specific)
│       ├── Main/          # Windows entry point
│       ├── Libraries/      # Third-party dependencies
│       └── Tools/         # Development tools
│
├── GeneralsMD/            # Zero Hour expansion (MD = MajorDestruction era)
│   └── Code/              # Identical structure to Generals/
│
└── Run/                   # Output binaries directory
```

### Generals vs GeneralsMD

- **Generals**: Original game (v1.0, ~2003)
- **GeneralsMD**: Zero Hour expansion, same code structure, differences in:
  - Map data and unit templates (INI files, not source code)
  - Game balance tweaks
  - New units/buildings specific to Zero Hour

**Both share identical source code architecture.**

---

## 2. GAMEENGINE MODULES (Core Game Logic)

### Directory Structure: `Code/GameEngine/Source/` and `Code/GameEngine/Include/`

```
GameEngine/
├── Source/
│   ├── Common/          # Shared utilities, game state, configuration
│   │   ├── Audio/
│   │   ├── Bezier/
│   │   ├── INI/         # Configuration file parsing
│   │   ├── RTS/         # High-level game concepts (Teams, Squads)
│   │   ├── System/      # Global managers (Radar, BuildAssistant, Upgrade)
│   │   └── Thing/       # Object templates and factories
│   │
│   ├── GameClient/      # Client-side simulation (UI, camera, input handling)
│   │   ├── Drawable/    # Visual representation layer
│   │   ├── GUI/         # Control bar, menus, UI callbacks
│   │   ├── Input/       # Keyboard/mouse input processing
│   │   ├── MessageStream/  # Message routing
│   │   ├── System/      # Display, particles, sounds
│   │   └── Terrain/     # Terrain visual system
│   │
│   ├── GameLogic/       # *** CORE GAME SIMULATION (CRITICAL FOR HEADLESS) ***
│   │   ├── AI/          # AI player logic, pathfinding, group management
│   │   ├── Object/      # Units, buildings, projectiles (Object class)
│   │   ├── Map/         # Terrain logic, triggers
│   │   ├── ScriptEngine/  # Map scripting (custom game logic)
│   │   └── System/      # GameLogic singleton, state management
│   │
│   ├── GameNetwork/     # Network multiplayer, command distribution
│   │   ├── GameSpy/     # Online matchmaking
│   │   └── WOLBrowser/  # Web browser integration
│   │
│   └── Precompiled/     # Precompiled headers
│
└── Include/
    ├── Common/          # Header files (parallel structure)
    ├── GameClient/
    ├── GameLogic/
    └── GameNetwork/
```

### Key Files in GameEngine

| File | Purpose | Location |
|------|---------|----------|
| **GameCommon.h** | **Frame rate & game constants** | `Include/Common/` |
| **GameEngine.h** | Main engine interface, `execute()` loop | `Include/Common/` |
| **GameLogic.h** | Game state manager, object registry | `Include/GameLogic/` |
| **Object.h** | Base entity class (units/buildings) | `Include/GameLogic/` |
| **Player.h** | Player state (money, power, supplies) | `Include/Common/` |
| **Money.h** | Resource tracking | `Include/Common/` |
| **PartitionManager.h** | Spatial partitioning & fog of war | `Include/GameLogic/` |
| **MessageStream.h** | Event/command propagation system | `Include/Common/` |
| **NetCommandMsg.h** | Multiplayer command serialization | `Include/GameNetwork/` |
| **UpdateModule.h** | Base class for unit/building behaviors | `Include/GameLogic/Module/` |

---

## 3. KEY SYSTEMS FOR HEADLESS ENGINE

### 3.1 Game Tick / Main Loop

**Frame Rate Definition:**
```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/GameCommon.h (Line 66)
enum {
    LOGICFRAMES_PER_SECOND = 30,      // *** 30 FPS fixed tick rate ***
    MSEC_PER_SECOND = 1000
};
```

**Main Loop Entry Points:**

```cpp
// GameEngine class hierarchy:
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/GameEngine.h

class GameEngine : public SubsystemInterface {
    void init(int argc, char *argv[]);     // Initialize
    void reset();                            // Reset to start state
    void execute();                          // Main loop (blocks until quit)
    void update();                           // Per-frame update
    void setFramesPerSecondLimit(Int fps);  // Cap FPS (default 45)
};
```

**Game Loop Architecture:**
1. `GameEngine::execute()` - Main loop (Win32-specific in `Win32GameEngine`)
2. Each frame:
   - `GameEngine::update()` calls
   - `GameLogic::update()` - Update all units/buildings
   - `GameClient::update()` - Update camera, input, UI
   - `Display::draw()` - Render scene
3. Loop continues until `GameEngine::getQuitting()` returns true

**File:** `Code/GameEngine/Source/Common/GameEngine.cpp`

**For headless operation:**
- Call `GameLogic::update()` directly in your own loop
- Stub out `GameClient` and `Display` initialization
- Use fixed 30 Hz tick rate (no frame-skipping sync needed)

---

### 3.2 Entity/Object System

**Base Class Hierarchy:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/Thing.h
class Thing : public MemoryPoolObject {
    // Base for all game entities
};

// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/Object.h (Line 154)
class Object : public Thing, public Snapshot {
    // *** MAIN ENTITY CLASS ***
    // All units, buildings, projectiles derive from this
};
```

**Object Structure:**

```cpp
class Object {
    // Identity
    ObjectID m_id;                    // Unique identifier
    ThingTemplate *m_template;        // Template data (from INI files)
    Player *m_owner;                  // Owner player
    Team *m_team;                     // Team affiliation

    // State
    Coord3D m_position;               // World position
    Real m_angle;                     // Facing angle (0-360 degrees)
    
    // Properties
    Int m_health;                     // Current health points
    VeterancyLevel m_veterancy;       // Rank (LEVEL_REGULAR, VETERAN, ELITE, HEROIC)
    ObjectStatusBits m_status;        // State flags (alive, moving, attacking, etc.)
    
    // Behavior System
    UpdateModule *m_updateModules[];  // Behavior modules (See Section 3.5)
    
    // Visibility
    ObjectShroudStatus m_shroudStatus; // Fog of war status (CLEAR, FOGGED, SHROUDED)
};

// Object types determined at creation:
enum ObjectKindOf {
    KINDOF_UNIT,              // Mobile combat unit
    KINDOF_STRUCTURE,         // Building
    KINDOF_PROJECTILE,        // Bullet, missile, etc.
    KINDOF_AIRCRAFT,          // Flying unit
    // ... ~50 more types
};
```

**Key Methods:**
- `getHealth()` / `takeDamage()` - Damage handling
- `setPosition()` / `getPosition()` - Location
- `getNextObject()` / Previous tracking for global iteration
- `CRC()` / `xfer()` - Serialization (for save/load)

**File:** `/Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/Object.h`

---

### 3.3 Player State & Resources

**Player Class:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/Player.h

class Player : public Snapshot {
    // Identity
    PlayerIndex m_playerIndex;        // 0-15 (MAX_PLAYER_COUNT = 16)
    AsciiString m_playerName;
    AsciiString m_side;               // "USA", "China", "GLA"
    
    // Resources
    Money m_money;                    // Class in Money.h
    Int m_powerPoints;                // Power bar level (for power plants)
    
    // Army composition
    std::list<Object*> m_allObjects;  // All units/buildings owned by player
    
    // Science/Upgrades
    std::vector<Science> m_sciences;  // Technology researched
    std::vector<UpgradeTemplate*> m_upgrades; // Permanent upgrades
    
    // Special Powers
    SpecialPowerReadyTimer m_specialPowers[]; // Cooldowns for special abilities
};

// Money management
class Money {
    UnsignedInt m_money;              // Current amount
    UnsignedInt withdraw(amount, playSound);  // Spend money
    void deposit(amount, playSound);  // Receive money
};
```

**Power System:**
- Buildings: Power plants generate power
- Units: Some units consume power when active
- Power bar fluctuates based on generation vs. consumption
- **Critical:** Building placement can be restricted if insufficient power available

**Files:** 
- `Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/Player.h`
- `Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/Money.h`
- `Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/Energy.h` (power tracking)

### 3.4 Action Injection & Command System

**Command Flow Architecture:**

```
User Input / AI Decision
    ↓
MessageStream (client-side translation)
    ↓
CommandList (queued commands)
    ↓
GameLogic::processCommandList()
    ↓
Object::issueCommand() → UpdateModule execution
    ↓
Unit moves / attacks / builds
```

**GameMessage System:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/MessageStream.h

union GameMessageArgumentType {
    Int integer;
    Real real;
    ObjectID objectID;
    Coord3D location;
    // ... 11 total types
};

class GameMessage {
    enum Type {
        MSG_MOUSE_LEFT_CLICK,
        MSG_MOUSE_RIGHT_CLICK,
        MSG_RAW_KEY_DOWN,
        // ... ~200+ message types
    };
    
    Type m_type;
    GameMessageArgument *m_argList;  // Linked list of arguments
};
```

**Network Command Messages:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/GameNetwork/NetCommandMsg.h

class NetCommandMsg {
    UnsignedInt m_executionFrame;     // When to execute (frame sync)
    UnsignedInt m_playerID;            // Which player
    NetCommandType m_commandType;     // Type of command (enum)
};

class NetGameCommandMsg : public NetCommandMsg {
    GameMessage *constructGameMessage();  // Convert to GameMessage
};
```

**For AI Integration:**

```cpp
// Inject unit move command:
Object *unit = gameLogic->findObjectByID(unitID);
unit->setPosition(newPosition);  // Direct manipulation
// OR
GameMessage msg;
msg.m_type = MSG_MOVE_UNIT;
msg.addArgument(ARGUMENTDATATYPE_OBJECTID, unitID);
msg.addArgument(ARGUMENTDATATYPE_LOCATION, targetLocation);
// Process through CommandList → GameLogic

// Inject attack command:
unit->setAttackTarget(targetObject);
```

**Key Files:**
- `Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/MessageStream.h` - Message definitions
- `Code/GameEngine/Include/GameNetwork/NetCommandMsg.h` - Network serialization
- `Code/GameEngine/Source/GameLogic/GameLogic.cpp` - `processCommandList()`
- `Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/Object.h` - Command methods

---

### 3.5 Object Update/Behavior Module System

**Core Architecture:**

Objects don't have hardcoded behavior. Instead, they use a **modular component system**:

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/Module/UpdateModule.h

class UpdateModuleInterface {
    virtual UpdateSleepTime update() = 0;   // Called each frame or when awoken
};

enum UpdateSleepTime {
    UPDATE_SLEEP_NONE = 1,           // Call every frame
    UPDATE_SLEEP_FOREVER = 0x3fffffff, // Never call until awakened
    // Custom: UPDATE_SLEEP(N) = N frames
};
```

**Module Examples:**

```
Object contains multiple UpdateModule instances:
├── PhysicsUpdate        → Movement, collision
├── AIUpdate            → Combat AI, pathfinding
├── WeaponUpdate        → Firing, cooldowns
├── DamageModule        → Health/armor
├── ProductionUpdate    → Building production (for factories)
├── LocomotorUpdate     → Walking/flying animation
└── ... ~100+ module types
```

**Update Schedule:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/Module/UpdateModule.h

enum SleepyUpdatePhase {
    PHASE_INITIAL = 0,     // Resolve object creation/deletion
    PHASE_PHYSICS = 1,     // Physics updates (movement)
    PHASE_NORMAL = 2,      // Standard updates (combat, AI)
    PHASE_FINAL = 3,       // Cleanup and rendering updates
};

// Within GameLogic each frame:
for (frame 0 to N_frames) {
    // Phase 0: Create/delete objects
    for (each object) { if (object->isAwake()) update(PHASE_INITIAL); }
    
    // Phase 1: Physics
    for (each object) { if (object->isAwake()) update(PHASE_PHYSICS); }
    
    // Phase 2: Standard logic
    for (each object) { if (object->isAwake()) update(PHASE_NORMAL); }
    
    // Phase 3: Final (visual updates)
    for (each object) { if (object->isAwake()) update(PHASE_FINAL); }
}
```

**Files:**
- `Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/Module/UpdateModule.h`
- `Code/GameEngine/Source/GameLogic/Object/` - 100+ module implementations

---

### 3.6 Fog of War / Shroud System

**PartitionManager:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/PartitionManager.h

class PartitionManager {
    // Spatial grid for fast queries
    PartitionCell m_cells[GRID_WIDTH][GRID_HEIGHT];
    
    // Fog of War tracking
    struct CellShroudStatus {
        CELLSHROUD_CLEAR,     // Visible right now
        CELLSHROUD_FOGGED,    // Seen before, but not now
        CELLSHROUD_SHROUDED   // Never seen
    };
};

// Object shroud status
enum ObjectShroudStatus {
    OBJECTSHROUD_CLEAR,        // Fully visible
    OBJECTSHROUD_PARTIAL_CLEAR, // Partially visible
    OBJECTSHROUD_FOGGED,       // Visible in past, fogged now
    OBJECTSHROUD_SHROUDED      // Never been seen
};
```

**How Fog Works:**

1. Units have a "sight radius"
2. Each frame, PartitionManager calculates visible cells around units
3. Objects update their `m_shroudStatus` based on visibility
4. AI/Players can only target units they can see (depends on `m_shroudStatus`)

**For Headless Engine:**
- Shroud system is **critical for AI decision making**
- Must track per-player visibility (different AI players see different maps)
- Query methods:
  - `PartitionManager::getObjectsInRegion()` - Objects in area
  - `Object::getShroudStatus(player)` - Can player see this object?

**File:** `Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/PartitionManager.h`

---

## 4. GAMEENGINEDEVICE (Rendering & Audio Layer)

### Directory Structure

```
GameEngineDevice/
├── Include/
│   ├── MilesAudioDevice/     # Miles Sound System audio
│   ├── VideoDevice/          # Video playback (Bink codec)
│   ├── W3DDevice/            # *** MAIN RENDERING LAYER (DirectX 9) ***
│   │   ├── Common/           # Conversion, utilities
│   │   ├── GameClient/       # Rendering subsystem
│   │   └── GameLogic/        # Physics visualization
│   └── Win32Device/          # Windows OS integration
│
├── Source/
│   ├── MilesAudioDevice/     # Audio playback
│   ├── VideoDevice/          # Video/cutscene playback
│   ├── W3DDevice/            # DirectX 9 rendering (WW3D engine)
│   └── Win32Device/          # Windows window, input hooks
│
└── [Libraries]/
    ├── DirectX9/             # DirectX 9.0c SDK
    ├── Miles6/               # Miles Sound System SDK
    └── Bink/                 # Bink Video SDK
```

### Key Components

**W3DDevice - Main Rendering Engine:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngineDevice/Include/W3DDevice/GameClient/W3DDisplay.h

class W3DDisplay : public Display {
    // Virtual methods from Display interface:
    void draw();                  // Render scene
    void update();                // Update render state
    void drawLine(...);           // Debug drawing
    void drawFillRect(...);       // UI drawing
    
    // W3D-specific:
    void renderTerrain();         // Terrain mesh
    void renderObjects();         // Unit/building meshes
    void renderParticles();       // Particle effects
    void renderUI();              // Control bar, text
};
```

**Win32Device - OS Integration:**

```cpp
class Win32GameEngine : public GameEngine {
    // Creates W3DDisplay
    // Handles Windows messages (WM_PAINT, WM_SIZE, etc.)
    // Manages DirectX device reset on window focus loss
};
```

### Rendering Dependencies

**Tightly Coupled:**
- `GameClient` → `Display` (virtual interface, loose coupling!)
- `GameLogic` → `Drawable` objects (for visual effects sync)
- `GameLogic` has minimal rendering dependency

**Abstract Interface:**

```cpp
// File: Code/GameEngine/Include/GameClient/Display.h

class Display : public SubsystemInterface {
    virtual void draw() = 0;                    // Pure virtual
    virtual void drawLine(...) = 0;
    virtual void drawFillRect(...) = 0;
    // ~20 pure virtual methods
};

// W3DDisplay implements Display interface
// Can be replaced with NullDisplay stub
```

### Headless Stub Strategy

**Option A: Null Rendering**
```cpp
// Create a NullDisplay : public Display
class NullDisplay : public Display {
    void draw() override {}           // No-op
    void drawLine(...) override {}    // No-op
    void drawFillRect(...) override {} // No-op
    // ... all methods = no-op
};

// Inject in engine initialization:
gameEngine->createDisplay() → new NullDisplay()
```

**Option B: Network Streaming**
```cpp
// Create a RemoteDisplay : public Display
class RemoteDisplay : public Display {
    void draw() override {
        // Send screen state to client via network/IPC
    }
};
```

**Option C: Headless Variant**
```cpp
// Build separate libGameEngineHeadless.lib
// Exclude W3DDevice/Win32Device entirely
// Link with GameEngine + GameLogic only
```

**Files to Stub:**
- `Code/GameEngineDevice/Source/W3DDevice/` - All rendering
- `Code/GameEngineDevice/Source/MilesAudioDevice/` - Audio
- `Code/GameEngineDevice/Source/VideoDevice/` - Video cutscenes
- `Code/Main/WinMain.cpp` - Windows message loop

## 6. GAME FLOW DIAGRAM

```
WinMain() [Code/Main/WinMain.cpp]
    ↓
CreateGameEngine() → Win32GameEngine [GameEngineDevice]
    ↓
TheGameEngine->init()
    ├─ GameLogic::init()
    │   ├─ Initialize object factory
    │   ├─ Initialize AI system
    │   ├─ Load map INI
    │   └─ Create player objects
    │
    ├─ GameClient::init()
    │   ├─ Display::init()
    │   ├─ Keyboard::init()
    │   └─ Mouse::init()
    │
    └─ MessageStream::init()
        └─ Register translators
        
TheGameEngine->execute() [MAIN LOOP]
    ├─ While !quitting:
    │   ├─ serviceWindowsOS()  [Windows messages]
    │   │
    │   ├─ GameEngine::update()
    │   │   ├─ GameLogic::update()
    │   │   │   ├─ Process CommandList
    │   │   │   ├─ For each frame in phase 0-3:
    │   │   │   │   └─ Call UpdateModule::update() on each object
    │   │   │   └─ Check victory conditions
    │   │   │
    │   │   ├─ GameClient::update()
    │   │   │   ├─ Update camera
    │   │   │   ├─ Process input messages
    │   │   │   └─ Update UI
    │   │   │
    │   │   └─ MessageStream::update()
    │   │       └─ Process messages through translators
    │   │
    │   └─ Display::draw()
    │       ├─ drawViews() [terrain, objects, effects]
    │       └─ drawUI() [control bar, text]
    │
    └─ Cleanup
```

## 7. KEY CLASS DEFINITIONS

Thing                           [Include/Common/Thing.h]

Object {

BehaviorModule                  [Include/GameLogic/Module/BehaviorModule.h]
├─ UpdateModule                 [Include/GameLogic/Module/UpdateModule.h]
│  ├─ PhysicsUpdate
│  ├─ AIUpdate

    Money m_money;

};

// Message system

};

## 9. BUILD CONFIGURATION

### Project Files

**Both Generals and GeneralsMD use:**
- `Code/RTS.dsp` - Visual Studio 6.0 project (master solution)
- `Code/RTS.dsw` - Visual Studio 6.0 workspace (master)
- Individual `.dsp` files:
  - `GameEngine/GameEngine.dsp` - Core library
  - `GameEngineDevice/GameEngineDevice.dsp` - Rendering library
  - Multiple tool projects

### Dependencies (from README.md)

```
Required external libraries:
- DirectX SDK (9.0+)           → Code/Libraries/DirectX/
- STLport (4.5.3)              → Code/Libraries/STLport-4.5.3
- 3DSMax 4 SDK                 → Code/Libraries/Max4SDK/
- Miles Sound System            → Code/Libraries/Source/WWVegas/Miles6/
- Bink Video SDK               → Code/GameEngineDevice/Include/VideoDevice/Bink
- GameSpy SDK                  → Code/Libraries/Source/GameSpy/
- ZLib (1.1.4)                 → Code/Libraries/Source/Compression/ZLib/
```

### Headless Build Strategy

1. Remove DirectX, Miles, Bink, GameSpy dependencies
2. Create `GameEngineHeadless.dsp` - Excludes all GameEngineDevice
3. Link only GameEngine + Libraries/Source
4. Replace WinMain with custom headless entry point
5. Use existing GameLogic/AI unchanged

---

## 10. DATA FLOW FOR AI INTEGRATION

### State Extraction Example

```cpp
// Get all units for player 0
GameLogic *logic = TheGameLogic;
Player *player = logic->getPlayer(0);

// Iterate units
for (Object *unit = player->getFirstObject(); unit; unit = unit->getNextObject()) {
    // Read unit state
    ObjectID id = unit->getId();
    Coord3D pos = unit->getPosition();
    Int health = unit->getHealth();
    ThingTemplate *tmpl = unit->getTemplate();
    AsciiString name = tmpl->getName();
    
    // Check visibility from player 1's perspective
    ObjectShroudStatus shroud = unit->getShroudStatus(1);
    if (shroud == OBJECTSHROUD_CLEAR) {
        // Player 1 can see this unit
    }
}

// Get resources
UnsignedInt money = player->getMoney()->countMoney();
Int power = player->getPowerPoints();
```

### Action Injection Example

```cpp
// Move unit to location
Object *unit = logic->findObjectByID(unitID);
unit->setPosition(Coord3D(100, 50, 0));

// Attack target
Object *target = logic->findObjectByID(targetID);
unit->setAttackTarget(target);

// Give attack move command (implicit attack while moving)
unit->setFormationAttackMoving(true);

// Build structure
Object *builder = logic->findObjectByID(builderID);
// Note: Building production handled by ProductionUpdate module
// Must use proper command system for most complex actions
```

## 11. SNAPSHOT & SERIALIZATION SYSTEM

**All game objects inherit from `Snapshot`:**

```cpp
// File: Include/Common/Snapshot.h

class Snapshot {
    virtual void crc(Xfer *xfer);      // Calculate checksum (for multiplayer sync)
    virtual void xfer(Xfer *xfer);     // Serialize state (for save/load)
    virtual void loadPostProcess();    // Post-load initialization
};
```

**Used for:**
- Save/load game functionality
- Replay recording
- **Multiplayer CRC checks** (verify all players have same game state)

**For headless engine:**
- Can replay recorded games
- Can snapshot state for AI training datasets
- Can verify multiplayer consistency

---

## 12. CRITICAL DESIGN PATTERNS

### 1. Singleton Pattern

```cpp
GameEngine *TheGameEngine;           // Global instance
GameLogic *TheGameLogic;
GameClient *TheGameClient;
MessageStream *TheMessageStream;
Display *TheDisplay;                 // Can be W3DDisplay or NullDisplay
```

### 2. Factory Pattern

```cpp
// ThingFactory creates objects from templates
class ThingFactory {
    Object *createObject(ThingTemplate *tmpl, ObjectStatusBits status, Team *team);
};

ModuleFactory *TheModuleFactory;     // Creates behavior modules
```

### 3. Observer Pattern

```cpp
// MessageStream routes messages to subscribers
class MessageStream {
    TranslatorID registerTranslator(Translator *t);  // Subscribe to messages
    void sendMessage(GameMessage *msg);              // Broadcast
};
```

### 4. Component Pattern

```cpp
// Objects contain multiple UpdateModule components
Object {
    UpdateModule *m_updateModules[MAX_UPDATE_MODULES];
    // Each module handles one aspect of behavior
};
```

---

## CONCLUSION

The C&C Generals Zero Hour source code is **well-architected for headless operation**:

1. **Clear separation:** GameLogic (logic-only) vs GameEngineDevice (rendering-only)
2. **Virtual interfaces:** Display, AudioManager, etc. are abstract (stub-friendly)
3. **Fixed tick rate:** 30 FPS deterministic, no rendering dependency
4. **Modular objects:** UpdateModule system is flexible and easy to extend
5. **Complete state:** Everything needed for AI training is in GameLogic

**Implementation Plan:**
1. Build GameEngine.lib without GameEngineDevice
2. Implement NullDisplay stub
3. Create custom WinMain → direct API calls
4. Inject AI decisions via Object::setPosition(), ::setAttackTarget(), etc.
5. Read game state from GameLogic singletons
6. Loop at 30 Hz fixed rate

The codebase is C++/DirectX 9, requiring modernization but the architecture is sound for headless operation.
```

No hardcoded behavior per unit type.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Player State\n\n```cpp\n// Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/Player.h\n\nclass Player : public Snapshot {\n    PlayerIndex  m_playerIndex;       // 0-15 (MAX_PLAYER_COUNT = 16)\n    AsciiString  m_side;              // \"USA\", \"China\", \"GLA\"\n    Money        m_money;             // UnsignedInt m_money\n    Int          m_powerPoints;       // Power surplus/deficit\n    std::list<Object*> m_allObjects;  // All owned units/buildings\n    Science      m_sciences[];        // Researched tech\n    UpgradeTemplate *m_upgrades[];    // Applied upgrades\n    SpecialPowerReadyTimer m_specialPowers[]; // Cooldowns\n};\n```\n\n---\n\n## 7. Command / Action Injection\n\n**Command flow:**\n```\nAI Decision\n    → GameMessage (type + args)\n    → CommandList queue\n    → GameLogic::processCommandList()\n    → Object::issueCommand()\n    → UpdateModule executes\n```\n\n```cpp\n// Code/GameEngine/Include/Common/MessageStream.h\nclass GameMessage {\n    enum Type { MSG_MOVE_UNIT, MSG_ATTACK_TARGET, MSG_BUILD_STRUCTURE, ... };\n    // ~200+ message types\n    GameMessageArgument *m_argList;  // objectID, location, etc.\n};\n\n// Code/GameEngine/Include/GameNetwork/NetCommandMsg.h\nclass NetCommandMsg {\n    UnsignedInt m_executionFrame;  // Deterministic execution frame\n    UnsignedInt m_playerID;\n    NetCommandType m_commandType;\n    GameMessage *constructGameMessage();\n};\n```\n\n**Direct manipulation (for headless training):**\n```cpp\nunit->setPosition(Coord3D(x, y, z));\nunit->setAttackTarget(targetObject);\nunit->setFormationAttackMoving(true);\nUnsignedInt money = player->getMoney()->countMoney();\nInt power = player->getPowerPoints();\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. Fog of War\n\n```cpp\n// Code/GameEngine/Include/GameLogic/PartitionManager.h\n\nclass PartitionManager {\n    PartitionCell m_cells[GRID_WIDTH][GRID_HEIGHT];\n    // Per-cell shroud status per player\n    // Updated each frame based on unit sight radii\n};\n\n// Query:\nObjectShroudStatus shroud = unit->getShroudStatus(playerIndex);\n// OBJECTSHROUD_CLEAR = visible to that player right now\n```\n\n---\n\n## 9. Rendering Layer (GameEngineDevice) — Stub This Out\n\n```\nCode/GameEngineDevice/\n├── W3DDevice/        # DirectX 9 rendering (Westwood 3D engine)\n├── MilesAudioDevice/ # Miles Sound System\n├── VideoDevice/      # Bink video codec (cutscenes)\n└── Win32Device/      # Windows OS (HWND, WndProc)\n```\n\n**Rendering uses a virtual interface:**\n```cpp\n// Code/GameEngine/Include/GameClient/Display.h\nclass Display : public SubsystemInterface {\n    virtual void draw() = 0;\n    virtual void drawLine(...) = 0;\n    // ~20 pure virtual methods\n};\n// W3DDisplay implements this — replace with NullDisplay\n```\n\n**Stub strategy:**\n```cpp\nclass NullDisplay : public Display {\n    void draw() override {}       // no-op all methods\n    void drawLine(...) override {}\n    // ...\n};\n```\n\n---\n\n## 10. Singleton Architecture\n\n```cpp\n// Global singletons (accessed everywhere)\nGameEngine    *TheGameEngine;\nGameLogic     *TheGameLogic;\nGameClient    *TheGameClient;\nMessageStream *TheMessageStream;\nDisplay       *TheDisplay;        // Swap for NullDisplay\nThingFactory  *TheThingFactory;\nModuleFactory *TheModuleFactory;\n```\n\n---\n\n## 11. Serialization / Snapshot\n\nAll game objects inherit `Snapshot`:\n```cpp\nclass Snapshot {\n    virtual void xfer(Xfer *xfer);         // Serialize full state\n    virtual void crc(Xfer *xfer);          // Checksum for MP sync\n    virtual void loadPostProcess();\n};\n```\n\nUsed for save/load AND multiplayer CRC verification. Can snapshot game state at any tick for dataset generation.\n\n---\n\n## 12. State Extraction Pattern (for pybind11)\n\n```cpp\n// Iterate all objects for a player\nPlayer *player = TheGameLogic->getPlayer(playerIndex);\nfor (Object *obj = player->getFirstObject(); obj; obj = obj->getNextObject()) {\n    ObjectID   id     = obj->getId();\n    Coord3D    pos    = obj->getPosition();\n    Int        hp     = obj->getHealth();\n    AsciiString type  = obj->getTemplate()->getName();\n    ObjectShroudStatus vis = obj->getShroudStatus(observerPlayerIndex);\n}\n\n// Fog of war grid\nPartitionManager *pm = ThePartitionManager;\n// Query cell shroud by world coordinate\n```\n\n---\n\n## 13. Build Dependencies (to strip for headless)\n\n| Keep | Strip |\n|------|-------|\n| `GameEngine/` (all) | `GameEngineDevice/W3DDevice/` |\n| `Libraries/Source/` | `GameEngineDevice/MilesAudioDevice/` |\n| `GameLogic/` | `GameEngineDevice/VideoDevice/` |\n| `GameNetwork/` (commands only) | `GameEngineDevice/Win32Device/` |\n| | `DirectX SDK` |\n| | `Miles SDK` |\n| | `Bink SDK` |\n| | `GameSpy SDK` |\n\n---\n\n## 14. Headless Engine Build Plan\n\n1. Create `CMakeLists.txt` targeting `GameEngine/` sources only\n2. Implement `NullDisplay : public Display` (all no-ops)\n3. Implement `NullAudioDevice` (all no-ops)\n4. Replace `WinMain` with a headless `main()` entry point\n5. Initialize `TheGameLogic` directly — skip `Win32GameEngine`\n6. Run loop: `TheGameLogic->update()` at 30 Hz\n7. Expose state/actions via pybind11 → Python gym environment\n\n**Key risk:** Win32 API calls scattered throughout codebase. May need stub headers for `HWND`, `HINSTANCE` etc. on non-Windows builds (or build under Wine/Windows VM initially).\n"
}
```

MODEL.transformer_dim)\n    assert pooled.shape == (B, MODEL.transformer_dim)\n\n\ndef test_max_entities(encoder):\n    \"\"\"Should handle up to max_entities without OOM.\"\"\"\n    B, N, D = 1, STATE.max_entities, STATE.entity_feature_dim\n    x = torch.zeros(B, N, D)\n    mask = torch.ones(B, N, dtype=torch.bool)\n    per_entity, pooled = encoder(x, mask)\n    assert per_entity.shape == (B, dtype=torch.bool)\n    _, pooled = encoder(x, mask)\n    pooled.sum().backward()\n    assert x.grad is not None\n```\n\n## File 2: src/zh_copilot/model/entity_encoder.py\n```python\n# src/zh_copilot/model/entity_encoder.py\n\"\"\"Transformer encoder for the entity (unit/building) list.\n\nInput:\n  features: (B, N, D)  — per-entity feature vectors\n  mask:     (B, N)     — True where entity slot is occupied\n\nOutput:\n  per_entity: (B, N, model_dim)  — contextual embedding per entity\n  pooled:     (B, model_dim)     — masked mean pool over valid entities\n\nUses PyTorch's built-in MultiheadAttention for efficiency.\nPadding mask applied so invalid (empty) entity slots don't pollute attention.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\n\n\nclass EntityEncoder(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Multi-layer Transformer encoder for the entity list.\n\n    Args:\n        feature_dim: Raw entity feature dimension (entity_feature_dim, 32)\n        model_dim:   Transformer hidden dimension (transformer_dim, 256)\n        num_layers:  Number of Transformer encoder layers (8)\n        num_heads:   Number of attention heads (8)\n        ff_dim:      Feed-forward hidden dim (default 4 × model_dim)\n        dropout:     Dropout rate (default 0.1)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        feature_dim: int = 32,\n        model_dim: int = 256,\n        num_layers: int = 8,\n        num_heads: int = 8,\n        ff_dim: int | None = None,\n        dropout: float = 0.1,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        ff_dim = ff_dim or model_dim * 4\n\n        self.input_proj = nn.Linear(feature_dim, model_dim)\n\n        layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(\n            d_model=model_dim,\n            nhead=num_heads,\n            dim_feedforward=ff_dim,\n            dropout=dropout,\n            batch_first=True,\n            norm_first=True,\n        )\n        self.transformer = nn.TransformerEncoder(layer, num_layers=num_layers)\n\n    def forward(\n        self,\n        features: torch.Tensor,   # (B, N, D)\n        mask: torch.Tensor,       # (B, N) bool — True where entity is valid\n    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n        x = self.input_proj(features)\n        padding_mask = ~mask\n        x = self.transformer(x, src_key_padding_mask=padding_mask)\n\n        valid = mask.unsqueeze(-1).float()\n        pooled = (x * valid).sum(dim=1)\n        count = valid.sum(dim=1).clamp(min=1.0)\n        pooled = pooled / count\n\n        return x, pooled\n```\n\n## Steps\n1. Write tests/model/test_entity_encoder.py\n2. Run tests — confirm FAIL with ModuleNotFoundError\n3. Write src/zh_copilot/model/entity_encoder.py\n4. 1	# Plan 1: Headless Engine & Game Interface
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Build a headless (no rendering, no audio, no input) version of the ZH game engine that can be driven by Python for AI training — stepping the simulation frame by frame, reading game state, and injecting actions.
6	
7	**Architecture:** Create a `HeadlessGameEngine` subclass that overrides all rendering/audio/input factory methods with null stubs. The engine runs on Windows (Intel Mac VM) because it remains deeply Win32-tied (ATL, HINSTANCE, timeGetTime, windows.h in every TU). Python communicates with it via ZeroMQ: the Windows machine runs the engine + a Python ZMQ server, the M4 Mac (or same machine) runs PyTorch training and sends actions / receives observations.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** MSVC 2019+ (Windows VM on Intel Mac), C++17, pybind11, ZeroMQ (pyzmq), Python 3.11
10	
11	---
12	
13	## Prerequisites
14	
15	- Intel Mac running Windows 10/11 via Parallels, VMware Fusion, or Boot Camp
16	- Visual Studio 2019 or 2022 (Community edition is free) installed in Windows VM
17	- The original ZH game installed (needed for `.big` asset files for INI loading)
18	- Python 3.11 installed in the Windows VM
19	- `pip install pybind11 pyzmq`
20	
21	---
22	
23	## File Structure
24	
25	All new files go under `src/engine/` in the zh-copilot repo (accessible from both Mac and Windows via shared folder):
26	
27	```
28	src/
29	  engine/
30	    CMakeLists.txt
31	    headless/
32	      HeadlessGameEngine.h         # HeadlessGameEngine class declaration
33	      HeadlessGameEngine.cpp       # Factory method overrides + stepFrame()
34	      HeadlessMain.cpp             # main() replaces WinMain, no window needed
35	      stubs/
36	        NullAudioManager.h         # isMusicAlreadyLoaded() = true, all else no-op
37	        NullAudioManager.cpp
38	        NullGameClient.h           # No-op GameClient (no rendering, no input)
39	        NullGameClient.cpp
40	        NullRadar.h                # No-op Radar
41	        NullRadar.cpp
42	        NullWebBrowser.h           # No-op WebBrowser
43	        NullParticleSystemManager.h
44	    state/
45	      StateExtractor.h             # Reads game state → StateObservation struct
46	      StateExtractor.cpp
47	      ActionInjector.h             # Translates ActionCommand → GameMessage
48	      ActionInjector.cpp
49	      Protocol.h                   # Shared data structs (observation + action)
50	    server/
