{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/docs/source-code/replay-findings.md",
  "content": "# ZH Replay Analysis — Findings from Real .rep Files\n\n> **Date:** 2026-04-01\n> **Source:** 5 community replays from GameReplays.org (1v1, various factions)\n> **Purpose:** Validate parser, characterise action space, correct plan-2 spec errors\n\n---\n\n## 1. Parser Format Bugs Found (Now Fixed)\n\n### Bug 1: Command Arg Encoding — Declarations Before Data\n\n**Plan spec said** (wrong):\n```\n(arg_type, arg_count, <data>) interleaved per group\n```\n\n**Real format**:\n```\nPhase 1: all (arg_type, arg_count) declarations in sequence\nPhase 2: all payload data in the same order\n```\nFixed in `parser.py::_parse_commands`. Old interleaved parser desynced after ~7 commands.\n\n### Bug 2: game_options String Format — Semicolon-Delimited\n\n**Plan spec said** (wrong):\n```\nM=maps/x.map C=1 F=America H=1 T=1 C=2 F=China H=1 T=2\n```\n\n**Real format**:\n```\nUS=1;M=<2-char-prefix><path>;...;S=H<name>,<hex>,<hex>,<faction_code>,<team>,...:...\n```\nFixed in `game_options.py`. Key differences:\n- Semicolon delimited (not space)\n- Map path has 2-char hex prefix to strip\n- Players encoded in `S=` slot string, not individual `C=`/`F=` tokens\n- Faction codes: `FT`=America, `AT`=China, `TT`=GLA (community replay codes)\n\n---\n\n## 2. Message Type Enum — Plan Values Were Wrong\n\n**Critical:** `message_types.py` had incorrect enum values for everything from ~1040 onwards.\nThe plan was written from memory; actual values were verified against `MessageStream.h`.\n\nKey corrections:\n\n| Action | Plan (wrong) | Correct |\n|--------|-------------|---------|\n| `DO_MOVETO` | 1056 | **1068** |\n| `DO_ATTACKMOVETO` | 1057 | **1069** |\n| `DO_SPECIAL_POWER` | 1086 | **1040** |\n| `QUEUE_UNIT_CREATE` | 1082 | **1047** |\n| `DOZER_CONSTRUCT` | 1076 | **1049** |\n| `SET_RALLY_POINT` | 1084 | **1043** |\n| `PURCHASE_SCIENCE` | 1085 | **1044** |\n| `SWITCH_WEAPONS` | 1095 | **1079** |\n| `LOGIC_CRC` | 1075 | **1095** |\n| `SELL` | 1079 | **1052** |\n| `EVACUATE` | 1091 | **1054** |\n\nThe 1059–1061 combat range (`DO_ATTACK_OBJECT`, `DO_FORCE_ATTACK_OBJECT`, `DO_FORCE_ATTACK_GROUND`) was correct.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Action Type Distribution (5 replays, ~38K commands)\n\nRanked by frequency (with corrected labels):\n\n| Count | Type | Category | Notes |\n|-------|------|----------|-------|\n| 10,412 | DESTROY_SELECTED_GROUP (1003) | select | Deselect on click — very frequent |\n| 6,012 | DO_MOVETO (1068) | move | Main move command |\n| 5,516 | CREATE_SELECTED_GROUP (1001) | select | Unit selection |\n| 3,876 | LOGIC_CRC (1095) | (sync) | Filter out — 1/sec MP sync |\n| 3,562 | AREA_SELECTION (1058) | select | Drag-select rectangle |\n| 1,479 | DO_ATTACK_OBJECT (1059) | attack | Direct attack |\n| 1,009 | QUEUE_UNIT_CREATE (1047) | produce | Train unit |\n| 724 | CAPTUREBUILDING (1081) | capture | |\n| 560 | DO_STOP (1074) | stop | |\n| 453 | SELECT_TEAM3 (1019) | select | Hotkey recall |\n| 410 | SELECT_TEAM4 (1020) | select | Hotkey recall |\n| 405 | SELECT_TEAM2 (1018) | select | Hotkey recall |\n| 356 | CREATE_SELECTED_GROUP_NO_SOUND (1002) | select | |\n| 327 | SET_RALLY_POINT (1043) | rally | (OBJECTID, LOCATION) |\n| 326 | DO_FORCE_ATTACK_GROUND (1061) | attack | Force-fire position |\n| 285 | DOZER_CONSTRUCT (1049) | build | Place building |\n| 260 | SELECT_TEAM1 (1017) | select | Hotkey recall |\n| 234 | SELECT_TEAM5 (1021) | select | Hotkey recall |\n| 195 | RESUME_CONSTRUCTION (1065) | build | |\n| 184 | DO_SPECIAL_POWER (1040) | ability | (location, integer, objectID) |\n| 128 | DO_SPECIAL_POWER_AT_OBJECT (1042) | ability | (integer, objectID) |\n| 119 | QUEUE_UPGRADE (1045) | research | (objectID, integer) |\n| 110 | GET_REPAIRED (1062) | repair | Go to repair depot |\n| 74 | PURCHASE_SCIENCE (1044) | general_power | General star |\n| 78 | DO_SPECIAL_POWER_AT_LOCATION (1041) | ability | |\n\n---\n\n## 4. Key Observations for AI Design\n\n### Selection dominates raw data (~50% of commands)\n`CREATE_SELECTED_GROUP`, `DESTROY_SELECTED_GROUP`, `AREA_SELECTION`, `SELECT_TEAM*` make up\nthe majority of commands. These are **UI meta-actions** — the AI doesn't need them in its\nabstract action space (it acts directly on entity IDs). Filter these when building training data.\n\n### Hotkey usage (control groups 0–9)\nPro players heavily use control groups (`SELECT_TEAM0`–`SELECT_TEAM9`). These appear as 1016–1025.\nConfirmed: SELECT_TEAM3 (1019) and SELECT_TEAM4 (1020) are among top commands.\n\n### LOGIC_CRC (1095) must be filtered\nOne CRC per ~30 frames in multiplayer. ~775/game at 13 min avg. Already handled by\n`is_crc` property in `parser.py` (filters both 1000 and 1095).\n\n### Production commands are present but not dominant\n- QUEUE_UNIT_CREATE: 1,009 (1047) ≈ 200/game — reasonable for a build-heavy RTS\n- DOZER_CONSTRUCT: 285 (1049) ≈ 57/game — build placement\n- QUEUE_UPGRADE: 119 (1045) ≈ 24/game — tech upgrades\n\n### SWITCH_WEAPONS is common (1079)\nPresent 3,876 times in old wrong enum (those were actually LOGIC_CRC at 1095).\nReal SWITCH_WEAPONS (1079) occurrences need to be re-measured with corrected enum.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Abstract Action Space vs Raw MsgType\n\nThe model uses 21 abstract action types (from `config.MODEL.num_action_types`), not raw MsgType values.\nThe mapping `ACTION_CATEGORIES` in `message_types.py` handles raw→abstract translation.\n\nSelect/CRC/UI commands are excluded from the abstract action space — the AI operates on\nentity IDs directly and doesn't need to model hotkey management or camera.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Faction Code Mapping (Unverified)\n\nCodes seen in community replays:\n- `FT` → America (observed in context)\n- `AT` → China (inferred from third slot)\n- `TT` → GLA (observed in context)\n\nStandard codes `USA`, `CHI`, `GLA` also supported. Mapping may be tournament-tool-specific.\nNeeds verification against a known replay with confirmed faction.\n"
}

> AGENT

Here's what changed and why:

**Critical bug fixed — `message_types.py`**:  
The plan had wrong enum values for everything from ~1040 onwards. Plan was written from memory, not the source. Key examples: `DOZER_CONSTRUCT` was `1076` (actually `INTERNET_HACK`) — real value is `1049`. `QUEUE_UNIT_CREATE` was `1082` (actually `DISABLEVEHICLE_HACK`) — real value is `1047`. The 1059–1061 attack range happened to be correct.

**Evidence from real data**: those 3,876 "SWITCH_WEAPONS" were actually `LOGIC_CRC` (1/sec sync, 775×5 games = ~13 min avg). Those 6,012 "DO_REPAIR" were actually `DO_MOVETO`. Etc.

**`parser.py`**: `is_crc` now filters both `1000` (network CRC) and `1095` (`LOGIC_CRC`).

**`plan-2-replay-parser.md`**: Both format bugs documented with correction notes.

**`docs/source-code/replay-findings.md`**: Full breakdown — distribution table, design implications (selection dominates raw data so filter it for training), faction codes.

**`IMPLEMENTATION.md`**: Phase 2 tasks 1–5 checked off.

**Model/tests — no changes needed.** `num_action_types = 21` is an abstract action space, not raw `MsgType` count. Model architecture is unaffected.

> DEVELOPER

ok great! thank youi have general question

> AGENT

Go ahead.

> DEVELOPER

we are going to train model from the scratch on replays...but each game is long and contains a lot of syb steps, such as base building, army creation...do you think is is ok to train model on 12k replays? or we need to learn model step by step, how to create units, how to move, how to build and them all together? or do it all at the same time? just question

> AGENT

Good question. Short answer: **train on full replays end-to-end, all actions at once.**

Here's why curriculum learning (step-by-step) is mostly unnecessary for imitation learning:

**The data is already naturally curricula'd**  
Every replay starts with base building, then unit production, then combat. The model sees thousands of examples of "early game → build barracks" just from the natural structure of games. You don't need to hand-craft stages.

**12K replays is borderline but workable**  
From the data we saw: ~200 unit trains per game, ~57 builds, ~1,200 moves. At 3 decisions/sec over a ~13 min game = ~2,340 decision steps per replay.

12K replays × ~2,340 steps = **~28M training samples**

That's plenty for imitation learning. AlphaStar used ~900K games but had far more complex action spaces. ZH is simpler.

**Where curriculum could actually help — but at the RL stage, not IL**  
When you get to Phase 5 (RL vs built-in AI), curriculum matters: Easy → Medium → Hard → Brutal. That's a real training stability concern. For IL on replays, it's unnecessary overhead.

**One thing that IS worth doing**: stratify your dataset by game phase when batching. Mix early-game and late-game samples in each batch so the model doesn't overfit to "always build" because early-game samples dominate the dataset. Easy to implement with a weighted sampler.

Bottom line: just train on full replays. Save curriculum thinking for RL.

> DEVELOPER

ok i found 35998 replays on https://www.gamereplays.org/ ...is it enogh?

> AGENT

Yes, ~36K replays is solid. That's well into the target range (10K–50K from the plan).

Rough numbers:
- 36K × ~2,340 steps = **~84M training samples**
- At 3 decisions/sec that's substantial coverage of all game phases and factions

A few things that will matter more than raw count:

**Quality filtering will shrink it**  
After `is_valid_for_training()` filters (desyncs, disconnects, <60s games), expect to lose 20–40%. You'll likely end up with ~20-28K usable replays. Still excellent.

**Version spread matters**  
ZH had patches (1.00–1.04). Balance changed. Ideally filter to 1.04 only (tournament standard). Check `version_number` in the header.

**Skill level matters more than quantity**  
36K random replays including noobs < 10K high-skill replays. GameReplays.org tends to be higher quality but still mixed. If you can filter by rating/tournament tag, do it.

**Faction balance**  
Check if USA/China/GLA are roughly even. If GLA is underrepresented, the model will play GLA poorly regardless of total size.

Short answer: **yes, 36K is enough. Focus on quality filtering over collecting more.**

> DEVELOPER

ok, we have next steps to play agains built-in ai in the game...will it be good training strategy? or not realy? if yes how many plays do you think we need to have?

> AGENT

**Yes, but it's a ceiling problem, not a starting point.**

Here's the honest breakdown:

**Built-in AI is good for RL bootstrapping, bad as sole teacher**

The ZH built-in AI (especially Brutal) is rule-based and predictable. It has fixed patterns — specific build orders, known rush timings, repetitive strategies. A model trained *only* against it will learn to exploit those patterns, not play general RTS.

**Recommended order (what the plan already says):**

1. **IL on 36K replays first** — model learns general human-like play
2. **Fine-tune with RL vs Easy/Medium** — stabilize, fix glaring mistakes  
3. **Progress to Hard → Brutal** — push skill ceiling
4. **Self-play league** — break out of built-in AI ceiling, learn vs unpredictable opponents

Skipping step 1 and going straight to RL vs built-in AI would require **10–50× more games** to learn basics from scratch. IL gives you a huge head start.

**How many RL games needed:**

| Stage | Approx games needed |
|-------|-------------------|
| vs Easy (stabilize IL) | ~5K–10K |
| vs Medium | ~20K–50K |
| vs Hard | ~50K–100K |
| vs Brutal | ~100K–500K |
| Self-play | millions |

These are rough estimates based on similar RTS work (StarCraft, Warcraft). ZH is simpler so lower end is achievable.

**The real bottleneck is speed, not count.**  
If your headless engine runs at 10× realtime, 100K games = ~130 hours of CPU time. At 30× realtime = ~43 hours. That's why parallel environments (the plan mentions N instances) matter more than anything else.

**Bottom line:** Built-in AI is a great training target *after* IL, not instead of it. The plan's order is correct.

> DEVELOPER

ok...what if we play our model agains our model? will it help?

> AGENT

**Yes — that's self-play, and it's the most powerful training signal available.**

This is exactly what Phase 5 "Self-play League" in the plan describes. It's how AlphaStar, OpenAI Five, and AlphaGo reached superhuman level.

**Why it works better than built-in AI:**

Built-in AI has fixed patterns → model learns to exploit those specific patterns, not general strategy.

Self-play opponent adapts as you improve → the challenge scales automatically. You can never "solve" yourself.

**The core problem: catastrophic forgetting**

If you just play current model vs current model, it converges to a Nash equilibrium — often a degenerate one (both players do the same thing, e.g. both rush with one strategy). The model forgets how to counter strategies it hasn't seen in a while.

**Solution: league training (what the plan describes)**

Instead of model vs model, you maintain a **pool**:
- **Main agent** — the model you're actively training
- **Past snapshots** — frozen checkpoints from earlier training
- **Exploiters** — agents specifically trained to find weaknesses in the main agent

Main agent plays against all of them. This prevents forgetting and forces robustness.

**Practical timeline for ZH:**

| Phase | What happens |
|-------|-------------|
| After IL | Model plays like a decent human |
| RL vs built-in | Fixes obvious mistakes, learns to win consistently |
| Self-play starts | Model discovers novel strategies, patches own weaknesses |
| League mature | Generalist play, hard to exploit |

**When to start self-play:**  
Not immediately. Start self-play only after the model can beat Medium AI reliably. Two random IL models playing each other early on is just chaos — neither has enough skill to provide useful signal.

Short answer: **self-play is the endgame strategy, not the starting point. The sequence matters.**

1	# Plan 5: Reinforcement Learning Training Pipeline
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Implement a complete PPO-based reinforcement learning training pipeline that trains `ZHCopilotModel` end-to-end using shaped rewards, GAE advantage estimation, self-play league matchmaking, and a CLI training script that loads from IL checkpoints.
6	
7	**Architecture:** A `RewardShaper` converts raw `GameState` deltas to a shaped scalar reward. A `RolloutBuffer` accumulates timesteps (including LSTM hidden states) and computes GAE on-policy advantages. `PPOTrainer` performs clipped surrogate updates on mini-batches drawn from the buffer. A `League` class manages a pool of frozen past snapshots and exploiter agents for self-play diversity, using ELO to track relative strength.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** PyTorch 2.x, MPS (Apple Silicon GPU), pytest, ZeroMQ (via Plan 1 `ZHEnv`), Python 3.11
10	
11	**Note:** RL training loop (`scripts/train_rl.py`) requires engine from Plan 1. All unit tests run on M4 without engine.
12	
13	---
14	
15	## File Structure
16	
17	```
18	src/zh_copilot/training/
19	├── __init__.py
20	├── reward.py              # RewardShaper: GameState delta → scalar reward
21	├── rollout_buffer.py      # RolloutBuffer: experience storage + GAE
22	├── ppo.py                 # PPOTrainer: clipped surrogate PPO update
23	└── league.py              # League: self-play snapshot pool + ELO
24	
25	scripts/
26	└── train_rl.py            # CLI: loads IL checkpoint, runs PPO loop
27	
28	tests/training/
29	├── __init__.py
30	├── test_reward.py

32	├── test_ppo.py

35	
36	---
37	
38	## Task 1: Reward Shaper
39	
40	**Files:**
41	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/reward.py`
42	- Create: `tests/training/__init__.py`
43	- Create: `tests/training/test_reward.py`
44	
45	The `RewardShaper` accepts the previous `GameState`, the current `GameState`, a `done` flag, and the current training step. It computes a shaped scalar reward as a weighted sum of signal components, where shaped (non-terminal) components decay linearly to zero over `decay_steps`.
46	
47	### Component definitions
48	
49	| Signal | Computation |
50	|---|---|
51	| Win/loss | `+reward_win` if `done and own team alive`, `-reward_win` if `done and own team dead` |
52	| Resource delta | `(money_now - money_prev) * reward_resources` (clamped to ±1.0 before weight) |
53	| Kill reward | `(enemy_units_prev - enemy_units_now) * reward_kills` (clamped ≥ 0) |
54	| Unit loss penalty | `(own_units_prev - own_units_now) * reward_losses` (clamped ≤ 0), weight is negative |
55	| Map control | `(fraction of spatial cells with own units) * reward_map_control` |
56	| Tech reward | `(tech_level_now - tech_level_prev) * reward_tech` |
57	
58	Shaped reward decay: after `decay_steps` training steps, all non-terminal shaped signals are multiplied by `max(0, 1 - step / decay_steps)`. Terminal win/loss is never decayed.
59	
60	`player_idx` is the agent's player slot (0-indexed). Enemy indices are all other non-team slots.
61

1	# ZH-Copilot: AI Bot for C&C Generals Zero Hour
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-03-30
4	**Status:** Design approved, pending implementation plan
5	
6	---
7	
8	## 1. Overview
9	
10	ZH-Copilot is an AI bot that plays Command & Conquer: Generals Zero Hour at a competitive level. It operates in two modes:
11	
12	- **Autopilot:** Bot plays fully autonomously. Human starts the game and hands off control.
13	- **Copilot:** Bot plays, human provides strategic guidance via UI buttons (Attack, Defend, Rush, Farm, Turtle, Auto).
14	
15	**Target:** 70%+ win rate against good-to-top human players. All three factions (USA, China, GLA). All official maps.
16	
17	**Deployment:** Screen capture + keyboard/mouse emulation. No game modification required.
18	
19	**Training:** Direct engine integration via headless build of the open-source EA codebase.
20

268	
269	### 6.2 Phase 2: RL vs Built-in AI
270	
271	**Setup:**
272	- Headless engine on Intel Mac (4-8 parallel instances)
273	- Agent plays full games vs built-in AI
274	- Curriculum: Easy -> Medium -> Hard -> Brutal
275	
276	**Reward Shaping:**
277	
278	| Signal | Weight | Decays |
279	|--------|--------|--------|
280	| Win/loss | 1.0 | No |
281	| Resources collected | 0.01 | Yes |
282	| Units killed (value) | 0.05 | Yes |
283	| Units lost (value) | -0.03 | Yes |
284	| Map control % | 0.02 | Yes |
285	| Tech progression | 0.01 | Yes |
286	
287	Shaped rewards decay over training. Eventually only win/loss matters.
288	
289	**Algorithm:** PPO (Proximal Policy Optimization)
290	- Batch size: 2048 steps
291	- Multiple parallel environments for throughput
292	
293	**Expected result:** Beats Brutal AI consistently. ~60% vs average humans.
294	
295	### 6.3 Phase 3: Self-play League
296	
297	**League System (simplified AlphaStar):**
298	- **Main agent** — always training, plays against exploiters + past selves
299	- **Past snapshots** — frozen checkpoints saved every N games
300	- **Exploiters** — agents trained to beat main agent's weaknesses
301	
302	**Why league:** Prevents rock-paper-scissors cycles in self-play. Forces robustness across strategies.
303	
304	**ELO tracking** for all agents. Matchmaking weighted toward close-ELO opponents.
305	
306	**Expected result:** 70%+ win rate vs good-to-top humans.
307	
308	### 6.4 Hardware Setup
309	
310	**Phase 1 (imitation):**
311	- Intel Mac: pre-process replays into datasets offline
312	- M4 Mac: train model locally (no live sync needed)
313	
314	**Phase 2-3 (RL):**
315	- Start with Intel Mac only (engine + training, simpler)
316	- Scale to dual-machine setup if speed becomes bottleneck:

  "new_string": "### 6.2 Phase 2: RL vs Built-in AI\n\n**Training strategy overview — why this order matters:**\n\nStart with IL (replays) to give the model a human-like baseline. Only then move to RL.\nSkipping IL and going straight to RL vs built-in AI requires ~10–50× more games to learn basics.\n\n| Phase | What happens |\n|-------|-------------|\n| After IL | Model plays like a decent human |\n| RL vs built-in | Fixes obvious mistakes, learns to win consistently |\n| Self-play starts | Model discovers novel strategies, patches own weaknesses |\n| League mature | Generalist play, hard to exploit |\n\n**Estimated games needed per RL stage:**\n\n| Stage | Approx games needed |\n|-------|-------------------|\n| vs Easy (stabilize IL) | ~5K–10K |\n| vs Medium | ~20K–50K |\n| vs Hard | ~50K–100K |\n| vs Brutal | ~100K–500K |\n| Self-play league | millions |\n\n> These are estimates based on comparable RTS work (StarCraft, Warcraft).\n> ZH is simpler — lower end of each range is likely achievable.\n> The real bottleneck is **speed**, not count. Run parallel environments (4-8 instances).\n> At 10× realtime: 100K games ≈ 130 CPU-hours. At 30×: ≈ 43 hours.\n\n**Start self-play only after beating Medium AI reliably.**\nTwo weak IL models playing each other produce noise, not signal.\n\n**Setup:**\n- Headless engine on Intel Mac (4-8 parallel instances)\n- Agent plays full games vs built-in AI\n- Curriculum: Easy -> Medium -> Hard -> Brutal"

  "new_string": "### 6.3 Phase 3: Self-play League\n\n**Why self-play beats built-in AI as the sole teacher:**\n- Built-in AI has fixed patterns → model learns to exploit those specific patterns, not general strategy\n- Self-play opponent adapts as you improve → challenge scales automatically\n\n**The core problem — catastrophic forgetting:**\nIf you play current model vs current model only, it converges to a degenerate Nash equilibrium\n(e.g. both players always rush the same strategy). The model forgets how to counter\nstrategies it hasn't seen in a while.\n\n**Solution — league training:**\n\n**League System (simplified AlphaStar):**\n- **Main agent** — always training, plays against exploiters + past selves\n- **Past snapshots** — frozen checkpoints saved every N games\n- **Exploiters** — agents trained to beat main agent's weaknesses\n\n**Why league:** Prevents rock-paper-scissors cycles in self-play. Forces robustness across strategies.\n\n**ELO tracking** for all agents. Matchmaking weighted toward close-ELO opponents.\n\n**Expected result:** 70%+ win rate vs good-to-top humans."
}

1	# Plan 5: Reinforcement Learning Training Pipeline
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Implement a complete PPO-based reinforcement learning training pipeline that trains `ZHCopilotModel` end-to-end using shaped rewards, GAE advantage estimation, self-play league matchmaking, and a CLI training script that loads from IL checkpoints.
6	
7	**Architecture:** A `RewardShaper` converts raw `GameState` deltas to a shaped scalar reward. A `RolloutBuffer` accumulates timesteps (including LSTM hidden states) and computes GAE on-policy advantages. `PPOTrainer` performs clipped surrogate updates on mini-batches drawn from the buffer. A `League` class manages a pool of frozen past snapshots and exploiter agents for self-play diversity, using ELO to track relative strength.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** PyTorch 2.x, MPS (Apple Silicon GPU), pytest, ZeroMQ (via Plan 1 `ZHEnv`), Python 3.11
10	
11	**Note:** RL training loop (`scripts/train_rl.py`) requires engine from Plan 1. All unit tests run on M4 without engine.
12	
13	---
14	
15	## File Structure

1	# Plan 4: Imitation Learning Training
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Train the ZHCopilotModel via behaviour cloning on HDF5 replay datasets, producing a checkpoint that accurately predicts human expert actions.
6	
7	**Architecture:** A PyTorch `Dataset` wraps one or more HDF5 files produced by Plan 2's `ReplayDatasetWriter`. Three losses (action-type cross-entropy, pointer cross-entropy, value MSE) are combined and minimised by `ILTrainer`, which manages LSTM hidden-state continuity within each replay episode. Strategy archetypes are assigned to replays by K-means clustering of opening-command frequencies before training, providing soft supervision for the strategy-embedding head.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** PyTorch 2.x, h5py, scikit-learn (K-means), TensorBoard, numpy, tqdm, pytest, MPS (Apple Silicon).
10	
11	**M4 Mac:** All tasks executable on M4 (no engine dependency). Synthetic HDF5 files replace real replay data for every test.
12	
13	---
14	
15	## File Structure
16	
17	```
18	src/zh_copilot/training/
19	├── __init__.py                  # exports ILTrainer, ILDataset, compute_il_loss
20	├── il_dataset.py                # PyTorch Dataset wrapping ReplayDataset HDF5 files
21	├── losses.py                    # action_type_loss, pointer_loss, value_loss, il_loss
22	├── il_trainer.py                # ILTrainer: train_epoch, val_epoch, checkpointing
23	└── strategy_cluster.py          # K-means clustering of replay opening commands
24	
25	scripts/
26	└── train_il.py                  # CLI entry point
27	
28	tests/training/
29	├── __init__.py
30	├── fixtures/                    # tiny synthetic .h5 files
31	│   └── make_fixtures.py         # helper that writes fixtures used by tests
32	├── test_il_dataset.py
33	├── test_losses.py
34	├── test_il_trainer.py
35	└── test_strategy_cluster.py
36	```
37	
38	---
39	
40	## Shared test-fixture helper
41	
42	Every test module that needs HDF5 data calls `make_synthetic_h5()`, defined once in
43	`tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py`, which writes a temporary HDF5 file using
44	`ReplayDatasetWriter` with random numpy arrays. This keeps tests fully self-contained and
45	independent of the game engine.
46	
47	---
48	
49	### Task 1: PyTorch Dataset (`il_dataset.py`)
50	
51	**Files:**
52	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/__init__.py`
53	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py`
54	- Create: `tests/training/__init__.py`
55	- Create: `tests/training/fixtures/__init__.py`
56	- Create: `tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py`
57	- Create: `tests/training/test_il_dataset.py`
58	
59	The dataset concatenates any number of HDF5 files. Each `__getitem__` returns a dict of
60	`torch.Tensor` objects plus integer labels. A class-level `split()` factory produces disjoint
61	train/val subsets by file index (not by step index, to avoid leaking temporal information).
62	
63	#### Step 1: Create the fixture helper
64	
65	- [ ] **Step 1.1: Create `tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py`**
66	
67	```python
68	# tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py
69	"""Synthetic HDF5 fixture builder for training tests.
70	
71	Creates small HDF5 files using ReplayDatasetWriter so tests do not
72	need the game engine or real replay data.
73	"""
74	
75	from __future__ import annotations
76	
77	from pathlib import Path
78	
79	import numpy as np
80	import h5py
81	
82	from zh_copilot.config import STATE
83	
84	
85	def make_synthetic_h5(
86	    path: str | Path,
87	    num_steps: int = 64,
88	    num_action_types: int = 21,
89	    seed: int = 0,
90	) -> Path:
91	    """Write a minimal synthetic HDF5 file that mimics ReplayDatasetWriter output.
92	
93	    Args:
94	        path: Destination file path.
95	        num_steps: Number of decision steps to write.
96	        num_action_types: Range for random action_type labels (0..num_action_types-1).
97	        seed: Random seed for reproducibility.
98	
99	    Returns:
100	        Path to the written file.

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

```
100	        Path to the written file.
101	    """
102	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
103	    path = Path(path)
104	    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
105	
106	    R = STATE.spatial_resolution      # 128
107	    C = STATE.spatial_channels        # 40
108	    N = STATE.max_entities            # 1024
109	    D = STATE.entity_feature_dim      # 32
110	    S = STATE.global_scalar_dim       # 80
111	
112	    with h5py.File(path, "w") as f:
113	        f.create_dataset("spatial",
114	                         data=rng.standard_normal((num_steps, C, R, R)).astype(np.float32))
115	        f.create_dataset("entity_features",
116	                         data=rng.standard_normal((num_steps, N, D)).astype(np.float32))
117	        f.create_dataset("entity_mask",
118	                         data=rng.integers(0, 2, size=(num_steps, N)).astype(bool))
119	        f.create_dataset("global_scalars",
120	                         data=rng.standard_normal((num_steps, S)).astype(np.float32))
121	        f.create_dataset("action_type",
122	                         data=rng.integers(0, num_action_types, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32))
123	        f.create_dataset("player_idx",
124	                         data=rng.integers(0, 2, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32))
125	        f.create_dataset("frame",
126	                         data=np.arange(num_steps, dtype=np.int32) * 10)
127	        f.create_dataset("game_time",
128	                         data=(np.arange(num_steps, dtype=np.float32) * (1.0 / 3.0)))
129	        # outcome: 1.0 = win, 0.0 = loss (one value per step, same for whole replay)
130	        outcome_val = float(rng.integers(0, 2))
131	        f.create_dataset("outcome",
132	                         data=np.full(num_steps, outcome_val, dtype=np.float32))
133	        # target_entity_idx: -1 means no entity target; otherwise 0..N-1
134	        target = rng.integers(-1, min(N, 32), size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32)
135	        f.create_dataset("target_entity_idx", data=target)
136	        f.attrs["num_steps"] = num_steps
137	
138	    return path
139	```

Good enough. The key info is on line 11: **"M4 Mac: All tasks executable on M4 (no engine dependency). Synthetic HDF5 files replace real replay data for every test."**

Plan 4 is fully M4-compatible. No engine, no Intel Mac, no Windows needed. All training code uses synthetic HDF5 fixtures.

**What's in Plan 4:**
- Task 1: `ILDataset` — PyTorch Dataset wrapping HDF5 files
- Task 2: `losses.py` — action_type CE + pointer CE + value MSE
- Task 3: `ILTrainer` — train/val loop, LSTM state continuity, checkpointing
- Task 4: `strategy_cluster.py` — K-means clustering of replay openings
- CLI: `scripts/train_il.py`

**Blockers: none.** We can start now.

> DEVELOPER

we will need to train model on batches each batch has to have different time length replays and different fractions

> AGENT

Good catch. The current plan has `__getitem__` returning **single steps**, not sequences. LSTM continuity is handled in the trainer by feeding steps one-at-a-time per episode.

Your requirement is different: each batch item = a **sequence** of steps from one replay, and sequences across the batch have different lengths (variable T) and come from different replay fractions (early/mid/late game windows).

This requires design changes before we execute Plan 4:

**What needs to change:**

| Component | Current plan | Required |
|---|---|---|
| `ILDataset.__getitem__` | returns single step dict | returns sequence dict `(T, ...)` |
| `DataLoader` | default collate | custom `collate_fn` — pad to max T in batch, return lengths |
| `ILTrainer` | step-by-step LSTM | `pack_padded_sequence` → LSTM → `pad_packed_sequence` |
| Sampling | by step index | by (replay, start_offset, length) |

**Two sub-decisions to nail down first:**

1. **Fixed chunks vs variable?** Fixed-length chunks (e.g. 128 steps, stride 64) are simpler and still cover different fractions. Variable-length requires `PackedSequence`. Which do you want?

2. **Fraction sampling strategy?** Random windows per epoch, or explicit early/mid/late splits?

My recommendation: **fixed-length chunks with random offset** (simple, covers all fractions, no padding needed if chunks are uniform). Variable-length only if you have a specific reason.

Okay, bro. U I don't want to overfit my model if for example for example if one batch we will have a lot of replace with rash strategy or we will have only GLA replace in one batch. In this case we'll overfit our model. We need to mix them. to avoid that we need to handle it somehow. Do you agree with me or not?

> AGENT

Yes, 100% agree. This is **batch composition bias** — if one batch is all GLA rushes, the model gets a skewed gradient update.

Standard fix: **stratified sampling**.

When building each batch, sample uniformly across:
- Faction (USA / China / GLA)
- Strategy archetype (rush / eco / turtle / etc.)
- Game phase / replay fraction (early / mid / late)

So every batch has a balanced mix of all three dimensions.

In PyTorch this is a custom `Sampler` — you assign each replay a `(faction, archetype, phase)` label, then the sampler ensures each batch draws roughly equal counts from each stratum.

**This is a dataset/sampler design question, not a model question.** The model architecture doesn't change. Plan 4's `ILDataset` and `ILTrainer` need:
1. Metadata labels per replay (faction, archetype, phase) stored in HDF5
2. A `StratifiedBatchSampler` instead of default random sampling

```
Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile my findings into a comprehensive report.

---

## Report: Stratified Batch Sampling in ZH-Copilot Training Pipeline

### 1. Current Metadata Structure in HDF5 (Plan 2)

**ReplayDatasetWriter stores per-replay and per-step:**

**Per-step datasets:**
- `spatial` (N, 40, 128, 128) float32
- `entity_features` (N, 1024, 32) float32  
- `entity_mask` (N, 1024) bool
- `global_scalars` (N, 80) float32
- `action_type` (N,) int32
- `player_idx` (N,) int32
- `frame` (N,) int32
- `game_time` (N,) float32
- `outcome` (N,) float32 (win=1.0, loss=0.0) — added in Plan 4
- `target_entity_idx` (N,) int32 — added in Plan 4
- `strategy_label_steps` (N,) int32 — added post-clustering in Plan 4

**Per-replay attributes (file-level):**
- `num_steps` (int)
- `strategy_label` (int, 0-19) — written after K-means clustering

**Currently NOT stored (need to add):**
- `faction` (e.g., "USA", "China", "GLA") — player faction per-step or per-replay
- `map_name` — replay map metadata
- `opponent_faction` — opponent's faction  
- `game_phase` markers (early/mid/late game) — NOT calculated yet

---

### 2. Strategy Clustering Implementation (Plan 4, Task 4)

**What happens:**
- K-means clustering on opening command-frequency histograms (first 5 minutes / 9000 frames @ 30fps)
- Feature vector: 21-bin histogram of `action_type` values, L2-normalized
- Produces: K=20 strategy archetypes per faction matchup (not per-matchup yet, just global)
- Output: `strategy_label` (int 0-19) written to HDF5 file attributes and per-step dataset

**Timing in pipeline:**
- Runs AFTER replay data collection (Plan 2), BEFORE training (Plan 4)
- Called as a preprocessing step before `ILTrainer.train()`
- Accessible via `StrategyClusterer` class (fit/predict/save/load)

**Current limitations:**
- Clusters ALL replays into same 20 archetypes (not faction-stratified)
- Only considers opening 5 minutes, ignores mid/late game strategy shifts
- No per-faction clustering (USA openers differ from China/GLA, but not separated)

---

### 3. Current Batch Construction (Plan 4, ILTrainer & DataLoader)

**How batches are built:**
```python
# plan-4-imitation-learning.md, ILTrainer.__init__
self.train_loader = DataLoader(
    train_dataset,
    batch_size=batch_size,
    shuffle=True,  # ← random shuffle across episodes
    num_workers=num_workers,
    drop_last=True,
    pin_memory=(self.device.type == "cuda"),
)
```

**Key points:**
- **Random sampling** — no stratification
- **Shuffles across episodes** — steps from different replays are mixed in batches
- **LSTM hidden state is NOT carried** — reset to None per batch (standard for IL)
- No diversity guarantees: batches could accidentally contain many replays of the same archetype/faction/phase

---

### 4. Existing Mentions of Batch Diversity / Overfitting Prevention

**Found in code:**
- **Plan 4, ILTrainer design:** Mentions "correct LSTM hidden-state handling" but no batch composition strategy
- **Plan 4, losses.py:** Multi-task loss (action + pointer + value) to prevent action-type overfitting
- **Plan 5, RolloutBuffer:** `normalize_advantages=True` flag for GAE mini-batches (not for sampling diversity)
- **Plan 5, self-play league:** "Use league training (main agent + past snapshots + exploiters), not naive current-vs-current self-play" — mentions avoiding degenerate equilibria, but this is RL-level, not batch-level

**NOT found:**
- No stratified sampling by faction, strategy archetype, or game phase
- No explicit discussion of batch composition preventing model overfitting to dominant strategies
- No curriculum or phase-based sampling

---

### 5. Recommendation: Where Stratified Sampling Fits

### **Best Location: NEW "Plan 2.5 - Replay Preprocessing & Stratification" (between Plan 2 and Plan 4)**

**Rationale:**
- Metadata (faction, opponent, game phase) must be extracted in Plan 2 (when replays are parsed)
- Strategy clustering happens in Plan 4, but batch sampling should be independent of clustering
- Creating a dedicated preprocessing step avoids mixing concerns in ILTrainer

### **Implementation Scope:**

#### **Phase 1: Extend Metadata in Plan 2 (ReplayDatasetWriter)**

Add per-replay attributes to HDF5:
```
f.attrs["faction_0"]        # "USA", "China", "GLA"
f.attrs["faction_1"]        # opponent faction
f.attrs["map_name"]         # e.g., "multi_1"
f.attrs["replay_duration"]  # frames or seconds
f.attrs["replay_quality"]   # inferred from command count/desync
```

Add per-step dataset:
```
f["faction_step"]           # (N,) int8 — replay player's faction (0=USA, 1=China, 2=GLA)
f["game_phase_step"]        # (N,) int8 — 0=early, 1=mid, 2=late (based on frame / game_time)
```

#### **Phase 2: NEW Task — Batch Sampler for Plan 4 (il_dataset.py enhancement)**

Create `StratifiedBatchSampler` class:

```python
class StratifiedBatchSampler:
    """Sample batches stratified by faction, strategy archetype, and game phase.
    
    Usage:
        sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(
            dataset=il_dataset,
            batch_size=256,
            stratify_by=['faction', 'strategy_label', 'game_phase'],
            max_same_group=0.3,  # max 30% from one group per batch
        )
        loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_sampler=sampler)
    """
```

**Sampling algorithm:**
1. Group all steps by (faction, strategy_label, game_phase) bins
2. On each batch request: sample equal numbers from each non-empty group (round-robin or proportional)
3. Constraint: no more than `max_same_group` fraction from any single group
4. Falls back to uniform sampling if groups are imbalanced

#### **Phase 3: Update ILTrainer (Plan 4, Task 3)**

```python
# Instead of:
self.train_loader = DataLoader(train_dataset, batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)

# Use:
sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(
    dataset=train_dataset,
    batch_size=batch_size,
    stratify_by=['faction', 'strategy_label'],  # exclude game_phase for now (too granular)
    max_same_group=0.4,
)
self.train_loader = DataLoader(train_dataset, batch_sampler=sampler)
```

---

### 6. Summary Table

| Aspect | Current State | Recommendation |
|--------|---------------|-----------------|
| **Faction metadata** | Not stored in HDF5 | Add per-replay attribute + per-step dataset in Plan 2 |
| **Game phase metadata** | Not tracked | Add per-step `game_phase_step` in Plan 2 (early/mid/late) |
| **Strategy archetypes** | K-means on opening 5min (Plan 4) → 20 labels | Keep as-is; good for soft supervision |
| **Batch construction** | Random shuffle (DataLoader) | Add StratifiedBatchSampler in Plan 4, Task 1 enhancement |
| **Batch diversity** | None enforced | Implement `max_same_group` constraint (e.g., ≤40% per group) |
| **Overfitting prevention** | Multi-task loss + val split | Combine with batch stratification for better generalization |
| **Where it fits** | N/A in current plan | Plan 2.5 (preprocessing) + Plan 4 enhancement (sampler) |

---

### **Deliverables for Implementation**

1. **Plan 2 (Replay Parser)** — extend `ReplayDatasetWriter`:
   - Add faction/opponent faction/map metadata to file attributes
   - Add per-step `game_phase_step` dataset (inferred from frame count)
   - Update ReplayFile/GameInfo to carry these fields

2. **Plan 2.5 (NEW)** — Metadata extraction utilities:
   - `extract_faction_from_replay(replay_bytes)` 
   - `infer_game_phase(frame_count, total_frames)`
   - `validate_metadata_completeness(hdf5_path)`

3. **Plan 4 (Imitation Learning)** — New sampler in `il_dataset.py`:
   - `StratifiedBatchSampler` class with configurable groups
   - Tests for uniform group coverage and constraint enforcement
   - Update `ILTrainer.__init__` to accept optional sampler

4. **Tests:**
   - Verify metadata stored correctly in HDF5
   - Verify batch sampler produces balanced groups
   - Measure model generalization with vs. without stratification (Plan 4 validation)

---

This approach ensures **faction/strategy/phase diversity without disrupting the existing IL pipeline**, while keeping metadata collection at the natural extraction point (Plan 2) and sampling at the training point (Plan 4).
```

> AGENT

Here's where it fits:

---

**Root problem:** Faction + strategy archetype metadata isn't stored in HDF5 yet, so you can't stratify even if you wanted to.

**Two-place fix:**

**Plan 2 (Replay Parser) — store the metadata:**
- Add per-replay HDF5 attributes: `faction_0`, `faction_1`, `map_name`
- Add per-step dataset: `game_phase` (0=early, 1=mid, 2=late, inferred from frame/total)
- This is the natural place — you already have the data when parsing

**Plan 4 (Imitation Learning) — use it when sampling:**
- Add `StratifiedBatchSampler` to `il_dataset.py`
- Groups by `(faction, strategy_label, game_phase)`
- Enforces a cap — e.g. max 30-40% from any one group per batch
- `ILTrainer` uses it instead of default random shuffle

**Plan 5 (RL) — reuse same sampler** for experience replay buffer if applicable.

---

**Current plan gaps:**
- Plan 2 doesn't save faction/phase metadata to HDF5
- Plan 4 has `shuffle=True` with no stratification whatsoever
- No sampler class exists anywhere

**My recommendation:** Update Plan 2 spec to add metadata fields, then add `StratifiedBatchSampler` as a new task in Plan 4 (before `ILTrainer`). No new plan file needed.

  "prompt": "You are updating a plan spec file for a Zero Hour (C&C Generals) AI copilot project. Do NOT implement any code — only update the plan document.\n\n**Task:** Update `plans/plan-4-imitation-learning.md` to add a StratifiedBatchSampler task and update the ILTrainer task to use it.\n\n**Context:** Training the model on replays risks overfitting to dominant faction/strategy combinations if batches are not balanced. For example, if one batch contains only GLA rush replays, the gradient update is biased. We need a `StratifiedBatchSampler` that ensures each batch contains a balanced mix of faction, strategy archetype, and game phase.\n\n**Changes to make to plan-4-imitation-learning.md:**\n\n1. **Update the File Structure section** — add `strategy_sampler.py` to `src/zh_copilot/training/` and `test_strategy_sampler.py` to `tests/training/`:\n```\nsrc/zh_copilot/training/\n├── __init__.py\n├── il_dataset.py\n├── strategy_sampler.py       # ← NEW: StratifiedBatchSampler\n├── losses.py\n├── il_trainer.py\n└── strategy_cluster.py\n```\n\n2. **Insert a new Task (call it Task 2) after the existing Task 1 (ILDataset)** and renumber subsequent tasks. The new task spec:\n\n---\n### Task 2: Stratified Batch Sampler (`strategy_sampler.py`)\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_sampler.py`\n- Create: `tests/training/test_strategy_sampler.py`\n\n**Purpose:** Prevent batch composition bias during IL training. Without stratification, a batch could contain mostly GLA replays or only rush-strategy steps, causing biased gradient updates and overfitting to dominant distributions.\n\n**Design:** `StratifiedBatchSampler` is a PyTorch `Sampler` that groups dataset indices by `(faction, strategy_label, game_phase)` strata and yields batches with capped representation per stratum.\n\n#### Implementation spec\n\n```python\n# src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_sampler.py\nclass StratifiedBatchSampler(torch.utils.data.Sampler):\n    \"\"\"Yield balanced batches across (faction, strategy_label, game_phase) strata.\n\n    Args:\n        labels: Sequence of (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase) tuples,\n                one per dataset index. faction_id: 0=USA,1=China,2=GLA.\n                strategy_label: int 0..K-1 from K-means clustering.\n                game_phase: 0=early,1=mid,2=late.\n        batch_size: Number of samples per batch.\n        max_same_stratum: Maximum fraction of a batch from any single stratum.\n                          Default 0.4 (40%). Prevents one stratum dominating.\n        drop_last: If True, drop the last incomplete batch. Default True.\n        shuffle: Shuffle within each stratum before sampling. Default True.\n        seed: Random seed for reproducibility.\n\n    Usage:\n        labels = [(item['faction_id'], item['strategy_label'], item['game_phase'])\n                  for item in dataset]\n        sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=256, max_same_stratum=0.4)\n        loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_sampler=sampler)\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, labels, batch_size, max_same_stratum=0.4,\n                 drop_last=True, shuffle=True, seed=0): ...\n\n    def __iter__(self) -> Iterator[List[int]]:\n        \"\"\"Yield batches. Each batch draws round-robin from strata,\n        capped at max_same_stratum * batch_size per stratum.\"\"\"\n        ...\n\n    def __len__(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Total number of batches.\"\"\"\n        ...\n```\n\n#### Tests spec\n\n```python\n# tests/training/test_strategy_sampler.py\n# Tests to implement:\n\ndef test_batch_size_correct():\n    # All batches have exactly batch_size items (drop_last=True)\n\ndef test_no_stratum_exceeds_cap():\n    # No stratum contributes more than max_same_stratum * batch_size items per batch\n\ndef test_all_indices_covered():\n    # Over one full epoch, every index appears at least once\n\ndef test_single_stratum_fallback():\n    # When only one stratum exists, all batches come from it (no crash)\n\ndef test_reproducible_with_seed():\n    # Same seed produces identical batch sequences\n\ndef test_dataloader_integration():\n    # StratifiedBatchSampler works as batch_sampler in DataLoader\n```\n\n**Acceptance criteria:**\n- [ ] `StratifiedBatchSampler` passes all 6 tests\n- [ ] No stratum ever exceeds `max_same_stratum` fraction in any batch\n- [ ] Works with DataLoader as `batch_sampler=` argument\n---\n\n3. **Update Task 3 (ILTrainer)** — in the ILTrainer `__init__` spec, change the DataLoader construction from `shuffle=True` to use `StratifiedBatchSampler`:\n\nFind text like:\n```python\nself.train_loader = DataLoader(\n    train_dataset,\n    batch_size=batch_size,\n    shuffle=True,\n    ...\n)\n```\n\nChange to:\n```python\n# Build stratum labels for stratified sampling\nlabels = [(item['faction_id'], item['strategy_label'], item['game_phase'])\n          for item in train_dataset]\nsampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=batch_size, max_same_stratum=0.4)\nself.train_loader = DataLoader(\n    train_dataset,\n    batch_sampler=sampler,\n    num_workers=num_workers,\n    pin_memory=(self.device.type == \"cuda\"),\n)\n```\n\nAlso update the `__init__.py` exports to include `StratifiedBatchSampler`.\n\n4. **Update make_fixtures.py spec** — add `faction_id`, `strategy_label`, `game_phase` fields to the synthetic HDF5 fixture:\n```python\nf.create_dataset(\"faction_id\", data=rng.integers(0, 3, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int8))\nf.create_dataset(\"strategy_label\", data=rng.integers(0, 20, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32))\nf.create_dataset(\"game_phase\", data=rng.integers(0, 3, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int8))\n```\n\n**How to update:**\n1. Read the full file: `plans/plan-4-imitation-learning.md` (it's large, read in chunks of 100 lines)\n2. Make all 4 changes described above\n3. Preserve all existing content\n\nWorking directory: /Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot\n\nAfter editing, commit with message: \"docs: add StratifiedBatchSampler task to plan-4 imitation learning spec\"",

40	---
41	
42	## Shared test-fixture helper
43	
44	Every test module that needs HDF5 data calls `make_synthetic_h5()`, defined once in
45	`tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py`, which writes a temporary HDF5 file using
46	`ReplayDatasetWriter` with random numpy arrays. This keeps tests fully self-contained and
47	independent of the game engine.
48	
49	---
50	
51	### Task 1: PyTorch Dataset (`il_dataset.py`)
52	
53	**Files:**
54	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/__init__.py`
55	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py`
56	- Create: `tests/training/__init__.py`
57	- Create: `tests/training/fixtures/__init__.py`
58	- Create: `tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py`
59	- Create: `tests/training/test_il_dataset.py`
60	
61	The dataset concatenates any number of HDF5 files. Each `__getitem__` returns a dict of
62	`torch.Tensor` objects plus integer labels. A class-level `split()` factory produces disjoint
63	train/val subsets by file index (not by step index, to avoid leaking temporal information).
64	
65	#### Step 1: Create the fixture helper
66	
67	- [ ] **Step 1.1: Create `tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py`**
68	
69	```python
70	# tests/training/fixtures/make_fixtures.py
71	"""Synthetic HDF5 fixture builder for training tests.
72	
73	Creates small HDF5 files using ReplayDatasetWriter so tests do not
74	need the game engine or real replay data.
75	"""
76	
77	from __future__ import annotations
78	
79	from pathlib import Path
80	
81	import numpy as np
82	import h5py
83	
84	from zh_copilot.config import STATE
85	
86	
87	def make_synthetic_h5(
88	    path: str | Path,
89	    num_steps: int = 64,
90	    num_action_types: int = 21,
91	    seed: int = 0,
92	) -> Path:
93	    """Write a minimal synthetic HDF5 file that mimics ReplayDatasetWriter output.
94	
95	    Args:
96	        path: Destination file path.
97	        num_steps: Number of decision steps to write.
98	        num_action_types: Range for random action_type labels (0..num_action_types-1).
99	        seed: Random seed for reproducibility.
100	
101	    Returns:
102	        Path to the written file.
103	    """
104	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
105	    path = Path(path)
106	    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
107	
108	    R = STATE.spatial_resolution      # 128
109	    C = STATE.spatial_channels        # 40
110	    N = STATE.max_entities            # 1024
111	    D = STATE.entity_feature_dim      # 32
112	    S = STATE.global_scalar_dim       # 80
113	
114	    with h5py.File(path, "w") as f:
115	        f.create_dataset("spatial",
116	                         data=rng.standard_normal((num_steps, C, R, R)).astype(np.float32))
117	        f.create_dataset("entity_features",
118	                         data=rng.standard_normal((num_steps, N, D)).astype(np.float32))
119	        f.create_dataset("entity_mask",
120	                         data=rng.integers(0, 2, size=(num_steps, N)).astype(bool))
121	        f.create_dataset("global_scalars",
122	                         data=rng.standard_normal((num_steps, S)).astype(np.float32))
123	        f.create_dataset("action_type",
124	                         data=rng.integers(0, num_action_types, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32))
125	        f.create_dataset("player_idx",
126	                         data=rng.integers(0, 2, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32))
127	        f.create_dataset("frame",
128	                         data=np.arange(num_steps, dtype=np.int32) * 10)
129	        f.create_dataset("game_time",
130	                         data=(np.arange(num_steps, dtype=np.float32) * (1.0 / 3.0)))
131	        # outcome: 1.0 = win, 0.0 = loss (one value per step, same for whole replay)
132	        outcome_val = float(rng.integers(0, 2))
133	        f.create_dataset("outcome",
134	                         data=np.full(num_steps, outcome_val, dtype=np.float32))
135	        # target_entity_idx: -1 means no entity target; otherwise 0..N-1
136	        target = rng.integers(-1, min(N, 32), size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32)
137	        f.create_dataset("target_entity_idx", data=target)
138	        # Stratification fields: faction, strategy archetype, game phase
139	        f.create_dataset("faction_id",
140	                         data=rng.integers(0, 3, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int8))
141	        f.create_dataset("strategy_label",
142	                         data=rng.integers(0, 20, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int32))
143	        f.create_dataset("game_phase",
144	                         data=rng.integers(0, 3, size=(num_steps,)).astype(np.int8))
145	        f.attrs["num_steps"] = num_steps
146	
147	    return path
148	```
149

311	- [ ] **Step 1.5: Create `src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py`**
312	
313	```python
314	# src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py
315	"""PyTorch Dataset wrapper for Plan 2 replay HDF5 files.
316	
317	Supports multiple HDF5 files (concatenated view) and train/val splits
318	by file index so no temporal leakage occurs across the boundary.
319	
320	Each __getitem__ returns a dict of torch.Tensor objects:
321	    spatial:            (C, H, W)   float32
322	    entity_features:    (N, D)      float32
323	    entity_mask:        (N,)        bool
324	    global_scalars:     (S,)        float32
325	    strategy_override:  (B_s,)      float32  — zeros unless stored in HDF5
326	    action_type:        ()          int64
327	    target_entity_idx:  ()          int64    — -1 if no entity target
328	    outcome:            ()          float32  — 1.0=win, 0.0=loss
329	"""
330	
331	from __future__ import annotations
332	
333	import math
334	from pathlib import Path
335	from typing import Sequence
336	
337	import h5py
338	import numpy as np
339	import torch
340	from torch.utils.data import Dataset
341	
342	from zh_copilot.config import STATE
343	
344	
345	class ILDataset(Dataset):
346	    """Concatenated view over one or more replay HDF5 files.
347	
348	    Args:
349	        file_paths: List of paths to HDF5 files produced by ReplayDatasetWriter.
350	            Files must each contain the standard datasets:
351	            spatial, entity_features, entity_mask, global_scalars,
352	            action_type, outcome, target_entity_idx.
353	    """
354	
355	    def __init__(self, file_paths: Sequence[str | Path]) -> None:
356	        self.file_paths: list[Path] = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]
357	        if not self.file_paths:
358	            raise ValueError("file_paths must not be empty")
359	
360	        # Lazily-opened handles — open on first access per worker
361	        self._handles: list[h5py.File | None] = [None] * len(self.file_paths)
362	
363	        # Compute cumulative step counts for O(log N) index lookup
364	        self._lengths: list[int] = []
365	        for fp in self.file_paths:
366	            with h5py.File(fp, "r") as f:
367	                n = int(f.attrs.get("num_steps", len(f["action_type"])))
368	            self._lengths.append(n)
369	
370	        self._cumulative: list[int] = []
371	        total = 0
372	        for n in self._lengths:
373	            total += n
374	            self._cumulative.append(total)
375	        self._total = total
376	
377	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
378	    # Dataset protocol
379	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
380	
381	    def __len__(self) -> int:
382	        return self._total
383	
384	    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
385	        if idx < 0 or idx >= self._total:
386	            raise IndexError(f"Index {idx} out of range for dataset of length {self._total}")
387	
388	        file_idx, local_idx = self._resolve_index(idx)
389	        f = self._get_handle(file_idx)
390	
391	        spatial = torch.from_numpy(f["spatial"][local_idx].astype(np.float32))
392	        entity_features = torch.from_numpy(f["entity_features"][local_idx].astype(np.float32))
393	        entity_mask = torch.from_numpy(f["entity_mask"][local_idx].astype(bool))
394	        global_scalars = torch.from_numpy(f["global_scalars"][local_idx].astype(np.float32))
395	
396	        action_type = torch.tensor(int(f["action_type"][local_idx]), dtype=torch.int64)
397	        outcome = torch.tensor(float(f["outcome"][local_idx]), dtype=torch.float32)
398	
399	        if "target_entity_idx" in f:
400	            tei = int(f["target_entity_idx"][local_idx])
401	        else:
402	            tei = -1
403	        target_entity_idx = torch.tensor(tei, dtype=torch.int64)
404	
405	        if "strategy_override" in f:
406	            strategy_override = torch.from_numpy(
407	                f["strategy_override"][local_idx].astype(np.float32)
408	            )
409	        else:
410	            strategy_override = torch.zeros(STATE.num_strategy_buttons, dtype=torch.float32)
411	
412	        return {
413	            "spatial": spatial,
414	            "entity_features": entity_features,
415	            "entity_mask": entity_mask,
416	            "global_scalars": global_scalars,
417	            "strategy_override": strategy_override,
418	            "action_type": action_type,
419	            "target_entity_idx": target_entity_idx,
420	            "outcome": outcome,
421	        }
422	
423	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
424	    # Train/val split
425	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
426	
427	    @classmethod
428	    def split(
429	        cls,
430	        file_paths: Sequence[str | Path],
431	        val_fraction: float = 0.1,
432	        shuffle: bool = False,
433	        seed: int = 42,
434	    ) -> tuple["ILDataset", "ILDataset"]:
435	        """Split files into train and val subsets by file index.
436	
437	        Val files are taken from the end of the list (or randomly if shuffle=True).
438	        No temporal leakage: all steps from a file go to the same split.
439	
440	        Args:
441	            file_paths: All available HDF5 file paths.
442	            val_fraction: Fraction of *files* (not steps) to use for validation.
443	            shuffle: If True, shuffle file order before splitting.
444	            seed: Random seed used when shuffle=True.
445	
446	        Returns:
447	            (train_dataset, val_dataset)
448	        """
449	        paths = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]
450	        if shuffle:
451	            rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
452	            idx = rng.permutation(len(paths)).tolist()
453	            paths = [paths[i] for i in idx]
454	
455	        n_val = max(1, math.ceil(len(paths) * val_fraction))
456	        n_train = len(paths) - n_val
457	        if n_train < 1:
458	            raise ValueError(
459	                f"Not enough files for a train split: {len(paths)} files, "
460	                f"val_fraction={val_fraction}"
461	            )
462	        return cls(paths[:n_train]), cls(paths[n_train:])
463	
464	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
465	    # Internal helpers
466	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
467	
468	    def _resolve_index(self, idx: int) -> tuple[int, int]:
469	        """Return (file_index, local_index) for a global step index."""
470	        for file_idx, cum in enumerate(self._cumulative):
471	            if idx < cum:
472	                local_idx = idx - (self._cumulative[file_idx - 1] if file_idx > 0 else 0)
473	                return file_idx, local_idx
474	        raise IndexError(f"Index {idx} out of range")  # should be unreachable
475	
476	    def _get_handle(self, file_idx: int) -> h5py.File:
477	        """Return open h5py.File, opening lazily on first call."""
478	        if self._handles[file_idx] is None:
479	            self._handles[file_idx] = h5py.File(self.file_paths[file_idx], "r")
480	        return self._handles[file_idx]  # type: ignore[return-value]
481	
482	    def __del__(self) -> None:
483	        for h in self._handles:
484	            if h is not None:
485	                try:
486	                    h.close()
487	                except Exception:
488	                    pass
489	```

517	### Task 2: Stratified Batch Sampler (`strategy_sampler.py`)
518	
519	**Files:**
520	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_sampler.py`
521	- Create: `tests/training/test_strategy_sampler.py`
522	
523	**Purpose:** Prevent batch composition bias during IL training. Without stratification, a batch could contain mostly GLA replays or only rush-strategy steps, causing biased gradient updates and overfitting to dominant distributions.
524	
525	**Design:** `StratifiedBatchSampler` is a PyTorch `Sampler` that groups dataset indices by `(faction, strategy_label, game_phase)` strata and yields batches with capped representation per stratum.
526	
527	#### Implementation spec
528	
529	```python
530	# src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_sampler.py
531	class StratifiedBatchSampler(torch.utils.data.Sampler):
532	    """Yield balanced batches across (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase) strata.
533	
534	    Args:
535	        labels: Sequence of (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase) tuples,
536	                one per dataset index. faction_id: 0=USA,1=China,2=GLA.
537	                strategy_label: int 0..K-1 from K-means clustering.
538	                game_phase: 0=early,1=mid,2=late.
539	        batch_size: Number of samples per batch.
540	        max_same_stratum: Maximum fraction of a batch from any single stratum.
541	                          Default 0.4 (40%). Prevents one stratum dominating.
542	        drop_last: If True, drop the last incomplete batch. Default True.
543	        shuffle: Shuffle within each stratum before sampling. Default True.
544	        seed: Random seed for reproducibility.
545	
546	    Usage:
547	        labels = [(item['faction_id'], item['strategy_label'], item['game_phase'])
548	                  for item in dataset]
549	        sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=256, max_same_stratum=0.4)
550	        loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_sampler=sampler)
551	    """
552	    def __init__(self, labels, batch_size, max_same_stratum=0.4,
553	                 drop_last=True, shuffle=True, seed=0): ...
554	
555	    def __iter__(self) -> Iterator[List[int]]:
556	        """Yield batches. Each batch draws round-robin from strata,
557	        capped at max_same_stratum * batch_size per stratum."""
558	        ...
559	
560	    def __len__(self) -> int:
561	        """Total number of batches."""
562	        ...
563	```
564

585	
586	def test_dataloader_integration():

```
600	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/losses.py`
601	- Create: `tests/training/test_losses.py`
602	
603	Three component losses plus a weighted combiner:
604	
605	| Loss | Formula | Notes |
606	|------|---------|-------|
607	| `action_type_loss` | cross-entropy(`action_logits`, `action_type`) | standard classification |
608	| `pointer_loss` | cross-entropy(`ptr_logits`, `target_entity_idx`) | skip steps where `target_entity_idx == -1` |
609	| `value_loss` | MSE(`value.squeeze(-1)`, `outcome`) | outcome is 0.0 or 1.0 |
610	| `il_loss` | `w_act * action_type_loss + w_ptr * pointer_loss + w_val * value_loss` | weights from config defaults |
611	
612	Default weights: `w_act=1.0, w_ptr=0.5, w_val=0.1`
613

618	```python
619	# tests/training/test_losses.py
620	"""Tests for IL loss functions."""
621	
622	import torch
623	import pytest
624	
625	from zh_copilot.training.losses import (
626	    action_type_loss,
627	    pointer_loss,
628	    value_loss,
629	    il_loss,
630	)
631	from zh_copilot.config import MODEL, STATE
632	
633	
634	# --- helpers ---
635	
636	def make_logits(B: int = 4):
637	    return torch.randn(B, MODEL.num_action_types)
638	
639	
640	def make_ptr_logits(B: int = 4):
641	    return torch.randn(B, STATE.max_entities)
642	
643	
644	def make_value(B: int = 4):
645	    return torch.sigmoid(torch.randn(B, 1))
646	
647	
648	def make_targets(B: int = 4):
649	    return torch.randint(0, MODEL.num_action_types, (B,))
650	
651	
652	# --- action_type_loss ---
653	
654	def test_action_type_loss_scalar():
655	    loss = action_type_loss(make_logits(), make_targets())
656	    assert loss.shape == ()
657	    assert loss.item() > 0
658	
659	
660	def test_action_type_loss_perfect_prediction():
661	    """CE loss with perfect logits should be near zero."""
662	    B = 4
663	    targets = torch.zeros(B, dtype=torch.long)
664	    logits = torch.full((B, MODEL.num_action_types), -1e6)
665	    logits[:, 0] = 1e6
666	    loss = action_type_loss(logits, targets)
667	    assert loss.item() < 1e-3
668	
669	
670	def test_action_type_loss_gradient():
671	    logits = make_logits().requires_grad_(True)
672	    loss = action_type_loss(logits, make_targets())
673	    loss.backward()
674	    assert logits.grad is not None
675	
676	
677	# --- pointer_loss ---
678	
679	def test_pointer_loss_all_valid():

681	    B = 4

684	    loss = pointer_loss(ptr, targets)

686	    assert loss.item() > 0
687	
688	
689	def test_pointer_loss_all_negative_one():
690	    """When all targets are -1 (no entity), loss must be zero (or nan-safe zero)."""
691	    B = 4

693	    targets = torch.full((B,), -1, dtype=torch.long)
694	    loss = pointer_loss(ptr, targets)

696	
697

703	    loss = pointer_loss(ptr, targets)

711	    loss = pointer_loss(ptr, targets)

713	    assert ptr.grad is not None

744

749	    loss.backward()

753	# --- il_loss ---
754	
755	def test_il_loss_is_weighted_sum():
756	    B = 4
757	    act_logits = make_logits(B)
758	    ptr_logits_ = make_ptr_logits(B)
759	    val = make_value(B)
760	    act_targets = make_targets(B)

762	    outcome = torch.randint(0, 2, (B,)).float()
763	
764	    total, components = il_loss(
765	        action_logits=act_logits,
766	        ptr_logits=ptr_logits_,
767	        value=val,
768	        action_targets=act_targets,
769	        ptr_targets=ptr_targets,

773	        w_val=0.1,
774	    )
775	    expected = (
776	        1.0 * components["action_type_loss"]
777	        + 0.5 * components["pointer_loss"]

827	#### Step 3: Implement `losses.py`
828	
829	- [ ] **Step 3.3: Create `src/zh_copilot/training/losses.py`**
830	
831	```python
832	# src/zh_copilot/training/losses.py
833	"""Imitation learning loss functions.
834	
835	Three component losses:
836	    action_type_loss  — cross-entropy over 21 action types
837	    pointer_loss      — cross-entropy over max_entities, skipping target_idx == -1
838	    value_loss        — MSE between predicted win probability and game outcome
839	
840	Combined loss:
841	    il_loss(...)  →  (total_loss, {"action_type_loss": ..., "pointer_loss": ..., "value_loss": ...})
842	"""
843	
844	from __future__ import annotations
845	
846	import torch
847	import torch.nn.functional as F
848	
849	
850	def action_type_loss(
851	    action_logits: torch.Tensor,
852	    action_targets: torch.Tensor,
853	) -> torch.Tensor:
854	    """Cross-entropy loss over action type predictions.
855	
856	    Args:
857	        action_logits: (B, num_action_types) raw logits.
858	        action_targets: (B,) int64 ground-truth action type indices.
859	
860	    Returns:
861	        Scalar loss tensor.
862	    """
863	    return F.cross_entropy(action_logits, action_targets)
864	
865	
866	def pointer_loss(
867	    ptr_logits: torch.Tensor,
868	    ptr_targets: torch.Tensor,
869	) -> torch.Tensor:
870	    """Cross-entropy loss over entity pointer predictions.
871	
872	    Steps where ptr_targets == -1 (no entity target) are skipped.
873	    If all targets in a batch are -1, returns a zero-gradient scalar.
874	
875	    Args:
876	        ptr_logits:  (B, max_entities) raw logits.
877	        ptr_targets: (B,) int64 entity indices; -1 means skip.
878	
879	    Returns:
880	        Scalar loss tensor (zero if no valid targets).
881	    """
882	    mask = ptr_targets >= 0  # (B,)
883	    if mask.sum() == 0:
884	        return ptr_logits.sum() * 0.0  # preserves device/dtype, zero gradient
885	
886	    return F.cross_entropy(
887	        ptr_logits[mask],          # (K, max_entities)
888	        ptr_targets[mask],         # (K,)
889	    )
890	
891	
892	def value_loss(
893	    value_preds: torch.Tensor,
894	    outcomes: torch.Tensor,
895	) -> torch.Tensor:
896	    """Mean squared error between predicted win probability and game outcome.
897	
898	    Args:
899	        value_preds: (B, 1) predicted win probabilities in [0, 1].
900	        outcomes:    (B,)  float32 targets; 1.0 = win, 0.0 = loss.
901	
902	    Returns:
903	        Scalar MSE loss tensor.
904	    """
905	    return F.mse_loss(value_preds.squeeze(-1), outcomes)
906	
907	
908	def il_loss(
909	    action_logits: torch.Tensor,
910	    ptr_logits: torch.Tensor,
911	    value: torch.Tensor,
912	    action_targets: torch.Tensor,
913	    ptr_targets: torch.Tensor,
914	    outcomes: torch.Tensor,
915	    w_act: float = 1.0,
916	    w_ptr: float = 0.5,
917	    w_val: float = 0.1,
918	) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, dict[str, torch.Tensor]]:
919	    """Weighted combination of all three IL losses.
920	
921	    Args:
922	        action_logits:  (B, num_action_types)
923	        ptr_logits:     (B, max_entities)
924	        value:          (B, 1)
925	        action_targets: (B,) int64
926	        ptr_targets:    (B,) int64; -1 = no target
927	        outcomes:       (B,) float32
928	        w_act:          weight for action_type_loss (default 1.0)
929	        w_ptr:          weight for pointer_loss (default 0.5)
930	        w_val:          weight for value_loss (default 0.1)
931	
932	    Returns:
933	        (total_loss, components) where components is a dict with keys:
934	        "action_type_loss", "pointer_loss", "value_loss".
935	    """
936	    l_act = action_type_loss(action_logits, action_targets)
937	    l_ptr = pointer_loss(ptr_logits, ptr_targets)
938	    l_val = value_loss(value, outcomes)
939	
940	    total = w_act * l_act + w_ptr * l_ptr + w_val * l_val
941	
942	    return total, {
943	        "action_type_loss": l_act,
944	        "pointer_loss":     l_ptr,
945	        "value_loss":       l_val,
946	    }
947	```

970	### Task 4: Training Loop (`il_trainer.py`)
971	
972	**Files:**
973	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/il_trainer.py`
974	- Create: `tests/training/test_il_trainer.py`
975	
976	`ILTrainer` manages the full training loop: device selection, optimizer, scheduler, TensorBoard
977	logging, checkpoint saving, and correct LSTM hidden-state handling.
978	
979	**LSTM hidden state protocol:**
980	- Each HDF5 file represents one replay episode.
981	- Hidden state is **reset to None** at the start of every new file's first step.
982	- Within a mini-batch, steps come from different episodes (shuffled), so hidden state is
983	  not carried across batches during standard training.
984	- For sequential per-episode evaluation, `val_epoch()` iterates file by file, resetting
985	  hidden state between files.
986

1144	#### Step 3: Implement `il_trainer.py`
1145	
1146	- [ ] **Step 4.3: Create `src/zh_copilot/training/il_trainer.py`**
1147	
1148	```python
1149	# src/zh_copilot/training/il_trainer.py
1150	"""Imitation learning training loop.
1151	
1152	ILTrainer manages the full behaviour-cloning pipeline:
1153	  - device selection: MPS if available, else CPU
1154	  - AdamW optimizer + cosine LR schedule
1155	  - train_epoch / val_epoch with loss + accuracy metrics
1156	  - TensorBoard logging of all scalars
1157	  - checkpoint saving every N epochs
1158	
1159	LSTM hidden state handling:
1160	    During training: hidden state is NOT carried across DataLoader batches
1161	    (steps are shuffled across episodes). It is reset to None every forward pass.
1162	    This is standard practice for IL where episodes are shuffled.
1163	
1164	    For sequential evaluation: val_epoch iterates the val DataLoader without
1165	    shuffle; hidden state is still reset per batch for simplicity (the LSTM
1166	    learns temporal coherence from episode-level ordering in RL fine-tuning,
1167	    not IL where episodes are batched together).
1168	"""
1169	
1170	from __future__ import annotations
1171	
1172	import time
1173	from pathlib import Path
1174	from typing import Any
1175	
1176	import torch
1177	import torch.nn as nn
1178	from torch.optim import AdamW
1179	from torch.optim.lr_scheduler import CosineAnnealingLR
1180	from torch.utils.data import DataLoader
1181	from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter  # type: ignore[import]
1182	
1183	from zh_copilot.config import MODEL
1184	from zh_copilot.training.losses import il_loss
1185	from zh_copilot.training.strategy_sampler import StratifiedBatchSampler
1186	
1187	# Map action type index → human-readable category (matches message_types.ACTION_CATEGORIES)
1188	_ACTION_CATEGORIES = {
1189	    0: "move",
1190	    1: "attack",
1191	    2: "build",
1192	    3: "produce",
1193	    4: "research",
1194	    5: "sell",
1195	    6: "rally",
1196	    7: "general_power",
1197	    8: "ability",
1198	    9: "guard",
1199	    10: "stop",
1200	    11: "garrison",
1201	    12: "select",
1202	    13: "repair",
1203	    14: "enter",
1204	    15: "capture",
1205	    16: "dock",
1206	    17: "evacuate",
1207	    18: "special",
1208	    19: "other",
1209	    20: "noop",
1210	}
1211	
1212	
1213	class ILTrainer:
1214	    """Behaviour-cloning trainer for ZHCopilotModel.
1215	
1216	    Args:
1217	        model:          ZHCopilotModel instance.
1218	        train_dataset:  ILDataset for training.
1219	        val_dataset:    ILDataset for validation.
1220	        output_dir:     Root directory for checkpoints and TensorBoard logs.
1221	        batch_size:     Mini-batch size (default: TRAINING.il_batch_size=256).
1222	        learning_rate:  Initial LR (default: TRAINING.il_learning_rate=1e-4).
1223	        device:         "mps", "cpu", or "cuda". If None, auto-select.
1224	        num_workers:    DataLoader workers (default 0 for HDF5 safety).
1225	        w_act:          Weight for action_type_loss (default 1.0).
1226	        w_ptr:          Weight for pointer_loss (default 0.5).
1227	        w_val:          Weight for value_loss (default 0.1).
1228	    """
1229	
1230	    def __init__(
1231	        self,
1232	        model: nn.Module,
1233	        train_dataset: Any,
1234	        val_dataset: Any,
1235	        output_dir: str | Path,
1236	        batch_size: int = 256,
1237	        learning_rate: float = 1e-4,
1238	        device: str | None = None,
1239	        num_workers: int = 0,
1240	        w_act: float = 1.0,
1241	        w_ptr: float = 0.5,
1242	        w_val: float = 0.1,
1243	    ) -> None:
1244	        self.output_dir = Path(output_dir)
1245	        self.output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
1246	
1247	        # Device selection
1248	        if device is None:
1249	            if torch.backends.mps.is_available():
1250	                device = "mps"
1251	            else:
1252	                device = "cpu"
1253	        self.device = torch.device(device)
1254	
1255	        self.model = model.to(self.device)
1256	        self.w_act = w_act
1257	        self.w_ptr = w_ptr
1258	        self.w_val = w_val
1259	
1260	        self.optimizer = AdamW(self.model.parameters(), lr=learning_rate)
1261	        # Scheduler is set up in train() when total epochs are known
1262	        self._scheduler: CosineAnnealingLR | None = None
1263	
1264	        # Build stratum labels for stratified sampling
1265	        labels = [(item['faction_id'], item['strategy_label'], item['game_phase'])
1266	                  for item in train_dataset]
1267	        sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=batch_size, max_same_stratum=0.4)
1268	        self.train_loader = DataLoader(
1269	            train_dataset,
1270	            batch_sampler=sampler,
1271	            num_workers=num_workers,
1272	            pin_memory=(self.device.type == "cuda"),
1273	        )
1274	        self.val_loader = DataLoader(
1275	            val_dataset,
1276	            batch_size=batch_size,
1277	            shuffle=False,
1278	            num_workers=num_workers,
1279	            drop_last=False,
1280	            pin_memory=(self.device.type == "cuda"),
1281	        )
1282	
1283	        log_dir = self.output_dir / "logs"
1284	        log_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
1285	        self.writer = SummaryWriter(log_dir=str(log_dir))
1286	
1287	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
1288	    # Public API
1289	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
1290	
1291	    def train(self, num_epochs: int, checkpoint_every: int = 5) -> None:
1292	        """Run full training loop.
1293	
1294	        Args:
1295	            num_epochs:        Total number of epochs to train.
1296	            checkpoint_every:  Save a checkpoint every N epochs (and always at the end).
1297	        """
1298	        self._scheduler = CosineAnnealingLR(self.optimizer, T_max=num_epochs)
1299	
1300	        for epoch in range(num_epochs):
1301	            t0 = time.time()
1302	            train_metrics = self.train_epoch(epoch)
1303	            val_metrics = self.val_epoch(epoch)
1304	            elapsed = time.time() - t0
1305	
1306	            # TensorBoard
1307	            self._log_metrics("train", train_metrics, epoch)
1308	            self._log_metrics("val", val_metrics, epoch)
1309	            self.writer.add_scalar("train/lr",
1310	                                   self.optimizer.param_groups[0]["lr"], epoch)
1311	
1312	            print(
1313	                f"[Epoch {epoch+1:03d}/{num_epochs}] "
1314	                f"train_loss={train_metrics['loss']:.4f}  "
1315	                f"val_loss={val_metrics['loss']:.4f}  "
1316	                f"train_acc={train_metrics['action_acc']:.3f}  "
1317	                f"val_acc={val_metrics['action_acc']:.3f}  "
1318	                f"({elapsed:.1f}s)"
1319	            )
1320	
1321	            if self._scheduler is not None:
1322	                self._scheduler.step()
1323	
1324	            if (epoch + 1) % checkpoint_every == 0 or epoch == num_epochs - 1:
1325	                self.save_checkpoint(epoch, metrics={**train_metrics, **{
1326	                    f"val_{k}": v for k, v in val_metrics.items()
1327	                }})
1328	
1329	        self.writer.close()
1330	
1331	    def train_epoch(self, epoch: int) -> dict[str, float]:
1332	        """One training epoch. Returns dict of scalar metrics."""
1333	        self.model.train()
1334	        total_loss = 0.0
1335	        total_act = 0
1336	        correct_act = 0
1337	        n_batches = 0
1338	
1339	        for batch in self.train_loader:
1340	            batch = {k: v.to(self.device) for k, v in batch.items()}
1341	
1342	            self.optimizer.zero_grad()
1343	
1344	            # Forward — hidden state reset to None for each batch
1345	            out = self.model(batch, hidden=None)
1346	
1347	            loss, components = il_loss(
1348	                action_logits=out["action_logits"],
1349	                ptr_logits=out["ptr_logits"],
