1353	                outcomes=batch["outcome"],
1354	                w_act=self.w_act,
1355	                w_ptr=self.w_ptr,
1356	                w_val=self.w_val,
1357	            )
1358	
1359	            loss.backward()
1360	            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(self.model.parameters(), max_norm=1.0)
1361	            self.optimizer.step()
1362	
1363	            total_loss += loss.item()
1364	            preds = out["action_logits"].argmax(dim=-1)
1365	            correct_act += (preds == batch["action_type"]).sum().item()
1366	            total_act += batch["action_type"].shape[0]
1367	            n_batches += 1
1368	
1369	        return {
1370	            "loss": total_loss / max(n_batches, 1),
1371	            "action_acc": correct_act / max(total_act, 1),
1372	        }
1373	
1374	    def val_epoch(self, epoch: int) -> dict[str, float]:
1375	        """One validation epoch. Returns dict of scalar metrics."""
1376	        self.model.eval()
1377	        total_loss = 0.0
1378	        total_act = 0
1379	        correct_act = 0
1380	        n_batches = 0
1381	
1382	        # Per-category accuracy tracking
1383	        cat_correct: dict[int, int] = {}
1384	        cat_total: dict[int, int] = {}
1385	
1386	        with torch.no_grad():
1387	            for batch in self.val_loader:
1388	                batch = {k: v.to(self.device) for k, v in batch.items()}
1389	
1390	                out = self.model(batch, hidden=None)
1391	
1392	                loss, _ = il_loss(
1393	                    action_logits=out["action_logits"],
1394	                    ptr_logits=out["ptr_logits"],
1395	                    value=out["value"],
1396	                    action_targets=batch["action_type"],
1397	                    ptr_targets=batch["target_entity_idx"],
1398	                    outcomes=batch["outcome"],
1399	                    w_act=self.w_act,
1400	                    w_ptr=self.w_ptr,
1401	                    w_val=self.w_val,
1402	                )
1403	
1404	                total_loss += loss.item()
1405	                preds = out["action_logits"].argmax(dim=-1)
1406	                targets = batch["action_type"]
1407	                correct_act += (preds == targets).sum().item()
1408	                total_act += targets.shape[0]
1409	                n_batches += 1
1410	
1411	                # Per-category accuracy
1412	                for at in targets.unique().tolist():
1413	                    mask = targets == at
1414	                    cat_correct[at] = cat_correct.get(at, 0) + int((preds[mask] == at).sum())
1415	                    cat_total[at] = cat_total.get(at, 0) + int(mask.sum())
1416	
1417	        metrics: dict[str, float] = {
1418	            "loss": total_loss / max(n_batches, 1),
1419	            "action_acc": correct_act / max(total_act, 1),
1420	        }
1421	        for at, total in cat_total.items():
1422	            name = _ACTION_CATEGORIES.get(at, f"action_{at}")
1423	            metrics[f"acc_{name}"] = cat_correct.get(at, 0) / max(total, 1)
1424	
1425	        return metrics
1426	
1427	    def save_checkpoint(self, epoch: int, metrics: dict | None = None) -> Path:
1428	        """Save model + optimizer state to output_dir/checkpoint_epoch_NNN.pt."""
1429	        path = self.output_dir / f"checkpoint_epoch_{epoch:03d}.pt"
1430	        torch.save(
1431	            {
1432	                "epoch": epoch,
1433	                "model_state_dict": self.model.state_dict(),
1434	                "optimizer_state_dict": self.optimizer.state_dict(),
1435	                "metrics": metrics or {},
1436	            },
1437	            path,
1438	        )
1439	        return path
1440	
1441	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
1442	    # Internal helpers
1443	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
1444	
1445	    def _log_metrics(self, split: str, metrics: dict[str, float], epoch: int) -> None:
1446	        for k, v in metrics.items():
1447	            self.writer.add_scalar(f"{split}/{k}", v, epoch)
1448	```

1474	### Task 5: Strategy Clustering (`strategy_cluster.py`)
1475	
1476	**Files:**
1477	- Create: `src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_cluster.py`
1478	- Create: `tests/training/test_strategy_cluster.py`
1479	
1480	Cluster replay openings into K=20 archetypes using K-means on command-frequency histograms
1481	from the first 5 minutes (9000 frames at 30fps). The cluster label per replay is stored as an
1482	integer in `[0, K-1]` and written back into each HDF5 file as a scalar attribute and as a
1483	per-step `strategy_label` dataset.
1484	
1485	**Feature vector per replay:** A histogram of `action_type` values (21 bins) over the first
1486	`max_frames` steps. The vector is L2-normalised before clustering.
1487

1638	
1639	```python
1640	# src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_cluster.py
1641	"""Strategy archetype clustering for replay openings.
1642	
1643	Assigns each replay to one of K=20 opening archetypes via K-means clustering
1644	on the first 5 minutes (max_frames=9000 @ 30fps) of command type frequencies.
1645	
1646	Pipeline:
1647	    1. extract_opening_features(path) → (21,) L2-normalised histogram
1648	    2. fit_strategy_clusters(paths, n_clusters=20) → list[int] labels
1649	    3. label_replay_files(paths, labels) → writes attrs + per-step dataset to HDF5
1650	    4. StrategyClusterer — convenience class wrapping steps 1-3 with save/load
1651	"""
1652	
1653	from __future__ import annotations
1654	
1655	import pickle
1656	from pathlib import Path
1657	from typing import Sequence
1658	
1659	import h5py
1660	import numpy as np
1661	from sklearn.cluster import KMeans  # type: ignore[import]
1662	
1663	from zh_copilot.config import MODEL, ENGINE
1664	
1665	
1666	# Frames per second × seconds per minute × minutes
1667	_DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES_OPENING = ENGINE.logic_fps * 60 * 5  # 9000
1668	
1669	
1670	def extract_opening_features(
1671	    path: str | Path,
1672	    num_action_types: int = MODEL.num_action_types,
1673	    max_frames: int = _DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES_OPENING,
1674	) -> np.ndarray:
1675	    """Build a normalised command-frequency histogram from the replay opening.
1676	
1677	    Reads the HDF5 `frame` and `action_type` datasets. Only steps with
1678	    frame < max_frames are counted. Returns a zero vector for empty replays
1679	    or when max_frames=0.
1680	
1681	    Args:
1682	        path:             Path to an HDF5 file produced by ReplayDatasetWriter.
1683	        num_action_types: Number of action type bins (histogram length).
1684	        max_frames:       Only count commands before this frame number.
1685	
1686	    Returns:
1687	        (num_action_types,) float32 L2-normalised frequency histogram.
1688	    """
1689	    with h5py.File(path, "r") as f:
1690	        n_steps = int(f.attrs.get("num_steps", len(f["action_type"])))
1691	        if n_steps == 0 or max_frames <= 0:
1692	            return np.zeros(num_action_types, dtype=np.float32)
1693	
1694	        frames = f["frame"][:n_steps]
1695	        actions = f["action_type"][:n_steps]
1696	
1697	    mask = frames < max_frames
1698	    if mask.sum() == 0:
1699	        return np.zeros(num_action_types, dtype=np.float32)
1700	
1701	    hist = np.bincount(actions[mask].astype(np.int64), minlength=num_action_types)
1702	    hist = hist[:num_action_types].astype(np.float32)
1703	
1704	    norm = np.linalg.norm(hist)
1705	    if norm > 0:
1706	        hist /= norm
1707	
1708	    return hist
1709	
1710	
1711	def fit_strategy_clusters(
1712	    file_paths: Sequence[str | Path],
1713	    n_clusters: int = MODEL.num_strategy_archetypes,
1714	    max_frames: int = _DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES_OPENING,
1715	    seed: int = 42,
1716	) -> list[int]:
1717	    """Fit K-means on opening features and return per-replay cluster labels.
1718	
1719	    If the number of files is smaller than n_clusters, n_clusters is capped
1720	    to avoid K-means failures.
1721	
1722	    Args:
1723	        file_paths:  HDF5 file paths.
1724	        n_clusters:  K for K-means (default MODEL.num_strategy_archetypes=20).
1725	        max_frames:  Opening window in frames (default 9000 = 5 min).
1726	        seed:        Random seed for K-means.
1727	
1728	    Returns:
1729	        List of integer cluster labels, one per file, in [0, effective_k).
1730	    """
1731	    features = np.stack(
1732	        [extract_opening_features(p, max_frames=max_frames) for p in file_paths],
1733	        axis=0,
1734	    )  # (N, num_action_types)
1735	
1736	    effective_k = min(n_clusters, len(file_paths))
1737	    km = KMeans(n_clusters=effective_k, random_state=seed, n_init="auto")
1738	    labels = km.fit_predict(features)
1739	    return [int(lbl) for lbl in labels]
1740	
1741	
1742	def label_replay_files(
1743	    file_paths: Sequence[str | Path],
1744	    labels: Sequence[int],
1745	) -> None:
1746	    """Write strategy cluster labels back into each HDF5 file.
1747	
1748	    Writes two things per file:
1749	      - `f.attrs["strategy_label"]` (int scalar) — the cluster index

```

```
1750	      - `f["strategy_label_steps"]` (int32 array of length num_steps) — same
1751	        value broadcast to every step, for easy per-step lookup during training.
1752	
1753	    Args:
1754	        file_paths: HDF5 file paths.
1755	        labels:     Integer cluster labels (one per file).
1756	    """
1757	    for path, label in zip(file_paths, labels):
1758	        with h5py.File(path, "a") as f:
1759	            n_steps = int(f.attrs.get("num_steps", len(f["action_type"])))
1760	            f.attrs["strategy_label"] = int(label)
1761	            ds_name = "strategy_label_steps"
1762	            if ds_name in f:
1763	                del f[ds_name]
1764	            f.create_dataset(
1765	                ds_name,
1766	                data=np.full(n_steps, int(label), dtype=np.int32),
1767	            )
1768	
1769	
1770	class StrategyClusterer:
1771	    """Convenience wrapper for the full strategy clustering pipeline.
1772	
1773	    Usage::
1774	
1775	        clusterer = StrategyClusterer(n_clusters=20)
1776	        clusterer.fit(train_files)
1777	        labels = clusterer.predict(all_files)
1778	        label_replay_files(all_files, labels)
1779	        clusterer.save("strategy_clusterer.pkl")
1780	
1781	        # Later:
1782	        clusterer = StrategyClusterer.load("strategy_clusterer.pkl")
1783	    """
1784	
1785	    def __init__(
1786	        self,
1787	        n_clusters: int = MODEL.num_strategy_archetypes,
1788	        max_frames: int = _DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES_OPENING,
1789	        seed: int = 42,
1790	    ) -> None:
1791	        self.n_clusters = n_clusters
1792	        self.max_frames = max_frames
1793	        self.seed = seed
1794	        self._km: KMeans | None = None
1795	
1796	    def fit(self, file_paths: Sequence[str | Path]) -> "StrategyClusterer":
1797	        """Fit K-means on the given replay files.
1798	
1799	        Returns self for chaining.
1800	        """
1801	        features = np.stack(
1802	            [extract_opening_features(p, max_frames=self.max_frames) for p in file_paths],
1803	            axis=0,
1804	        )
1805	        effective_k = min(self.n_clusters, len(file_paths))
1806	        self._km = KMeans(n_clusters=effective_k, random_state=self.seed, n_init="auto")
1807	        self._km.fit(features)
1808	        return self
1809	
1810	    def predict(self, file_paths: Sequence[str | Path]) -> list[int]:
1811	        """Assign cluster labels to a list of replay files.
1812	
1813	        Raises:
1814	            RuntimeError: If clusterer has not been fitted yet.
1815	        """
1816	        if self._km is None:
1817	            raise RuntimeError("StrategyClusterer is not fitted. Call fit() first.")
1818	        features = np.stack(
1819	            [extract_opening_features(p, max_frames=self.max_frames) for p in file_paths],
1820	            axis=0,
1821	        )
1822	        labels = self._km.predict(features)
1823	        return [int(lbl) for lbl in labels]
1824	
1825	    def save(self, path: str | Path) -> None:
1826	        """Serialise the fitted clusterer to disk."""
1827	        with open(path, "wb") as f:
1828	            pickle.dump(self, f)
1829	
1830	    @classmethod
1831	    def load(cls, path: str | Path) -> "StrategyClusterer":
1832	        """Deserialise a previously saved StrategyClusterer."""
1833	        with open(path, "rb") as f:
1834	            return pickle.load(f)  # noqa: S301 — trusted internal file
1835	```

2012	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2013	# scripts/train_il.py
2014	"""CLI entry point for imitation learning training.
2015	
2016	Usage:
2017	    python scripts/train_il.py \\
2018	        --data-dir  /path/to/hdf5_replays/ \\
2019	        --output-dir /path/to/checkpoints/ \\
2020	        --epochs 50 \\
2021	        --batch-size 256 \\
2022	        --device mps
2023	
2024	Options:
2025	    --data-dir          Directory containing .h5 replay files (required)
2026	    --output-dir        Output directory for checkpoints + TensorBoard logs (required)
2027	    --epochs            Number of training epochs (default: 50)
2028	    --batch-size        Mini-batch size (default: 256)
2029	    --learning-rate     Initial learning rate (default: 1e-4)
2030	    --device            "mps", "cpu", or "cuda"; auto-detected if omitted
2031	    --val-fraction      Fraction of HDF5 files to use for validation (default: 0.1)
2032	    --checkpoint-every  Save a checkpoint every N epochs (default: 5)
2033	    --resume            Path to a checkpoint .pt file to resume from
2034	    --cluster-strategies  Run K-means strategy clustering before training
2035	    --n-clusters        Number of strategy archetypes for K-means (default: 20)
2036	    --seed              Random seed (default: 42)
2037	    --w-act             Weight for action_type_loss (default: 1.0)
2038	    --w-ptr             Weight for pointer_loss (default: 0.5)
2039	    --w-val             Weight for value_loss (default: 0.1)
2040	"""
2041	
2042	from __future__ import annotations
2043	
2044	import argparse
2045	import sys
2046	from pathlib import Path
2047	
2048	# Ensure the src package is on sys.path when running from repo root
2049	_REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
2050	sys.path.insert(0, str(_REPO_ROOT / "src"))
2051	
2052	import torch
2053	
2054	from zh_copilot.config import TRAINING
2055	from zh_copilot.model import ZHCopilotModel
2056	from zh_copilot.training.il_dataset import ILDataset
2057	from zh_copilot.training.il_trainer import ILTrainer
2058	from zh_copilot.training.strategy_cluster import (
2059	    StrategyClusterer,
2060	    label_replay_files,
2061	)
2062	
2063	
2064	def parse_args(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> argparse.Namespace:
2065	    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
2066	        description="Train ZHCopilotModel via imitation learning on replay HDF5 files.",
2067	        formatter_class=argparse.ArgumentDefaultsHelpFormatter,
2068	    )
2069	    p.add_argument("--data-dir",   required=True,  type=Path,
2070	                   help="Directory containing .h5 replay files")
2071	    p.add_argument("--output-dir", required=True,  type=Path,
2072	                   help="Output directory for checkpoints and TensorBoard logs")
2073	    p.add_argument("--epochs",      type=int,   default=TRAINING.il_epochs)
2074	    p.add_argument("--batch-size",  type=int,   default=TRAINING.il_batch_size)
2075	    p.add_argument("--learning-rate", type=float, default=TRAINING.il_learning_rate)
2076	    p.add_argument("--device",      type=str,   default=None,
2077	                   help="Device: mps, cpu, or cuda. Auto-detected if omitted.")
2078	    p.add_argument("--val-fraction", type=float, default=0.1)
2079	    p.add_argument("--checkpoint-every", type=int, default=5)
2080	    p.add_argument("--resume",      type=Path,  default=None,
2081	                   help="Path to a checkpoint to resume training from")
2082	    p.add_argument("--cluster-strategies", action="store_true",
2083	                   help="Run K-means strategy clustering before training")
2084	    p.add_argument("--n-clusters",  type=int,   default=20)
2085	    p.add_argument("--seed",        type=int,   default=42)
2086	    p.add_argument("--w-act",       type=float, default=1.0)
2087	    p.add_argument("--w-ptr",       type=float, default=0.5)
2088	    p.add_argument("--w-val",       type=float, default=0.1)
2089	    return p.parse_args(argv)
2090	
2091	
2092	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> None:
2093	    args = parse_args(argv)
2094	
2095	    # --- Discover HDF5 files ---
2096	    data_dir = args.data_dir
2097	    h5_files = sorted(data_dir.glob("*.h5"))
2098	    if not h5_files:
2099	        print(f"ERROR: No .h5 files found in {data_dir}", file=sys.stderr)
2100	        sys.exit(1)
2101	    print(f"Found {len(h5_files)} replay files in {data_dir}")
2102	
2103	    # --- Optional strategy clustering ---
2104	    if args.cluster_strategies:
2105	        print(f"Clustering {len(h5_files)} replays into {args.n_clusters} archetypes...")
2106	        clusterer = StrategyClusterer(n_clusters=args.n_clusters, seed=args.seed)
2107	        clusterer.fit(h5_files)
2108	        labels = clusterer.predict(h5_files)
2109	        label_replay_files(h5_files, labels)
2110	        clusterer_path = args.output_dir / "strategy_clusterer.pkl"
2111	        args.output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
2112	        clusterer.save(clusterer_path)
2113	        print(f"  Saved clusterer → {clusterer_path}")
2114	
2115	    # --- Dataset split ---
2116	    train_ds, val_ds = ILDataset.split(
2117	        h5_files,
2118	        val_fraction=args.val_fraction,
2119	        shuffle=True,
2120	        seed=args.seed,
2121	    )
2122	    print(
2123	        f"Dataset split: {len(train_ds)} train steps "
2124	        f"/ {len(val_ds)} val steps"
2125	    )
2126	
2127	    # --- Model ---
2128	    model = ZHCopilotModel()
2129	    n_params = model.count_parameters()
2130	    print(f"Model parameters: {n_params:,} ({n_params / 1e6:.1f}M)")
2131	
2132	    # --- Resume from checkpoint ---
2133	    start_epoch = 0
2134	    if args.resume is not None:
2135	        print(f"Resuming from {args.resume}")
2136	        state = torch.load(args.resume, map_location="cpu", weights_only=True)
2137	        model.load_state_dict(state["model_state_dict"])
2138	        start_epoch = state.get("epoch", 0) + 1
2139	        print(f"  Resuming from epoch {start_epoch}")
2140	
2141	    # --- Trainer ---
2142	    trainer = ILTrainer(
2143	        model=model,
2144	        train_dataset=train_ds,
2145	        val_dataset=val_ds,
2146	        output_dir=args.output_dir,
2147	        batch_size=args.batch_size,
2148	        learning_rate=args.learning_rate,
2149	        device=args.device,

2286	---
2287	
2288	## Key Design Decisions
2289	
2290	**Why split by file, not by step?**
2291	Splitting at the step level would place early steps of a replay in train and later steps in
2292	val, leaking temporal context via the LSTM. Splitting by file ensures the model sees
2293	completely unseen games during validation.
2294	
2295	**Why reset LSTM hidden state per batch during training?**
2296	Replay steps are shuffled across episodes in the DataLoader. Carrying hidden state across
2297	batches would produce incorrect conditioning (state from one replay fed into a different
2298	replay's steps). The LSTM learns temporal dependencies within the 128-step context window
2299	that fits inside a single batch from one episode. In Phase 5 (RL fine-tuning), hidden state
2300	will be carried across sequential rollout steps correctly.
2301	
2302	**Why pointer loss returns zero (not skipped) when all targets are -1?**
2303	Returning `0.0` via `ptr_logits.sum() * 0.0` keeps the tensor on the correct device and
2304	preserves the computation graph without introducing NaNs, which would occur with an empty
2305	tensor cross-entropy computation.
2306	
2307	**Why L2-normalise the strategy feature vector?**
2308	K-means uses Euclidean distance. Without normalisation, high-APM replays would have
2309	proportionally larger raw counts and would dominate cluster centroids, conflating play
2310	style with player speed.
2311

### One Issue Found

**ISSUE — `__getitem__` does not handle negative indices as IndexError consistently with Python convention.**

The check at line 54 of `il_dataset.py` is:

```python
if idx < 0 or idx >= self._total:
    raise IndexError(...)
```

The spec states "`IndexError` on out-of-bounds access." Raising on all negative indices is a stricter-than-spec choice (Python's `Dataset` convention allows negative indexing in principle), but the spec does not explicitly require negative index support either. This is a borderline case — the spec says "out-of-bounds" without defining whether negative indices should wrap. The implementation raises `IndexError` on negative indices, which is consistent with the spec's stated behavior of raising on out-of-bounds.

However, `test_index_out_of_range` only tests `ds[128]` (positive out-of-bounds). There is no test for a negative index. The spec does not list a negative-index test as a required test case, so this is not a spec violation on the test side.

This is not a spec violation — just noting the behavior is stricter than strictly required. No issue to report.

## Code Quality Review: ILDataset (Task 1)

### Strengths

The implementation is well-structured and matches the plan closely. The docstring on the class correctly describes the multi-file concat behavior. The `split()` factory cleanly separates concerns (file-level split, no step-level leakage). Scalar fields like `action_type` are correctly upcast to `int64` via `torch.tensor(..., dtype=torch.int64)` rather than relying on `torch.from_numpy`, which is the right defensive move for int32 HDF5 data. Optional fields (`strategy_override`, `faction_id`, etc.) have sensible defaults. Test coverage is good for the happy path.

---

### Issues

#### CRITICAL — Shared HDF5 handles will corrupt multi-worker DataLoader reads

File: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py`, lines 144–148

`_get_handle` caches open `h5py.File` objects in `self._handles` on the `ILDataset` instance. When `DataLoader` is created with `num_workers > 0`, PyTorch forks the worker processes **after** pickling the dataset. Each worker receives a copy of `self._handles`, but `h5py.File` objects are **not fork-safe and not pickle-safe**. The result is either a silent deadlock, corrupted reads, or an `OSError: Unable to open file` at runtime depending on the HDF5 threading mode.

The test `test_dataloader_collation` only runs with `num_workers=0` (the DataLoader default), so this bug is not caught by the test suite.

The standard correct pattern for multi-worker HDF5 is to open files **inside `__getitem__`** keyed on the worker's PID (`os.getpid()`), or to implement a `worker_init_fn` that opens handles. The lazy-handle dict must be populated per-worker process, not carried across fork. As written, the `_handles` dict that gets forked will contain live file descriptors pointing into the parent process's file table, which child workers cannot safely use.

#### CRITICAL — `_resolve_index` silently returns `file_idx=0` for the last file

File: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py`, lines 133–142

Trace through the loop for a dataset with two files of 64 steps each, with `idx=127` (the last valid index):

- `i=0`: `self._offsets[1]` is 64, `idx >= 64` is true, so `continue`
- `i=1`: `i + 1 < len(self._offsets)` is `2 < 2` which is **false**, so the inner `if` is skipped entirely and `file_idx = 1`, `break`

This case actually resolves correctly. Now trace `idx=64` (first index of file 2):

- `i=0`: `self._offsets[1]` is 64, `idx >= 64` is true, so `continue`
- `i=1`: correctly sets `file_idx=1`

This case also resolves correctly. However, the logic is unnecessarily fragile. The loop exits correctly only because the last iteration always falls through to `file_idx = i`. If someone inserts an early `return` or refactors the loop, the invariant breaks silently. More importantly: the linear scan is O(N files) per `__getitem__`. For a training run with hundreds of replay files this adds measurable overhead. `bisect.bisect_right(self._offsets, idx) - 1` would be O(log N) and unambiguous.

The deeper actual bug embedded in this logic: when `idx` lands exactly on `self._offsets[0]` (i.e., `idx=0`), the condition `idx >= self._offsets[i + 1]` checks `0 >= self._offsets[1]`. If file 0 has 0 steps (see next issue), `self._offsets[1]` is also 0, so this is `0 >= 0 = True` and the loop continues past file 0 incorrectly.

#### CRITICAL — Zero-step files cause silent index corruption

Files: `il_dataset.py` lines 30–41, `make_fixtures.py`

`__init__` reads `num_steps` from `f.attrs["num_steps"]` and builds `_offsets` and `_total` without validating that `num_steps > 0`. If any file has `num_steps=0`:

- `_offsets` gets a repeated value (e.g., `[0, 0, 64]` for files with 0, 0, 64 steps)
- `_resolve_index` with `idx=0` will match the first file with offset 0, then see `self._offsets[1]` is also 0, and `0 >= 0` causes it to skip to the second file, which also has offset 0 — it will eventually land on whichever file happens to survive the loop
- `local_idx` becomes `0 - 0 = 0`, which may or may not be valid

The fixture helper `make_synthetic_h5` defaults `num_steps=64` so this case is never exercised in tests. There is no guard in `__init__` and no test for `num_steps=0`.

#### IMPORTANT — `faction_id` and `game_phase` stored as int8 but read as int without dtype guard

File: `make_fixtures.py` line 40, 42; `il_dataset.py` lines 85–96

The fixture writes `faction_id` and `game_phase` as `np.int8`. In `__getitem__`, the pattern is `int(f["faction_id"][local_idx])`. `int()` on a numpy int8 scalar works, but `torch.tensor(int8_value, dtype=torch.int64)` also works — so this path is actually safe. However, the inconsistency between the HDF5 dtype (int8) and the rest of the stratification fields (int32/int64) is worth flagging because a future reader slicing the whole column as `f["faction_id"][:]` would get an int8 numpy array and then `torch.from_numpy()` would produce `torch.int8`, which would silently break a caller expecting int64.

#### IMPORTANT — `__del__` is not a reliable resource management mechanism

File: `il_dataset.py` lines 150–156

`__del__` is not guaranteed to be called, and if it is called during interpreter shutdown, h5py may already be partially torn down, making the `try/except Exception` necessary but not sufficient. The dataset has no `close()` method and does not implement the context manager protocol (`__enter__`/`__exit__`). Any code that creates an `ILDataset` outside a `with` block (which is all current usage) leaks file handles until GC runs. For a training loop that creates and discards datasets across epochs or cross-validation folds, this will exhaust the OS file descriptor limit.

The plan does not require a context manager, but the lazy-open design makes the absence of `close()` a real risk in practice.

#### IMPORTANT — DataLoader test does not exercise multi-worker mode

File: `tests/training/test_il_dataset.py`, lines 72–78

`DataLoader(ds, batch_size=4, shuffle=False)` uses `num_workers=0` (default), which runs `__getitem__` in the main process and never exercises the fork path. The critical bug above would pass this test. A test with `num_workers=2` is needed to catch the shared-handle issue.

#### Minor — `test_index_across_file_boundary` does not assert data correctness

File: `test_il_dataset.py` lines 51–56

The test confirms items at idx=63 and idx=64 have the right shape, but does not confirm they come from different files or that their data differs. Since the two fixture files use different seeds, `item_63["spatial"] != item_64["spatial"]` would be a useful additional assertion to prove the file boundary is being crossed correctly rather than the same row being returned twice.

#### Minor — Plan specified `ReplayDatasetWriter` in fixture docstring; implementation writes HDF5 directly

File: `make_fixtures.py`

The plan's fixture docstring says "Creates small HDF5 files using `ReplayDatasetWriter`". The implementation writes HDF5 directly with h5py, bypassing `ReplayDatasetWriter`. This is likely a deliberate pragmatic choice to avoid the engine dependency, and the comment at the top of `make_fixtures.py` was updated accordingly. This is a justified deviation, not a bug.

---

### Summary Table

| # | Severity | Location | Issue |
|---|----------|----------|-------|
| 1 | CRITICAL | `il_dataset.py:144-148` | Cached `h5py.File` handles are forked into DataLoader workers — unsafe with `num_workers > 0` |
| 2 | CRITICAL | `il_dataset.py:133-142` | `_resolve_index` logic is fragile and fails for zero-step files; should use `bisect` |
| 3 | CRITICAL | `il_dataset.py:30-41` | No guard against `num_steps=0`; causes silent index corruption |
| 4 | IMPORTANT | `il_dataset.py:150-156` | No `close()` method; `__del__` is unreliable for file descriptor cleanup |
| 5 | IMPORTANT | `test_il_dataset.py:72-78` | `test_dataloader_collation` uses `num_workers=0`; the critical fork-safety bug is untested |
| 6 | Minor | `test_il_dataset.py:51-56` | File boundary test checks shape only, not that data actually crossed files |

---

### Verdict: NOT APPROVED

The implementation must fix issues 1 and 3 before it is safe to use in training. Issue 1 (the fork-unsafe handle cache) will silently corrupt reads or crash as soon as `num_workers > 0` is used in a real training run, which is the expected production configuration on M4 Mac. Issue 3 means a single bad replay file (with `num_steps=0`) can cause wrong indices across all subsequent files without any error being raised. Issue 5 (the missing multi-worker test) is the test gap that let issue 1 through.

When `DataLoader(num_workers > 0)` forks, worker processes inherit the live file descriptors from the parent — h5py is not fork-safe. This causes silent data corruption or crashes.\n\n**Fix:** Use a per-process handle cache keyed by `os.getpid()`. On each `__getitem__` call, check if the current PID matches the cached PID; if not (new worker process), reopen all handles.\n\nPattern:\n```python\nimport os\n\nclass ILDataset(Dataset):\n    def __init__(self, ...):\n        ...\n        self._handles: dict[int, h5py.File] = {}  # key: file_idx -> h5py.File, per worker\n        self._handle_pid: int = -1  # PID that opened the handles\n\n    def _get_handle(self, file_idx: int) -> h5py.File:\n        \"\"\"Return open h5py.File, safe across DataLoader worker forks.\"\"\"\n        pid = os.getpid()\n        if self._handle_pid != pid:\n            # New worker process — close any inherited handles and reopen\n            self._close_handles()\n            self._handle_pid = pid\n        if file_idx not in self._handles:\n            self._handles[file_idx] = h5py.File(self.file_paths[file_idx], \"r\")\n        return self._handles[file_idx]\n\n    def _close_handles(self) -> None:\n        for h in self._handles.values():\n            try:\n                h.close()\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n        self._handles.clear()\n\n    def __del__(self) -> None:\n        self._close_handles()\n```\n\n---\n\n## CRITICAL Issue 2: `_resolve_index` is fragile — use `bisect`\n\n**Problem:** The current loop-based `_resolve_index` is O(N) and has edge cases with zero-step files. Replace with `bisect`.\n\n**Fix:**\n```python\nimport bisect\n\ndef _resolve_index(self, idx: int) -> tuple[int, int]:\n    \"\"\"Return (file_index, local_index) for a global step index using bisect.\"\"\"\n    file_idx = bisect.bisect_right(self._cumulative, idx)\n    if file_idx >= len(self._cumulative):\n        raise IndexError(f\"Index {idx} out of range\")\n    offset = self._cumulative[file_idx - 1] if file_idx > 0 else 0\n    return file_idx, idx - offset\n```\n\nWhere `self._cumulative` is a list of cumulative sums (e.g. [64, 128] for two files of 64 steps each).\n\n---\n\n## CRITICAL Issue 3: No guard against `num_steps=0`\n\n**Problem:** A file with `num_steps=0` causes silent index corruption.\n\n**Fix:** In `__init__`, skip files with 0 steps (or raise ValueError):\n```python\nfor fp in self.file_paths:\n    with h5py.File(fp, \"r\") as f:\n        n = int(f.attrs.get(\"num_steps\", len(f[\"action_type\"])))\n    if n == 0:\n        continue  # skip empty files silently, or raise ValueError\n    self._lengths.append(n)\n    self._file_paths_valid.append(fp)\n```\n\nActually, simpler: just skip zero-length files and use only valid files for the cumulative index. OR raise a `ValueError` if any file has 0 steps. Choose: raise ValueError — empty replay files are a data quality issue and should be caught early.\n\n```python\nif n == 0:\n    raise ValueError(f\"HDF5 file {fp} has num_steps=0. Remove empty files before training.\")\n```\n\n---\n\n## IMPORTANT Issue 4: Add `close()` method\n\nAdd an explicit `close()` method so callers can clean up without relying on `__del__`:\n```python\ndef close(self) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Close all open HDF5 file handles.\"\"\"\n    self._close_handles()\n```\n\n---\n\n## IMPORTANT Issue 5: Add multi-worker test\n\nIn `tests/training/test_il_dataset.py`, add a test that uses `num_workers=2` to verify fork safety:\n```python\ndef test_dataloader_multiworker(two_h5_files):\n    \"\"\"DataLoader with num_workers=2 must not corrupt reads.\"\"\"\n    from torch.utils.data import DataLoader\n    ds = ILDataset(two_h5_files)\n    loader = DataLoader(ds, batch_size=4, shuffle=False, num_workers=2)\n    batches = list(loader)\n    # All batches should have correct shape\n    for batch in batches:\n        assert batch[\"spatial\"].shape[1] == STATE.spatial_channels\n    # Total samples across batches should equal dataset length\n    total = sum(b[\"spatial\"].shape[0] for b in batches)\n    assert total == len(ds)\n```\n\n---\n\n## After fixing:\n\n1. Run ALL existing tests + new multi-worker test:\n```bash\ncd /Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npython -m pytest tests/training/test_il_dataset.py -v\n```\n\nAll 13 tests (12 original + 1 new multi-worker) must PASS.\n\n2. **1. Fork-safety fix** — VERIFIED CORRECT

`_get_handle` (lines 166-175) calls `os.getpid()`, compares against `self._handle_pid`, and when a mismatch is detected calls `_close_handles()` before updating the pid and lazily reopening. The `_close_handles()` implementation (lines 177-183) iterates all cached handles, silences exceptions on individual close failures, and clears the dict. Logic is sound.

**2. bisect fix** — VERIFIED CORRECT

`_resolve_index` (lines 158-164) uses `bisect.bisect_right(self._cumulative, idx)`. The offset calculation `self._cumulative[file_idx - 1] if file_idx > 0 else 0` is correct for both edge cases: file_idx=0 returns offset 0 (no prior cumulative), and for the last file it uses the previous file's cumulative total. The post-bisect bounds check guards against a global idx that somehow slips past `__getitem__`'s guard.

**3. Zero-step guard** — VERIFIED CORRECT

`__init__` (lines 42-45) raises `ValueError` with a descriptive message for any file where `n == 0`. Empty files are rejected before being appended to `self.file_paths` or contributing to `_cumulative`.

`close()` (lines 150-152) exists as a public method and directly delegates to `_close_handles()`. `__del__` also calls `_close_handles()` as a safety net.

**5. Multi-worker test** — VERIFIED CORRECT

`test_dataloader_multiworker` (lines 105-116) exists with `num_workers=2`, iterates all batches, checks spatial channel shape on every batch, and asserts the total sample count equals `len(ds)`. This exercises the fork-safety path meaningfully.

+1 for rounding tolerance\n    for batch in sampler:\n        stratum_counts = Counter()\n        for idx in batch:\n            stratum_counts[labels[idx]] += 1\n        for stratum, count in stratum_counts.items():\n            assert count <= cap, f\"Stratum {stratum} appears {count} times (cap {cap})\"\n\n\ndef test_all_indices_covered():\n    \"\"\"Over one full epoch, every index must appear at least once.\"\"\"\n    labels = make_labels(200, seed=3)\n    sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=16, drop_last=False)\n    seen = set()\n    for batch in sampler:\n        seen.update(batch)\n    assert seen == set(range(len(labels)))\n\n\ndef test_single_stratum_fallback():\n    \"\"\"When only one stratum exists, all batches come from it without crashing.\"\"\"\n    labels = [(0, 0, 0)] * 100  # Algorithm:\n  1. Shuffle each stratum's index list if `shuffle=True`\n  2. Now let me trace through the algorithm carefully to check the cap invariant and `__len__` correctness.

The key logic to analyze is in `__iter__` (lines 91-118) and `__len__` (lines 128-132).

**Cap invariant analysis (lines 105-108):**

The `take` computation is `min(per_stratum_cap, slots_left_in_batch, _remaining(key))`. This correctly bounds each stratum's contribution to `per_stratum_cap` per turn. However, the round-robin loop iterates over all `active_keys` within a single "round" (lines 98-112), and critically: when a batch fills mid-round (lines 100-103), a new empty batch starts and the same stratum in the next key iteration can immediately begin contributing to that new batch. This means a single stratum cannot exceed `per_stratum_cap` in the batch it was filling when the flush occurred, but the new empty batch has no memory of what the previous batch contained. This is the intended design and is correct.

**`__len__` correctness — the critical bug:**

`__len__` at line 131 returns `n // batch_size` where `n = len(self.labels)`. But `__iter__` does not emit one batch per `batch_size` labels. Because of the cap invariant, the sampler may need more than `batch_size` label slots to fill a single batch when some strata run dry mid-fill. This means `__iter__` can yield **fewer** batches than `__len__` predicts.

Concrete example: 100 labels, all in one stratum except 1 label in another, `batch_size=16`, `max_same_stratum=0.4`, `drop_last=True`. `per_stratum_cap = max(1, int(0.4*16)) = 6`. The dominant stratum can only contribute 6 per round. The minority stratum contributes 1. A round fills 7 slots of a batch. After that minority stratum is exhausted, only the dominant stratum remains — its cap overrides to `batch_size` (no, it does NOT: the override only fires at construction when `n_strata == 1`). With `n_strata > 1` at construction, `per_stratum_cap` stays at 6 even after the minority stratum empties. This means the batch can only ever receive 6 items per round from the dominant stratum, and `slots_left_in_batch` will shrink toward 0 but the last batch will fill 6 items, then 6 more, reaching 12, then have 4 slots left. Next round: dominant contributes `min(6, 4, remaining)` = 4. Batch completes. So batches still fill correctly in this case.

But consider `batch_size=10`, `max_same_stratum=0.4`, `per_stratum_cap = max(1, int(0.4*10)) = 4`. Two strata. Stratum A: 8 items. Stratum B: 8 items. Round 1: A contributes 4, B contributes 4 -> batch full, emit. Round 2: A contributes 4, B contributes 4 -> batch full, emit. A and B both exhausted. Total emitted: 2. `__len__` returns `16 // 10 = 1`. Off by one in the other direction (over-reports). With 20 labels and `batch_size=10`, `__len__` = 2. But actual yield depends on stratum distribution. This is not a reliable invariant.

**`__len__` vs actual yield — confirmed discrepancy:**

`__len__` assumes every index contributes to exactly one batch of size `batch_size`, which is the standard sampler assumption. But the cap constraint can cause the algorithm to emit **more** batches than `n // batch_size` when total indices across strata align favorably, or **fewer** when strata are exhausted before a batch completes. The `__len__` implementation is fundamentally wrong for a capped sampler.

**CODE QUALITY**

NOT APPROVED. One critical bug and one important issue:

---

**[CRITICAL] `__len__` does not agree with `__iter__` yield count**

`/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_sampler.py`, line 131:

```python
return n // self.batch_size
```

This formula assumes all `n` labels are packed into full batches with no waste, which is true for a standard sampler but not for a capped one. The cap prevents a stratum from filling more than `per_stratum_cap` slots per turn. When strata are unevenly sized, the round-robin will exhaust some strata with remaining slots unfilled in `current_batch`. Those leftover slots are filled from other strata in later rounds, but the total number of emitted batches depends on stratum distribution, not just `n`.

Counter-example: `n=20`, `batch_size=10`, `max_same_stratum=0.4` (`per_stratum_cap=4`). Stratum A: 10 items, Stratum B: 10 items. Each round: A gives 4, B gives 4 = 8 slots. Batch not full. Next round: A gives 4 (2 remain), but `slots_left=2`, so `take=min(4,2,6)=2`. Batch full. Emit. Remaining: A=4, B=6. Continue filling next batch similarly. Final batch count: `__iter__` emits 2 batches. `__len__` returns `20//10 = 2`. Matches here, but this is coincidental.

Now: Stratum A: 17 items, Stratum B: 3 items. `n=20`, `batch_size=10`, `drop_last=True`. `__len__` = 2. Trace `__iter__`: Round 1 of batch 1: A gives 4, B gives 3 (exhausted), batch has 7. B removed from active. Round 2 of batch 1: only A active, A gives `min(4, 3, 13)=3`. Batch has 10. Emit batch 1. Remaining A=10. But now `active_keys` refresh removes B (already gone). Only A. Round fills: A gives 4 (batch=4), A gives 4 (batch=8), A gives `min(4,2,6)=2` (batch=10). Emit batch 2. Remaining A=4. Round: `min(4,10,4)=4`. `current_batch=[4 items]`. A exhausted. `active_keys` empty. Break. `current_batch` has 4 items, `drop_last=True`, discarded. Total yielded: 2. `__len__` = 2. Matches here too.

The real risk is with `drop_last=False`. `__len__` returns `(n + batch_size - 1) // batch_size`. With `n=20`, `batch_size=10` this gives 2. But `__iter__` with the example above would yield 3 (2 full + 1 partial). `__len__` would return 2. That is a genuine discrepancy when `drop_last=False`.

More generally: `__len__` should count the actual batches `__iter__` will yield. For a capped sampler, the only reliable way to compute this is to simulate the iteration or use `sum(1 for _ in self)`. The current simple formula is not equivalent to actual yield count.

This matters because PyTorch's `DataLoader` uses `__len__` of the batch sampler to set `len(dataloader)`. An incorrect value will cause incorrect `tqdm` progress bars and can break schedulers that rely on `len(dataloader)`.

---

**[IMPORTANT] `test_all_indices_covered` does not test `drop_last=False` correctly for uneven cases**

`/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/tests/training/test_strategy_sampler.py`, line 49-56.

The test uses `n=200`, `batch_size=16`. With `drop_last=False`, leftover indices go into a partial batch. The test correctly checks `seen == set(range(200))`. However, the test uses the default `max_same_stratum=0.4` and a randomized label distribution. If any stratum ends up with fewer than `per_stratum_cap` items and those items cannot complete a batch (remaining from other strata also exhausted), they land in `current_batch` which is emitted because `drop_last=False`. This should be fine as implemented. The concern is that the test doesn't assert `seen == set(range(len(labels)))` would fail if `drop_last=True` were accidentally used — the test correctly uses `drop_last=False`, so this is a minor robustness note, not a blocker.

---

**[IMPORTANT] `__len__` discrepancy is not caught by any test**

None of the 6 tests assert `len(sampler) == len(list(sampler))`. The `test_dataloader_integration` test only checks one batch shape, not `len(loader)`. Add a test:

```python
def test_len_agrees_with_iter():
    labels = make_labels(200, seed=6)
    for drop_last in (True, False):
        sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=16, drop_last=drop_last)
        assert len(sampler) == len(list(sampler))
```

This test would currently fail with certain label distributions and `drop_last=False`, which would surface the bug.

### Code Quality

**1. `pointer_loss` all-targets-minus-one handling.**
The `ptr_logits.sum() * 0.0` pattern is correctly used at `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/src/zh_copilot/training/losses.py` line 36. This produces a zero scalar that is attached to the computation graph with a valid (zero) gradient, preventing NaN. Correct.

**2. `pointer_loss` zero gradient for ignored rows.**
The masked indexing `ptr_logits[mask]` on line 37 creates a new tensor containing only the valid rows. PyTorch scatter-accumulates gradient back only to the rows that participated in the forward pass. Rows where `ptr_targets == -1` receive no gradient. The test `test_pointer_loss_skips_gradient_for_ignored` verifies this with an `abs().sum() == approx(0.0)` assertion on the ignored row. Correct.

**3. `value_loss` squeeze handling.**
`.squeeze(-1)` on line 50 correctly collapses a `(B, 1)` tensor to `(B,)` before passing to `F.mse_loss` alongside the `(B,)` outcomes tensor. Correct.

**4. `il_loss` return contract.**
Returns `(total, dict)` at lines 85-89. The dict contains exactly the three required keys: `"action_type_loss"`, `"pointer_loss"`, `"value_loss"`. Correct.

**One minor issue worth noting:** `test_pointer_loss_all_valid` at line 51 generates pointer targets with `torch.randint(0, 10, (B,))`. `STATE.max_entities` is the logit dimension, and the test does not confirm that 10 is a valid upper bound relative to `STATE.max_entities`. If `STATE.max_entities < 10`, this test would produce an out-of-range index and fail at runtime. This is the same pattern used in `test_il_loss_is_weighted_sum` and `test_il_loss_gradient_flows`. Depending on the configured value of `STATE.max_entities`, these tests may be fragile. This is an Important issue, not critical, since it is test code rather than production logic.

Quality: APPROVED (with one Important caveat on hardcoded pointer target range in tests)

Summary of what was implemented:

**`src/zh_copilot/training/il_trainer.py`** — `ILTrainer` class with:
- Auto device selection (CUDA > MPS > CPU), overridable via `device=` arg
- `StratifiedBatchSampler` for train loader, built efficiently via `_build_stratum_labels()` that reads HDF5 datasets directly (no `__getitem__` calls)
- `val_loader` with `shuffle=False`, no sampler
- AdamW optimizer; `CosineAnnealingLR` scheduler initialized in `train()` once `num_epochs` is known
- `train_epoch(epoch)` → `{"loss": float, "action_acc": float}` with gradient clipping (`max_norm=1.0`) and LSTM hidden reset each forward pass
- `val_epoch(epoch)` → same dict plus `acc_{category}` per action type seen in the batch
- `save_checkpoint(epoch, metrics)` → `checkpoint_epoch_NNN.pt` with model state, optimizer state, epoch, and metrics
- `load_checkpoint(path)` → restores both model and optimizer
- `train(num_epochs, checkpoint_every)` → full loop with auto-checkpointing and TensorBoard `SummaryWriter` writing to `<output_dir>/logs/`

**`src/zh_copilot/training/__init__.py`** — `ILTrainer` added to imports and `__all__`

---

## Spec Compliance Review

### Public API

All required `__init__` parameters are present and match the spec exactly, including defaults (`batch_size=256`, `learning_rate=1e-4`, `device=None`, `num_workers=0`, `w_act=1.0`, `w_ptr=0.5`, `w_val=0.1`).

`train_epoch` and `val_epoch` return the correct minimum keys. `val_epoch` adds `acc_{category}` keys from `_ACTION_CATEGORIES`. `save_checkpoint` uses the `checkpoint_epoch_{epoch:03d}.pt` pattern. `train` accepts `num_epochs` and `checkpoint_every=5`. `__init__.py` exports `ILTrainer`. All loaders use `StratifiedBatchSampler` for train and `shuffle=False` for val. LSTM hidden state is passed as `None` every forward call.

**Spec: COMPLIANT**

---

### Test Suite — All 11 Tests Present?

| # | Required Test | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `test_trainer_instantiation` | Present |
| 2 | `test_train_epoch_returns_metrics` | Present |
| 3 | `test_val_epoch_returns_metrics` | Present |
| 4 | `test_loss_decreases_over_two_epochs` | Present |
| 5 | `test_checkpoint_saved` | Present |
| 6 | `test_checkpoint_loadable` | Present |
| 7 | `test_checkpoint_restores_model` | Present |
| 8 | `test_auto_checkpoint_every_n_epochs` | Present |
| 9 | `test_tensorboard_logs_written` | Present |
| 10 | `test_device_selection_cpu` | Present |
| 11 | `test_action_acc_per_category_in_val` | Present (weakly — see below) |

All 11 tests are present.

---

## Code Quality Review — Targeted Checks

**1. `_build_stratum_labels()` efficiency**

This is correct. It opens HDF5 files directly with `h5py`, slices the raw arrays via `f["faction_id"][:n]`, and never calls `dataset.__getitem__`. No PyTorch tensors are allocated. This is the efficient path the spec required.

**2. Batch key filtering before model forward pass**

The `_MODEL_KEYS` set and `_model_batch()` helper correctly strip all non-model keys (e.g., `action_type`, `target_entity_idx`, `outcome`) before calling `self.model(model_input, hidden=None)`. Targets are extracted separately from the raw batch dict. No risk of extra keys crashing the forward pass.

**3. `val_epoch` model state protection**

`model.eval()` is called before the loop and the entire loop body is wrapped in `torch.no_grad()`. BatchNorm/Dropout behave correctly, and no gradients accumulate. This is clean.

**4. TensorBoard writer closure**

This is a real bug. `train()` calls `self._writer.flush()` at the end but never calls `self._writer.close()`. `SummaryWriter` holds a file handle and a background thread. Without `close()`:
- The event file may not be fully flushed on all platforms despite the explicit flush
- The background thread is leaked for the lifetime of the process
- The writer remains open if `train()` is called multiple times, and a second call would reuse the same open writer rather than creating a new one (which is correct behavior, but the first run's writer is still never closed)

The fix is to add `self._writer.close()` after `self._writer.flush()` at line 394, and reset `self._writer = None`.

**5. Scheduler called before `train()` is invoked**

The scheduler is initialized to `None` in `__init__`. In `train_epoch`, the step at line 232 is guarded by `if self._scheduler is not None`. This means calling `train_epoch` directly (as done in 7 of the 11 tests) will silently skip the LR schedule, which is the correct and intentional behavior for standalone epoch calls. The scheduler is only created when `train()` is invoked. No premature step risk exists.

---

## Issues Found

**Critical**

None.

**Important**

`train()` does not close the `SummaryWriter`. Line 394 flushes but never closes. The writer and its background thread are leaked. This will cause flaky behavior in environments that check for clean process exit, and is almost certain to cause issues in the test `test_tensorboard_logs_written` on some platforms where the event file is not fully written before the test asserts on it.

File: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/src/zh_copilot/training/il_trainer.py`, lines 393–395.

Current code:
```python
if self._writer is not None:
    self._writer.flush()
```

Should be:
```python
if self._writer is not None:
    self._writer.flush()
    self._writer.close()
    self._writer = None
```

**Suggestions**

1. `test_loss_decreases_over_two_epochs` (line 61) does not assert that loss actually decreases — it only asserts that both losses are finite. The test name is misleading. It should either assert `m1["loss"] < m0["loss"]` (which may be flaky on 2 epochs) or be renamed to `test_train_epoch_loss_is_finite`. As written it will never catch a regression in training direction.

2. `test_action_acc_per_category_in_val` (line 124) only asserts that `"action_acc"` exists, which is already verified by `test_val_epoch_returns_metrics`. It does not assert any `acc_{category}` key is present, which is the entire point of the spec requirement. The test should assert something like `any(k.startswith("acc_") for k in metrics)`.

3. `test_auto_checkpoint_every_n_epochs` asserts `len(ckpts) >= 1`. With `num_epochs=3` and `checkpoint_every=2`, the spec logic saves at epoch index 1 (epoch 2, the 2nd epoch) and epoch index 2 (the final epoch), so exactly 2 checkpoints should be saved. The assertion `>= 1` is too weak to catch a regression where only the final-epoch fallback fires.

4. The `optimizer.zero_grad()` call at line 221 comes after `loss.backward()`. While not a bug (zeroing before the next forward pass is valid), the conventional and safer ordering is `zero_grad()` → `forward()` → `loss` → `backward()` → `step()`. The current order works only because there is one optimizer step per batch, but it is a footgun if the loop is ever restructured.

---

## Summary

**Spec: COMPLIANT** — All required API surface, loader configurations, and test names are present and correctly implemented.

**Quality: NOT APPROVED** — One important issue must be resolved before this is production-ready: the `SummaryWriter` is never closed in `train()`. Two of the required tests also fail to test what their names and the spec actually require (`test_loss_decreases_over_two_epochs` and `test_action_acc_per_category_in_val`), leaving real regressions undetected.

DONE. All 13 tests pass (47/47 total in the training suite excluding ILTrainer).

**Summary of what was done:**

- Installed `scikit-learn` and added `"scikit-learn>=1.3"` to `pyproject.toml` dependencies.
- Created `tests/training/test_strategy_cluster.py` per spec, but with a lean `_make_cluster_h5` helper instead of `make_synthetic_h5` — the disk was at 100% capacity (633 MB free) and the full synthetic fixture writes ~320 MB of spatial tensors per file (30 files = ~9 GB). The clustering code only reads `frame` and `action_type`, so the lean helper writes only those two datasets.
- Implemented `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_cluster.py` with:
  - `extract_opening_features` — reads `frame`+`action_type`, filters by `max_frames`, returns L2-normalised 21-bin histogram (zero vector if no qualifying steps).
  - `fit_strategy_clusters` — extracts features for all files, caps `n_clusters` to `len(file_paths)` if smaller, runs KMeans, returns `list[int]`.
  - `label_replay_files` — writes `f.attrs["strategy_label"]` and `f["strategy_label_steps"]` (per-step broadcast array) into each HDF5.
  - `StrategyClusterer` class — `fit`/`predict`/`save`/`load` with `RuntimeError("not fitted")` guard.
- Added `StrategyClusterer` to `src/zh_copilot/training/__init__.py` exports.
